<p>cmake-properties &#8211; CMake Properties Reference</p>
<h2>Properties Of Global Scope</h2>
<h3>Allow_Duplicate_Custom_Targets</h3>
<p>Allow duplicate custom targets to be created. </p>
<p> Normally CMake requires that all targets built in a project have globally unique logical names (see policy <code>CMP0002</code>). This is necessary to generate meaningful project file names in <code>Xcode</code> and Visual Studio Generators IDE generators. It also allows the target names to be referenced unambiguously. </p>
<p> Makefile generators are capable of supporting duplicate <code>add_custom_target()</code> names. For projects that care only about Makefile Generators and do not wish to support <code>Xcode</code> or Visual Studio Generators IDE generators, one may set this property to <code>True</code> to allow duplicate custom targets. The property allows multiple <code>add_custom_target()</code> command calls in different directories to specify the same target name. However, setting this property will cause non-Makefile generators to produce an error and refuse to generate the project.</p>
<h3>Autogen_Source_Group</h3>
<p>New in version 3.9. </p>
<p> Name of the <code>source_group()</code> for <code>AUTOMOC</code>, <code>AUTORCC</code> and <code>AUTOUIC</code> generated files. </p>
<p> Files generated by <code>AUTOMOC</code>, <code>AUTORCC</code> and <code>AUTOUIC</code> are not always known at configure time and therefore can&#8217;t be passed to <code>source_group()</code>. <i>AUTOGEN_SOURCE_GROUP</i> can be used instead to generate or select a source group for <code>AUTOMOC</code>, <code>AUTORCC</code> and <code>AUTOUIC</code> generated files. </p>
<p> For <code>AUTOMOC</code>, <code>AUTORCC</code> and <code>AUTOUIC</code> specific overrides see <code>AUTOMOC_SOURCE_GROUP</code>, <code>AUTORCC_SOURCE_GROUP</code> and <code>AUTOUIC_SOURCE_GROUP</code> respectively.</p>
<h3>Autogen_Targets_Folder</h3>
<p>Name of <code>FOLDER</code> for <code>*_autogen</code> targets that are added automatically by CMake for targets for which <code>AUTOMOC</code> is enabled. </p>
<p> If not set, CMake uses the <code>FOLDER</code> property of the parent target as a default value for this property. See also the documentation for the <code>FOLDER</code> target property and the <code>AUTOMOC</code> target property.</p>
<h3>Automoc_Source_Group</h3>
<p>New in version 3.9. </p>
<p> Name of the <code>source_group()</code> for <code>AUTOMOC</code> generated files. </p>
<p> When set this is used instead of <code>AUTOGEN_SOURCE_GROUP</code> for files generated by <code>AUTOMOC</code>.</p>
<h3>Automoc_Targets_Folder</h3>
<p>Name of <code>FOLDER</code> for <code>*_autogen</code> targets that are added automatically by CMake for targets for which <code>AUTOMOC</code> is enabled. </p>
<p> This property is obsolete. Use <code>AUTOGEN_TARGETS_FOLDER</code> instead. </p>
<p> If not set, CMake uses the <code>FOLDER</code> property of the parent target as a default value for this property. See also the documentation for the <code>FOLDER</code> target property and the <code>AUTOMOC</code> target property.</p>
<h3>Autorcc_Source_Group</h3>
<p>New in version 3.9. </p>
<p> Name of the <code>source_group()</code> for <code>AUTORCC</code> generated files. </p>
<p> When set this is used instead of <code>AUTOGEN_SOURCE_GROUP</code> for files generated by <code>AUTORCC</code>.</p>
<h3>Autouic_Source_Group</h3>
<p>New in version 3.21. </p>
<p> Name of the <code>source_group()</code> for <code>AUTOUIC</code> generated files. </p>
<p> When set this is used instead of <code>AUTOGEN_SOURCE_GROUP</code> for files generated by <code>AUTOUIC</code>.</p>
<h3>Cmake_C_Known_Features</h3>
<p>New in version 3.1. </p>
<p> List of C features known to this version of CMake. </p>
<p> The features listed in this global property may be known to be available to the C compiler. If the feature is available with the C compiler, it will be listed in the <code>CMAKE_C_COMPILE_FEATURES</code> variable. </p>
<p> The features listed here may be used with the <code>target_compile_features()</code> command. See the <code><a href="/docs/linux/man/7-cmake-compile-features/">cmake-compile-features</a>(7)</code> manual for information on compile features and a list of supported compilers. </p>
<p> The features known to this version of CMake are listed below.</p>
<h3>High level meta features indicating C standard support</h3>
<p>New in version 3.8. </p>
<dl>
<dt><code>c_std_90</code></p>
<dd>Compiler mode is at least C 90.</p>
<dt><code>c_std_99</code></p>
<dd>Compiler mode is at least C 99.</p>
<dt><code>c_std_11</code></p>
<dd>Compiler mode is at least C 11.</p>
<dt><code>c_std_17</code></p>
<dd>New in version 3.21. </p>
<p> Compiler mode is at least C 17.</p>
<dt><code>c_std_23</code></p>
<dd>New in version 3.21. </p>
<p> Compiler mode is at least C 23.</dl>
<h3>Low level individual compile features</h3>
<dl>
<dt><code>c_function_prototypes</code></p>
<dd>Function prototypes, as defined in <code>ISO/IEC 9899:1990</code>.</p>
<dt><code>c_restrict</code></p>
<dd><code>restrict</code> keyword, as defined in <code>ISO/IEC 9899:1999</code>.</p>
<dt><code>c_static_assert</code></p>
<dd>Static assert, as defined in <code>ISO/IEC 9899:2011</code>.</p>
<dt><code>c_variadic_macros</code></p>
<dd>Variadic macros, as defined in <code>ISO/IEC 9899:1999</code>.</dl>
<h3>Cmake_Cuda_Known_Features</h3>
<p>New in version 3.17. </p>
<p> List of CUDA features known to this version of CMake. </p>
<p> The features listed in this global property may be known to be available to the CUDA compiler. If the feature is available with the C++ compiler, it will be listed in the <code>CMAKE_CUDA_COMPILE_FEATURES</code> variable. </p>
<p> The features listed here may be used with the <code>target_compile_features()</code> command. See the <code><a href="/docs/linux/man/7-cmake-compile-features/">cmake-compile-features</a>(7)</code> manual for information on compile features and a list of supported compilers. </p>
<p> The features known to this version of CMake are:</p>
<dl>
<dt><code>cuda_std_03</code></p>
<dd>Compiler mode is at least CUDA/C++ 03.</p>
<dt><code>cuda_std_11</code></p>
<dd>Compiler mode is at least CUDA/C++ 11.</p>
<dt><code>cuda_std_14</code></p>
<dd>Compiler mode is at least CUDA/C++ 14.</p>
<dt><code>cuda_std_17</code></p>
<dd>Compiler mode is at least CUDA/C++ 17.</p>
<dt><code>cuda_std_20</code></p>
<dd>Compiler mode is at least CUDA/C++ 20.</p>
<dt><code>cuda_std_23</code></p>
<dd>New in version 3.20. </p>
<p> Compiler mode is at least CUDA/C++ 23.</dl>
<h3>Cmake_Cxx_Known_Features</h3>
<p>New in version 3.1. </p>
<p> List of C++ features known to this version of CMake. </p>
<p> The features listed in this global property may be known to be available to the C++ compiler. If the feature is available with the C++ compiler, it will be listed in the <code>CMAKE_CXX_COMPILE_FEATURES</code> variable. </p>
<p> The features listed here may be used with the <code>target_compile_features()</code> command. See the <code><a href="/docs/linux/man/7-cmake-compile-features/">cmake-compile-features</a>(7)</code> manual for information on compile features and a list of supported compilers. </p>
<p> The features known to this version of CMake are listed below.</p>
<h3>High level meta features indicating C++ standard support</h3>
<p>New in version 3.8. </p>
<p> The following meta features indicate general support for the associated language standard. It reflects the language support claimed by the compiler, but it does not necessarily imply complete conformance to that standard.</p>
<dl>
<dt><code>cxx_std_98</code></p>
<dd>Compiler mode is at least C++ 98.</p>
<dt><code>cxx_std_11</code></p>
<dd>Compiler mode is at least C++ 11.</p>
<dt><code>cxx_std_14</code></p>
<dd>Compiler mode is at least C++ 14.</p>
<dt><code>cxx_std_17</code></p>
<dd>Compiler mode is at least C++ 17.</p>
<dt><code>cxx_std_20</code></p>
<dd>New in version 3.12. </p>
<p> Compiler mode is at least C++ 20.</p>
<dt><code>cxx_std_23</code></p>
<dd>New in version 3.20. </p>
<p> Compiler mode is at least C++ 23.</dl>
<h3>Low level individual compile features</h3>
<p>For C++ 11 and C++ 14, compilers were sometimes slow to implement certain language features. CMake provided some individual compile features to help projects determine whether specific features were available. These individual features are now less relevant and projects should generally prefer to use the high level meta features instead. Individual compile features are not provided for C++ 17 or later. </p>
<p> See the <code><a href="/docs/linux/man/7-cmake-compile-features/">cmake-compile-features</a>(7)</code> manual for further discussion of the use of individual compile features.</p>
<h3>Individual features from C++ 98</h3>
<dl>
<dt><code>cxx_template_template_parameters</code></p>
<dd>Template template parameters, as defined in <code>ISO/IEC 14882:1998</code>.</dl>
<h3>Individual features from C++ 11</h3>
<dl>
<dt><code>cxx_alias_templates</code></p>
<dd>Template aliases, as defined in <i>N2258</i>.</p>
<dt><code>cxx_alignas</code></p>
<dd>Alignment control <code>alignas</code>, as defined in <i>N2341</i>.</p>
<dt><code>cxx_alignof</code></p>
<dd>Alignment control <code>alignof</code>, as defined in <i>N2341</i>.</p>
<dt><code>cxx_attributes</code></p>
<dd>Generic attributes, as defined in <i>N2761</i>.</p>
<dt><code>cxx_auto_type</code></p>
<dd>Automatic type deduction, as defined in <i>N1984</i>.</p>
<dt><code>cxx_constexpr</code></p>
<dd>Constant expressions, as defined in <i>N2235</i>.</p>
<dt><code>cxx_decltype_incomplete_return_types</code></p>
<dd>Decltype on incomplete return types, as defined in <i>N3276</i>.</p>
<dt><code>cxx_decltype</code></p>
<dd>Decltype, as defined in <i>N2343</i>.</p>
<dt><code>cxx_default_function_template_args</code></p>
<dd>Default template arguments for function templates, as defined in <i>DR226</i></p>
<dt><code>cxx_defaulted_functions</code></p>
<dd>Defaulted functions, as defined in <i>N2346</i>.</p>
<dt><code>cxx_defaulted_move_initializers</code></p>
<dd>Defaulted move initializers, as defined in <i>N3053</i>.</p>
<dt><code>cxx_delegating_constructors</code></p>
<dd>Delegating constructors, as defined in <i>N1986</i>.</p>
<dt><code>cxx_deleted_functions</code></p>
<dd>Deleted functions, as defined in <i>N2346</i>.</p>
<dt><code>cxx_enum_forward_declarations</code></p>
<dd>Enum forward declarations, as defined in <i>N2764</i>.</p>
<dt><code>cxx_explicit_conversions</code></p>
<dd>Explicit conversion operators, as defined in <i>N2437</i>.</p>
<dt><code>cxx_extended_friend_declarations</code></p>
<dd>Extended friend declarations, as defined in <i>N1791</i>.</p>
<dt><code>cxx_extern_templates</code></p>
<dd>Extern templates, as defined in <i>N1987</i>.</p>
<dt><code>cxx_final</code></p>
<dd>Override control <code>final</code> keyword, as defined in <i>N2928</i>, <i>N3206</i> and <i>N3272</i>.</p>
<dt><code>cxx_func_identifier</code></p>
<dd>Predefined <code>__func__</code> identifier, as defined in <i>N2340</i>.</p>
<dt><code>cxx_generalized_initializers</code></p>
<dd>Initializer lists, as defined in <i>N2672</i>.</p>
<dt><code>cxx_inheriting_constructors</code></p>
<dd>Inheriting constructors, as defined in <i>N2540</i>.</p>
<dt><code>cxx_inline_namespaces</code></p>
<dd>Inline namespaces, as defined in <i>N2535</i>.</p>
<dt><code>cxx_lambdas</code></p>
<dd>Lambda functions, as defined in <i>N2927</i>.</p>
<dt><code>cxx_local_type_template_args</code></p>
<dd>Local and unnamed types as template arguments, as defined in <i>N2657</i>.</p>
<dt><code>cxx_long_long_type</code></p>
<dd><code>long long</code> type, as defined in <i>N1811</i>.</p>
<dt><code>cxx_noexcept</code></p>
<dd>Exception specifications, as defined in <i>N3050</i>.</p>
<dt><code>cxx_nonstatic_member_init</code></p>
<dd>Non-static data member initialization, as defined in <i>N2756</i>.</p>
<dt><code>cxx_nullptr</code></p>
<dd>Null pointer, as defined in <i>N2431</i>.</p>
<dt><code>cxx_override</code></p>
<dd>Override control <code>override</code> keyword, as defined in <i>N2928</i>, <i>N3206</i> and <i>N3272</i>.</p>
<dt><code>cxx_range_for</code></p>
<dd>Range-based for, as defined in <i>N2930</i>.</p>
<dt><code>cxx_raw_string_literals</code></p>
<dd>Raw string literals, as defined in <i>N2442</i>.</p>
<dt><code>cxx_reference_qualified_functions</code></p>
<dd>Reference qualified functions, as defined in <i>N2439</i>.</p>
<dt><code>cxx_right_angle_brackets</code></p>
<dd>Right angle bracket parsing, as defined in <i>N1757</i>.</p>
<dt><code>cxx_rvalue_references</code></p>
<dd>R-value references, as defined in <i>N2118</i>.</p>
<dt><code>cxx_sizeof_member</code></p>
<dd>Size of non-static data members, as defined in <i>N2253</i>.</p>
<dt><code>cxx_static_assert</code></p>
<dd>Static assert, as defined in <i>N1720</i>.</p>

<dd>Strongly typed enums, as defined in <i>N2347</i>.</p>

<dd>Thread-local variables, as defined in <i>N2659</i>.</p>

<dd>Automatic function return type, as defined in <i>N2541</i>.</p>
<dt><code>cxx_unicode_literals</code></p>
<dd>Unicode string literals, as defined in <i>N2442</i>.</p>
<dt><code>cxx_uniform_initialization</code></p>
<dd>Uniform initialization, as defined in <i>N2640</i>.</p>
<dt><code>cxx_unrestricted_unions</code></p>
<dd>Unrestricted unions, as defined in <i>N2544</i>.</p>
<dt><code>cxx_user_literals</code></p>
<dd>User-defined literals, as defined in <i>N2765</i>.</p>
<dt><code>cxx_variadic_macros</code></p>
<dd>Variadic macros, as defined in <i>N1653</i>.</p>
<dt><code>cxx_variadic_templates</code></p>
<dd>Variadic templates, as defined in <i>N2242</i>.</dl>
<h3>Individual features from C++ 14</h3>
<dl>
<dt><code>cxx_aggregate_default_initializers</code></p>
<dd>Aggregate default initializers, as defined in <i>N3605</i>.</p>
<dt><code>cxx_attribute_deprecated</code></p>
<dd><code>[[deprecated]]</code> attribute, as defined in <i>N3760</i>.</p>
<dt><code>cxx_binary_literals</code></p>
<dd>Binary literals, as defined in <i>N3472</i>.</p>
<dt><code>cxx_contextual_conversions</code></p>
<dd>Contextual conversions, as defined in <i>N3323</i>.</p>
<dt><code>cxx_decltype_auto</code></p>
<dd><code>decltype(auto)</code> semantics, as defined in <i>N3638</i>.</p>
<dt><code>cxx_digit_separators</code></p>
<dd>Digit separators, as defined in <i>N3781</i>.</p>
<dt><code>cxx_generic_lambdas</code></p>
<dd>Generic lambdas, as defined in <i>N3649</i>.</p>
<dt><code>cxx_lambda_init_captures</code></p>
<dd>Initialized lambda captures, as defined in <i>N3648</i>.</p>
<dt><code>cxx_relaxed_constexpr</code></p>
<dd>Relaxed constexpr, as defined in <i>N3652</i>.</p>
<dt><code>cxx_return_type_deduction</code></p>
<dd>Return type deduction on normal functions, as defined in <i>N3386</i>.</p>
<dt><code>cxx_variable_templates</code></p>
<dd>Variable templates, as defined in <i>N3651</i>.</dl>
<h3>Cmake_Role</h3>
<p>New in version 3.14. </p>
<p> Tells what mode the current running script is in. Could be one of several values:</p>
<dl>
<dt><code>PROJECT</code></p>
<dd>Running in project mode (processing a <code>CMakeLists.txt</code> file).</p>
<dt><code>SCRIPT</code></p>
<dd>Running in <code>-P</code> script mode.</p>
<dt><code>FIND_PACKAGE</code></p>
<dd>Running in <code>&#8211;find-package</code> mode.</p>
<dt><code>CTEST</code></p>
<dd>Running in CTest script mode.</p>
<dt><code>CPACK</code></p>
<dd>Running in CPack.</dl>
<h3>Debug_Configurations</h3>
<p>Specify which configurations are for debugging. </p>
<p> The value must be a semi-colon separated list of configuration names. Currently this property is used only by the <code>target_link_libraries()</code> command. Additional uses may be defined in the future. </p>
<p> This property must be set at the top level of the project and before the first <code>target_link_libraries()</code> command invocation. If any entry in the list does not match a valid configuration for the project the behavior is undefined.</p>
<h3>Disabled_Features</h3>
<p>List of features which are disabled during the CMake run. </p>
<p> List of features which are disabled during the CMake run. By default it contains the names of all packages which were not found. This is determined using the <code>&lt;NAME&gt;_FOUND</code> variables. Packages which are searched <code>QUIET</code> are not listed. A project can add its own features to this list. This property is used by the macros in <code>FeatureSummary.cmake</code>.</p>
<h3>Eclipse_Extra_Cproject_Contents</h3>
<p>New in version 3.12. </p>
<p> Additional contents to be inserted into the generated Eclipse cproject file. </p>
<p> The cproject file defines the CDT specific information. Some third party IDE&#8217;s are based on Eclipse with the addition of other information specific to that IDE. Through this property, it is possible to add this additional contents to the generated project. It is expected to contain valid XML. </p>
<p> Also see the <code>ECLIPSE_EXTRA_NATURES</code> property.</p>
<h3>Eclipse_Extra_Natures</h3>
<p>List of natures to add to the generated Eclipse project file. </p>
<p> Eclipse projects specify language plugins by using natures. This property should be set to the unique identifier for a nature (which looks like a Java package name). </p>
<p> Also see the <code>ECLIPSE_EXTRA_CPROJECT_CONTENTS</code> property.</p>
<h3>Enabled_Features</h3>
<p>List of features which are enabled during the CMake run. </p>
<p> List of features which are enabled during the CMake run. By default it contains the names of all packages which were found. This is determined using the <code>&lt;NAME&gt;_FOUND</code> variables. Packages which are searched <code>QUIET</code> are not listed. A project can add its own features to this list. This property is used by the macros in <code>FeatureSummary.cmake</code>.</p>
<h3>Enabled_Languages</h3>
<p>Read-only property that contains the list of currently enabled languages </p>
<p> Set to list of currently enabled languages.</p>
<h3>Find_Library_Use_Lib32_Paths</h3>
<p>New in version 3.7. </p>
<p> Whether the <code>find_library()</code> command should automatically search <code>lib32</code> directories. </p>
<p> <code>FIND_LIBRARY_USE_LIB32_PATHS</code> is a boolean specifying whether the <code>find_library()</code> command should automatically search the <code>lib32</code> variant of directories called <code>lib</code> in the search path when building 32-bit binaries. </p>
<p> See also the <code>CMAKE_FIND_LIBRARY_CUSTOM_LIB_SUFFIX</code> variable.</p>
<h3>Find_Library_Use_Lib64_Paths</h3>
<p>Whether <code>find_library()</code> should automatically search lib64 directories. </p>
<p> FIND_LIBRARY_USE_LIB64_PATHS is a boolean specifying whether the <code>find_library()</code> command should automatically search the lib64 variant of directories called lib in the search path when building 64-bit binaries. </p>
<p> See also the <code>CMAKE_FIND_LIBRARY_CUSTOM_LIB_SUFFIX</code> variable.</p>
<h3>Find_Library_Use_Libx32_Paths</h3>
<p>New in version 3.9. </p>
<p> Whether the <code>find_library()</code> command should automatically search <code>libx32</code> directories. </p>
<p> <code>FIND_LIBRARY_USE_LIBX32_PATHS</code> is a boolean specifying whether the <code>find_library()</code> command should automatically search the <code>libx32</code> variant of directories called <code>lib</code> in the search path when building x32-abi binaries. </p>
<p> See also the <code>CMAKE_FIND_LIBRARY_CUSTOM_LIB_SUFFIX</code> variable.</p>
<h3>Find_Library_Use_Openbsd_Versioning</h3>
<p>Whether <code>find_library()</code> should find OpenBSD-style shared libraries. </p>
<p> This property is a boolean specifying whether the <code>find_library()</code> command should find shared libraries with OpenBSD-style versioned extension: &quot;.so.&lt;major&gt;.&lt;minor&gt;&quot;. The property is set to true on OpenBSD and false on other platforms.</p>
<h3>Generator_Is_Multi_Config</h3>
<p>New in version 3.9. </p>
<p> Read-only property that is true on multi-configuration generators. </p>
<p> True when using a multi-configuration generator such as:</p>
<dl>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd><code>Ninja Multi-Config</code></p>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd>Visual Studio Generators</p>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd><code>Xcode</code> </p>
<p> Multi-config generators use <code>CMAKE_CONFIGURATION_TYPES</code> as the set of configurations and ignore <code>CMAKE_BUILD_TYPE</code>.</dl>
<h3>Global_Depends_Debug_Mode</h3>
<p>Enable global target dependency graph debug mode. </p>
<p> CMake automatically analyzes the global inter-target dependency graph at the beginning of native build system generation. This property causes it to display details of its analysis to stderr.</p>
<h3>Global_Depends_No_Cycles</h3>
<p>Disallow global target dependency graph cycles. </p>
<p> CMake automatically analyzes the global inter-target dependency graph at the beginning of native build system generation. It reports an error if the dependency graph contains a cycle that does not consist of all STATIC library targets. This property tells CMake to disallow all cycles completely, even among static libraries.</p>
<h3>In_Try_Compile</h3>
<p>Read-only property that is true during a try-compile configuration. </p>
<p> True when building a project inside a <code>try_compile()</code> or <code>try_run()</code> command.</p>
<h3>Job_Pools</h3>
<p>Ninja only: List of available pools. </p>
<p> A pool is a named integer property and defines the maximum number of concurrent jobs which can be started by a rule assigned to the pool. The <i>JOB_POOLS</i> property is a semicolon-separated list of pairs using the syntax NAME=integer (without a space after the equality sign). </p>
<p> For instance: </p>
<pre>set_property(GLOBAL PROPERTY JOB_POOLS two_jobs=2 ten_jobs=10)</pre>
<p>Defined pools could be used globally by setting <code>CMAKE_JOB_POOL_COMPILE</code> and <code>CMAKE_JOB_POOL_LINK</code> or per target by setting the target properties <code>JOB_POOL_COMPILE</code> and <code>JOB_POOL_LINK</code>. <code>Custom commands</code> and <code>custom targets</code> can specify pools using the option <code>JOB_POOL</code>. Using a pool that is not defined by <i>JOB_POOLS</i> causes an error by ninja at build time. </p>
<p> If not set, this property uses the value of the <code>CMAKE_JOB_POOLS</code> variable. </p>
<p> Build targets provided by CMake that are meant for individual interactive use, such as <code>install</code>, are placed in the <code>console</code> pool automatically.</p>
<h3>Packages_Found</h3>
<p>List of packages which were found during the CMake run. </p>
<p> List of packages which were found during the CMake run. Whether a package has been found is determined using the &lt;NAME&gt;_FOUND variables.</p>
<h3>Packages_Not_Found</h3>
<p>List of packages which were not found during the CMake run. </p>
<p> List of packages which were not found during the CMake run. Whether a package has been found is determined using the &lt;NAME&gt;_FOUND variables.</p>
<h3>Predefined_Targets_Folder</h3>
<p>Name of FOLDER for targets that are added automatically by CMake. </p>
<p> If not set, CMake uses &quot;CMakePredefinedTargets&quot; as a default value for this property. Targets such as INSTALL, PACKAGE and RUN_TESTS will be organized into this FOLDER. See also the documentation for the <code>FOLDER</code> target property.</p>
<h3>Report_Undefined_Properties</h3>
<p>If set, report any undefined properties to this file. </p>
<p> If this property is set to a filename then when CMake runs it will report any properties or variables that were accessed but not defined into the filename specified in this property.</p>
<h3>Rule_Launch_Compile</h3>
<p>Specify a launcher for compile rules. </p>
<p> Makefile Generators and the <code>Ninja</code> generator prefix compiler commands with the given launcher command line. This is intended to allow launchers to intercept build problems with high granularity. Other generators ignore this property because their underlying build systems provide no hook to wrap individual commands with a launcher.</p>
<h3>Rule_Launch_Custom</h3>
<p>Specify a launcher for custom rules. </p>
<p> Makefile Generators and the <code>Ninja</code> generator prefix custom commands with the given launcher command line. This is intended to allow launchers to intercept build problems with high granularity. Other generators ignore this property because their underlying build systems provide no hook to wrap individual commands with a launcher.</p>
<h3>Rule_Launch_Link</h3>
<p>Specify a launcher for link rules. </p>
<p> Makefile Generators and the <code>Ninja</code> generator prefix link and archive commands with the given launcher command line. This is intended to allow launchers to intercept build problems with high granularity. Other generators ignore this property because their underlying build systems provide no hook to wrap individual commands with a launcher.</p>
<h3>Rule_Messages</h3>
<p>Specify whether to report a message for each make rule. </p>
<p> This property specifies whether Makefile generators should add a progress message describing what each build rule does. If the property is not set the default is ON. Set the property to OFF to disable granular messages and report only as each target completes. This is intended to allow scripted builds to avoid the build time cost of detailed reports. If a <code>CMAKE_RULE_MESSAGES</code> cache entry exists its value initializes the value of this property. Non-Makefile generators currently ignore this property.</p>
<h3>Target_Archives_May_Be_Shared_Libs</h3>
<p>Set if shared libraries may be named like archives. </p>
<p> On AIX shared libraries may be named &quot;lib&lt;name&gt;.a&quot;. This property is set to true on such platforms.</p>
<h3>Target_Messages</h3>
<p>New in version 3.4. </p>
<p> Specify whether to report the completion of each target. </p>
<p> This property specifies whether Makefile Generators should add a progress message describing that each target has been completed. If the property is not set the default is <code>ON</code>. Set the property to <code>OFF</code> to disable target completion messages. </p>
<p> This option is intended to reduce build output when little or no work needs to be done to bring the build tree up to date. </p>
<p> If a <code>CMAKE_TARGET_MESSAGES</code> cache entry exists its value initializes the value of this property. </p>
<p> Non-Makefile generators currently ignore this property. </p>
<p> See the counterpart property <code>RULE_MESSAGES</code> to disable everything except for target completion messages.</p>
<h3>Target_Supports_Shared_Libs</h3>
<p>Does the target platform support shared libraries. </p>
<p> TARGET_SUPPORTS_SHARED_LIBS is a boolean specifying whether the target platform supports shared libraries. Basically all current general general purpose OS do so, the exception are usually embedded systems with no or special OSs.</p>
<h3>Use_Folders</h3>
<p>Use the <code>FOLDER</code> target property to organize targets into folders. </p>
<p> If not set, CMake treats this property as <code>OFF</code> by default. CMake generators that are capable of organizing into a hierarchy of folders use the values of the <code>FOLDER</code> target property to name those folders. See also the documentation for the <code>FOLDER</code> target property.</p>
<h3>Xcode_Emit_Effective_Platform_Name</h3>
<p>New in version 3.8. </p>
<p> Control emission of <code>EFFECTIVE_PLATFORM_NAME</code> by the <code>Xcode</code> generator. </p>
<p> It is required for building the same target with multiple SDKs. A common use case is the parallel use of <code>iphoneos</code> and <code>iphonesimulator</code> SDKs. </p>
<p> Three different states possible that control when the <code>Xcode</code> generator emits the <code>EFFECTIVE_PLATFORM_NAME</code> variable:</p>
<dl>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd>If set to <code>ON</code> it will always be emitted</p>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd>If set to <code>OFF</code> it will never be emitted</p>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd>If unset (the default) it will only be emitted when the project was configured for an embedded Xcode SDK like iOS, tvOS, watchOS or any of the simulators. </p>
<p> <code>NOTE:</code> When this behavior is enable for generated Xcode projects, the <code>EFFECTIVE_PLATFORM_NAME</code> variable will leak into <code>Generator expressions</code> like <code>TARGET_FILE</code> and will render those mostly unusable.</dl>
<h2>Properties On Directories</h2>
<h3>Additional_Clean_Files</h3>
<p>New in version 3.15. </p>
<p> A ;-list of files or directories that will be removed as a part of the global <code>clean</code> target. It is useful for specifying generated files or directories that are used by multiple targets or by CMake itself, or that are generated in ways which cannot be captured as outputs or byproducts of custom commands. </p>
<p> If an additional clean file is specific to a single target only, then the <code>ADDITIONAL_CLEAN_FILES</code> target property would usually be a better choice than this directory property. </p>
<p> Relative paths are allowed and are interpreted relative to the current binary directory. </p>
<p> Contents of <code>ADDITIONAL_CLEAN_FILES</code> may use <code>generator expressions</code>. </p>
<p> This property only works for the <code>Ninja</code> and the Makefile generators. It is ignored by other generators.</p>
<h3>Binary_Dir</h3>
<p>New in version 3.7. </p>
<p> This read-only directory property reports absolute path to the binary directory corresponding to the source on which it is read.</p>
<h3>Buildsystem_Targets</h3>
<p>New in version 3.7. </p>
<p> This read-only directory property contains a semicolon-separated list of buildsystem targets added in the directory by calls to the <code>add_library()</code>, <code>add_executable()</code>, and <code>add_custom_target()</code> commands. The list does not include any Imported Targets or Alias Targets, but does include Interface Libraries. Each entry in the list is the logical name of a target, suitable to pass to the <code>get_property()</code> command <code>TARGET</code> option. </p>
<p> See also the <code>IMPORTED_TARGETS</code> directory property.</p>
<h3>Cache_Variables</h3>
<p>List of cache variables available in the current directory. </p>
<p> This read-only property specifies the list of CMake cache variables currently defined. It is intended for debugging purposes.</p>
<h3>Clean_No_Custom</h3>
<p>Set to true to tell Makefile Generators not to remove the outputs of custom commands for this directory during the <code>make clean</code> operation. This is ignored on other generators because it is not possible to implement.</p>
<h3>Cmake_Configure_Depends</h3>
<p>Tell CMake about additional input files to the configuration process. If any named file is modified the build system will re-run CMake to re-configure the file and generate the build system again. </p>
<p> Specify files as a semicolon-separated list of paths. Relative paths are interpreted as relative to the current source directory.</p>
<h3>Compile_Definitions</h3>
<p>Preprocessor definitions for compiling a directory&#8217;s sources. </p>
<p> This property specifies the list of options given so far to the <code>add_compile_definitions()</code> (or <code>add_definitions()</code>) command. </p>
<p> The <code>COMPILE_DEFINITIONS</code> property may be set to a semicolon-separated list of preprocessor definitions using the syntax <code>VAR</code> or <code>VAR=value</code>. Function-style definitions are not supported. CMake will automatically escape the value correctly for the native build system (note that CMake language syntax may require escapes to specify some values). </p>
<p> This property will be initialized in each directory by its value in the directory&#8217;s parent. </p>
<p> CMake will automatically drop some definitions that are not supported by the native build tool. </p>
<p> Disclaimer: Most native build tools have poor support for escaping certain values. CMake has work-arounds for many cases but some values may just not be possible to pass correctly. If a value does not seem to be escaped correctly, do not attempt to work-around the problem by adding escape sequences to the value. Your work-around may break in a future version of CMake that has improved escape support. Instead consider defining the macro in a (configured) header file. Then report the limitation. Known limitations include: </p>
<pre># - broken almost everywhere
; - broken in VS IDE 7.0 and Borland Makefiles
, - broken in VS IDE
% - broken in some cases in NMake
&amp; | - broken in some cases on MinGW
^ &lt; &gt; \&quot; - broken in most Make tools on Windows</pre>
<p>CMake does not reject these values outright because they do work in some cases. Use with caution. </p>
<p> Contents of <code>COMPILE_DEFINITIONS</code> may use &quot;generator expressions&quot; with the syntax <code>$&lt;&#8230;&gt;</code>. See the <code><a href="/docs/linux/man/7-cmake-generator-expressions/">cmake-generator-expressions</a>(7)</code> manual for available expressions. See the <code><a href="/docs/linux/man/7-cmake-buildsystem/">cmake-buildsystem</a>(7)</code> manual for more on defining buildsystem properties. </p>
<p> The corresponding <code>COMPILE_DEFINITIONS_&lt;CONFIG&gt;</code> property may be set to specify per-configuration definitions. Generator expressions should be preferred instead of setting the alternative property.</p>
<h3>Compile_Options</h3>
<p>List of options to pass to the compiler. </p>
<p> This property holds a semicolon-separated list of options given so far to the <code>add_compile_options()</code> command. </p>
<p> This property is used to initialize the <code>COMPILE_OPTIONS</code> target property when a target is created, which is used by the generators to set the options for the compiler. </p>
<p> Contents of <code>COMPILE_OPTIONS</code> may use &quot;generator expressions&quot; with the syntax <code>$&lt;&#8230;&gt;</code>. See the <code><a href="/docs/linux/man/7-cmake-generator-expressions/">cmake-generator-expressions</a>(7)</code> manual for available expressions. See the <code><a href="/docs/linux/man/7-cmake-buildsystem/">cmake-buildsystem</a>(7)</code> manual for more on defining buildsystem properties.</p>
<h3>Definitions</h3>
<p>For CMake 2.4 compatibility only. Use <code>COMPILE_DEFINITIONS</code> instead. </p>
<p> This read-only property specifies the list of flags given so far to the <code>add_definitions()</code> command. It is intended for debugging purposes. Use the <code>COMPILE_DEFINITIONS</code> directory property instead. </p>
<p> This built-in read-only property does not exist if policy <code>CMP0059</code> is set to <code>NEW</code>.</p>
<h3>Exclude_From_All</h3>
<p>Set this directory property to a true value on a subdirectory to exclude its targets from the &quot;all&quot; target of its ancestors. If excluded, running e.g. <code>make</code> in the parent directory will not build targets the subdirectory by default. This does not affect the &quot;all&quot; target of the subdirectory itself. Running e.g. <code>make</code> inside the subdirectory will still build its targets. </p>
<p> If the <code>EXCLUDE_FROM_ALL</code> target property is set on a target then its value determines whether the target is included in the &quot;all&quot; target of this directory and its ancestors.</p>
<h3>Implicit_Depends_Include_Transform</h3>
<p>Specify <code>#include</code> line transforms for dependencies in a directory. </p>
<p> This property specifies rules to transform macro-like <code>#include</code> lines during implicit dependency scanning of C and C++ source files. The list of rules must be semicolon-separated with each entry of the form <code>A_MACRO(%)=value-with-%</code> (the <code>%</code> must be literal). During dependency scanning occurrences of <code>A_MACRO(&#8230;)</code> on <code>#include</code> lines will be replaced by the value given with the macro argument substituted for <code>%</code>. For example, the entry </p>
<pre>MYDIR(%)=&lt;mydir/%&gt;</pre>
<p>will convert lines of the form </p>
<pre>#include MYDIR(myheader.h)</pre>
<p>to </p>
<pre>#include &lt;mydir/myheader.h&gt;</pre>
<p>allowing the dependency to be followed. </p>
<p> This property applies to sources in all targets within a directory. The property value is initialized in each directory by its value in the directory&#8217;s parent.</p>
<h3>Imported_Targets</h3>
<p>New in version 3.21. </p>
<p> This read-only directory property contains a semicolon-separated list of Imported Targets added in the directory by calls to the <code>add_library()</code> and <code>add_executable()</code> commands. Each entry in the list is the logical name of a target, suitable to pass to the <code>get_property()</code> command <code>TARGET</code> option when called in the same directory. </p>
<p> See also the <code>BUILDSYSTEM_TARGETS</code> directory property.</p>
<h3>Include_Directories</h3>
<p>List of preprocessor include file search directories. </p>
<p> This property specifies the list of directories given so far to the <code>include_directories()</code> command. </p>
<p> This property is used to populate the <code>INCLUDE_DIRECTORIES</code> target property, which is used by the generators to set the include directories for the compiler. </p>
<p> In addition to accepting values from that command, values may be set directly on any directory using the <code>set_property()</code> command, and can be set on the current directory using the <code>set_directory_properties()</code> command. A directory gets its initial value from its parent directory if it has one. The initial value of the <code>INCLUDE_DIRECTORIES</code> target property comes from the value of this property. Both directory and target property values are adjusted by calls to the <code>include_directories()</code> command. Calls to <code>set_property()</code> or <code>set_directory_properties()</code>, however, will update the directory property value without updating target property values. Therefore direct property updates must be made before calls to <code>add_executable()</code> or <code>add_library()</code> for targets they are meant to affect. </p>
<p> The target property values are used by the generators to set the include paths for the compiler. </p>
<p> Contents of <code>INCLUDE_DIRECTORIES</code> may use &quot;generator expressions&quot; with the syntax <code>$&lt;&#8230;&gt;</code>. See the <code><a href="/docs/linux/man/7-cmake-generator-expressions/">cmake-generator-expressions</a>(7)</code> manual for available expressions. See the <code><a href="/docs/linux/man/7-cmake-buildsystem/">cmake-buildsystem</a>(7)</code> manual for more on defining buildsystem properties.</p>
<h3>Include_Regular_Expression</h3>
<p>Include file scanning regular expression. </p>
<p> This property specifies the regular expression used during dependency scanning to match include files that should be followed. See the <code>include_regular_expression()</code> command for a high-level interface to set this property.</p>
<h3>Interprocedural_Optimization</h3>
<p>Enable interprocedural optimization for targets in a directory. </p>
<p> If set to true, enables interprocedural optimizations if they are known to be supported by the compiler.</p>
<h3>INTERPROCEDURAL_OPTIMIZATION_&lt;CONFIG&gt;</h3>
<p>Per-configuration interprocedural optimization for a directory. </p>
<p> This is a per-configuration version of <code>INTERPROCEDURAL_OPTIMIZATION</code>. If set, this property overrides the generic property for the named configuration.</p>
<h3>Labels</h3>
<p>New in version 3.10. </p>
<p> Specify a list of text labels associated with a directory and all of its subdirectories. This is equivalent to setting the <code>LABELS</code> target property and the <code>LABELS</code> test property on all targets and tests in the current directory and subdirectories. Note: Launchers must enabled to propagate labels to targets. </p>
<p> The <code>CMAKE_DIRECTORY_LABELS</code> variable can be used to initialize this property. </p>
<p> The list is reported in dashboard submissions.</p>
<h3>Link_Directories</h3>
<p>List of linker search directories. </p>
<p> This property holds a semicolon-separated list of directories and is typically populated using the <code>link_directories()</code> command. It gets its initial value from its parent directory, if it has one. </p>
<p> The directory property is used to initialize the <code>LINK_DIRECTORIES</code> target property when a target is created. That target property is used by the generators to set the library search directories for the linker. </p>
<p> Contents of <code>LINK_DIRECTORIES</code> may use &quot;generator expressions&quot; with the syntax <code>$&lt;&#8230;&gt;</code>. See the <code><a href="/docs/linux/man/7-cmake-generator-expressions/">cmake-generator-expressions</a>(7)</code> manual for available expressions. See the <code><a href="/docs/linux/man/7-cmake-buildsystem/">cmake-buildsystem</a>(7)</code> manual for more on defining buildsystem properties.</p>
<h3>Link_Options</h3>
<p>New in version 3.13. </p>
<p> List of options to use for the link step of shared library, module and executable targets as well as the device link step. </p>
<p> This property holds a semicolon-separated list of options given so far to the <code>add_link_options()</code> command. </p>
<p> This property is used to initialize the <code>LINK_OPTIONS</code> target property when a target is created, which is used by the generators to set the options for the compiler. </p>
<p> Contents of <code>LINK_OPTIONS</code> may use &quot;generator expressions&quot; with the syntax <code>$&lt;&#8230;&gt;</code>. See the <code><a href="/docs/linux/man/7-cmake-generator-expressions/">cmake-generator-expressions</a>(7)</code> manual for available expressions. See the <code><a href="/docs/linux/man/7-cmake-buildsystem/">cmake-buildsystem</a>(7)</code> manual for more on defining buildsystem properties.</p>
<h3>Listfile_Stack</h3>
<p>The current stack of listfiles being processed. </p>
<p> This property is mainly useful when trying to debug errors in your CMake scripts. It returns a list of what list files are currently being processed, in order. So if one listfile does an <code>include()</code> command then that is effectively pushing the included listfile onto the stack.</p>
<h3>Macros</h3>
<p>List of macro commands available in the current directory. </p>
<p> This read-only property specifies the list of CMake macros currently defined. It is intended for debugging purposes. See the <code>macro()</code> command.</p>
<h3>Parent_Directory</h3>
<p>Source directory that added current subdirectory. </p>
<p> This read-only property specifies the source directory that added the current source directory as a subdirectory of the build. In the top-level directory the value is the empty-string.</p>
<h3>Rule_Launch_Compile</h3>
<p>Specify a launcher for compile rules. </p>
<p> See the global property of the same name for details. This overrides the global property for a directory.</p>
<h3>Rule_Launch_Custom</h3>
<p>Specify a launcher for custom rules. </p>
<p> See the global property of the same name for details. This overrides the global property for a directory.</p>
<h3>Rule_Launch_Link</h3>
<p>Specify a launcher for link rules. </p>
<p> See the global property of the same name for details. This overrides the global property for a directory.</p>
<h3>Source_Dir</h3>
<p>New in version 3.7. </p>
<p> This read-only directory property reports absolute path to the source directory on which it is read.</p>
<h3>Subdirectories</h3>
<p>New in version 3.7. </p>
<p> This read-only directory property contains a semicolon-separated list of subdirectories processed so far by the <code>add_subdirectory()</code> or <code>subdirs()</code> commands. Each entry is the absolute path to the source directory (containing the <code>CMakeLists.txt</code> file). This is suitable to pass to the <code>get_property()</code> command <code>DIRECTORY</code> option. </p>
<p> <code>NOTE:</code> The <code>subdirs()</code> command does not process its arguments until after the calling directory is fully processed. Therefore looking up this property in the current directory will not see them.</p>
<h3>Tests</h3>
<p>New in version 3.12. </p>
<p> List of tests. </p>
<p> This read-only property holds a semicolon-separated list of tests defined so far, in the current directory, by the <code>add_test()</code> command.</p>
<h3>Test_Include_Files</h3>
<p>New in version 3.10. </p>
<p> A list of cmake files that will be included when ctest is run. </p>
<p> If you specify <code>TEST_INCLUDE_FILES</code>, those files will be included and processed when ctest is run on the directory.</p>
<h3>Variables</h3>
<p>List of variables defined in the current directory. </p>
<p> This read-only property specifies the list of CMake variables currently defined. It is intended for debugging purposes.</p>
<h3>VS_GLOBAL_SECTION_POST_&lt;section&gt;</h3>
<p>Specify a postSolution global section in Visual Studio. </p>
<p> Setting a property like this generates an entry of the following form in the solution file: </p>
<pre>GlobalSection(&lt;section&gt;) = postSolution
 &lt;contents based on property value&gt;
EndGlobalSection</pre>
<p>The property must be set to a semicolon-separated list of <code>key=value</code> pairs. Each such pair will be transformed into an entry in the solution global section. Whitespace around key and value is ignored. List elements which do not contain an equal sign are skipped. </p>
<p> This property only works for Visual Studio 9 and above; it is ignored on other generators. The property only applies when set on a directory whose <code>CMakeLists.txt</code> contains a <code>project()</code> command. </p>
<p> Note that CMake generates postSolution sections <code>ExtensibilityGlobals</code> and <code>ExtensibilityAddIns</code> by default. If you set the corresponding property, it will override the default section. For example, setting <code>VS_GLOBAL_SECTION_POST_ExtensibilityGlobals</code> will override the default contents of the <code>ExtensibilityGlobals</code> section, while keeping ExtensibilityAddIns on its default. However, CMake will always add a <code>SolutionGuid</code> to the <code>ExtensibilityGlobals</code> section if it is not specified explicitly.</p>
<h3>VS_GLOBAL_SECTION_PRE_&lt;section&gt;</h3>
<p>Specify a preSolution global section in Visual Studio. </p>
<p> Setting a property like this generates an entry of the following form in the solution file: </p>
<pre>GlobalSection(&lt;section&gt;) = preSolution
 &lt;contents based on property value&gt;
EndGlobalSection</pre>
<p>The property must be set to a semicolon-separated list of <code>key=value</code> pairs. Each such pair will be transformed into an entry in the solution global section. Whitespace around key and value is ignored. List elements which do not contain an equal sign are skipped. </p>
<p> This property only works for Visual Studio 9 and above; it is ignored on other generators. The property only applies when set on a directory whose <code>CMakeLists.txt</code> contains a <code>project()</code> command.</p>
<h3>Vs_Startup_Project</h3>
<p>New in version 3.6. </p>
<p> Specify the default startup project in a Visual Studio solution. </p>
<p> The Visual Studio Generators create a <code>.sln</code> file for each directory whose <code>CMakeLists.txt</code> file calls the <code>project()</code> command. Set this property in the same directory as a <code>project()</code> command call (e.g. in the top-level <code>CMakeLists.txt</code> file) to specify the default startup project for the corresponding solution file. </p>
<p> The property must be set to the name of an existing target. This will cause that project to be listed first in the generated solution file causing Visual Studio to make it the startup project if the solution has never been opened before. </p>
<p> If this property is not specified, then the <code>ALL_BUILD</code> project will be the default.</p>
<h2>Properties On Targets</h2>
<h3>Additional_Clean_Files</h3>
<p>New in version 3.15. </p>
<p> A ;-list of files or directories that will be removed as a part of the global <code>clean</code> target. It can be used to specify files and directories that are generated as part of building the target or that are directly associated with the target in some way (e.g. created as a result of running the target). </p>
<p> For custom targets, if such files can be captured as outputs or byproducts instead, then that should be preferred over adding them to this property. If an additional clean file is used by multiple targets or isn&#8217;t target-specific, then the <code>ADDITIONAL_CLEAN_FILES</code> directory property may be the more appropriate property to use. </p>
<p> Relative paths are allowed and are interpreted relative to the current binary directory. </p>
<p> Contents of <code>ADDITIONAL_CLEAN_FILES</code> may use <code>generator expressions</code>. </p>
<p> This property only works for the <code>Ninja</code> and the Makefile generators. It is ignored by other generators.</p>
<h3>Aix_Export_All_Symbols</h3>
<p>New in version 3.17. </p>
<p> On AIX, CMake automatically exports all symbols from shared libraries, and from executables with the <code>ENABLE_EXPORTS</code> target property set. Explicitly disable this boolean property to suppress the behavior and export no symbols by default. In this case it is expected that the project will use other means to export some symbols. </p>
<p> This property is initialized by the value of the <code>CMAKE_AIX_EXPORT_ALL_SYMBOLS</code> variable if it is set when a target is created.</p>
<h3>Alias_Global</h3>
<p>New in version 3.18. </p>
<p> Read-only property indicating of whether an ALIAS target is globally visible. </p>
<p> The boolean value of this property is <code>TRUE</code> for aliases to IMPORTED targets created with the <code>GLOBAL</code> options to <code>add_executable()</code> or <code>add_library()</code>, <code>FALSE</code> otherwise. It is undefined for targets built within the project. </p>
<p> <code>NOTE:</code> Promoting an IMPORTED target from <code>LOCAL</code> to <code>GLOBAL</code> scope by changing the value or <code>IMPORTED_GLOBAL</code> target property do not change the scope of local aliases.</p>
<h3>Aliased_Target</h3>
<p>Name of target aliased by this target. </p>
<p> If this is an Alias Target, this property contains the name of the target aliased.</p>
<h3>Android_Ant_Additional_Options</h3>
<p>New in version 3.4. </p>
<p> Set the additional options for Android Ant build system. This is a string value containing all command line options for the Ant build. This property is initialized by the value of the <code>CMAKE_ANDROID_ANT_ADDITIONAL_OPTIONS</code> variable if it is set when a target is created.</p>
<h3>Android_Api</h3>
<p>New in version 3.1. </p>
<p> When Cross Compiling for Android with NVIDIA Nsight Tegra Visual Studio Edition, this property sets the Android target API version (e.g. <code>15</code>). The version number must be a positive decimal integer. This property is initialized by the value of the <code>CMAKE_ANDROID_API</code> variable if it is set when a target is created.</p>
<h3>Android_Api_Min</h3>
<p>New in version 3.2. </p>
<p> Set the Android MIN API version (e.g. <code>9</code>). The version number must be a positive decimal integer. This property is initialized by the value of the <code>CMAKE_ANDROID_API_MIN</code> variable if it is set when a target is created. Native code builds using this API version.</p>
<h3>Android_Arch</h3>
<p>New in version 3.4. </p>
<p> When Cross Compiling for Android with NVIDIA Nsight Tegra Visual Studio Edition, this property sets the Android target architecture. </p>
<p> This is a string property that could be set to the one of the following values:</p>
<dl>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd><code>armv7-a</code>: &quot;ARMv7-A (armv7-a)&quot;</p>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd><code>armv7-a-hard</code>: &quot;ARMv7-A, hard-float ABI (armv7-a)&quot;</p>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd><code>arm64-v8a</code>: &quot;ARMv8-A, 64bit (arm64-v8a)&quot;</p>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd><code>x86</code>: &quot;x86 (x86)&quot;</p>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd><code>x86_64</code>: &quot;x86_64 (x86_64)&quot; </p>
<p> This property is initialized by the value of the <code>CMAKE_ANDROID_ARCH</code> variable if it is set when a target is created.</dl>
<h3>Android_Assets_Directories</h3>
<p>New in version 3.4. </p>
<p> Set the Android assets directories to copy into the main assets folder before build. This a string property that contains the directory paths separated by semicolon. This property is initialized by the value of the <code>CMAKE_ANDROID_ASSETS_DIRECTORIES</code> variable if it is set when a target is created.</p>
<h3>Android_Gui</h3>
<p>New in version 3.1. </p>
<p> When Cross Compiling for Android with NVIDIA Nsight Tegra Visual Studio Edition, this property specifies whether to build an executable as an application package on Android. </p>
<p> When this property is set to true the executable when built for Android will be created as an application package. This property is initialized by the value of the <code>CMAKE_ANDROID_GUI</code> variable if it is set when a target is created. </p>
<p> Add the <code>AndroidManifest.xml</code> source file explicitly to the target <code>add_executable()</code> command invocation to specify the root directory of the application package source.</p>
<h3>Android_Jar_Dependencies</h3>
<p>New in version 3.4. </p>
<p> Set the Android property that specifies JAR dependencies. This is a string value property. This property is initialized by the value of the <code>CMAKE_ANDROID_JAR_DEPENDENCIES</code> variable if it is set when a target is created.</p>
<h3>Android_Jar_Directories</h3>
<p>New in version 3.4. </p>
<p> Set the Android property that specifies directories to search for the JAR libraries. </p>
<p> This a string property that contains the directory paths separated by semicolons. This property is initialized by the value of the <code>CMAKE_ANDROID_JAR_DIRECTORIES</code> variable if it is set when a target is created. </p>
<p> Contents of <code>ANDROID_JAR_DIRECTORIES</code> may use &quot;generator expressions&quot; with the syntax <code>$&lt;&#8230;&gt;</code>. See the <code><a href="/docs/linux/man/7-cmake-generator-expressions/">cmake-generator-expressions</a>(7)</code> manual for available expressions.</p>
<h3>Android_Java_Source_Dir</h3>
<p>New in version 3.4. </p>
<p> Set the Android property that defines the Java source code root directories. This a string property that contains the directory paths separated by semicolon. This property is initialized by the value of the <code>CMAKE_ANDROID_JAVA_SOURCE_DIR</code> variable if it is set when a target is created.</p>
<h3>Android_Native_Lib_Dependencies</h3>
<p>New in version 3.4. </p>
<p> Set the Android property that specifies the .so dependencies. This is a string property. </p>
<p> This property is initialized by the value of the <code>CMAKE_ANDROID_NATIVE_LIB_DEPENDENCIES</code> variable if it is set when a target is created. </p>
<p> Contents of <code>ANDROID_NATIVE_LIB_DEPENDENCIES</code> may use &quot;generator expressions&quot; with the syntax <code>$&lt;&#8230;&gt;</code>. See the <code><a href="/docs/linux/man/7-cmake-generator-expressions/">cmake-generator-expressions</a>(7)</code> manual for available expressions.</p>
<h3>Android_Native_Lib_Directories</h3>
<p>New in version 3.4. </p>
<p> Set the Android property that specifies directories to search for the <code>.so</code> libraries. </p>
<p> This a string property that contains the directory paths separated by semicolons. </p>
<p> This property is initialized by the value of the <code>CMAKE_ANDROID_NATIVE_LIB_DIRECTORIES</code> variable if it is set when a target is created. </p>
<p> Contents of <code>ANDROID_NATIVE_LIB_DIRECTORIES</code> may use &quot;generator expressions&quot; with the syntax <code>$&lt;&#8230;&gt;</code>. See the <code><a href="/docs/linux/man/7-cmake-generator-expressions/">cmake-generator-expressions</a>(7)</code> manual for available expressions.</p>
<h3>Android_Process_Max</h3>
<p>New in version 3.4. </p>
<p> Set the Android property that defines the maximum number of a parallel Android NDK compiler processes (e.g. <code>4</code>). This property is initialized by the value of the <code>CMAKE_ANDROID_PROCESS_MAX</code> variable if it is set when a target is created.</p>
<h3>Android_Proguard</h3>
<p>New in version 3.4. </p>
<p> When this property is set to true that enables the ProGuard tool to shrink, optimize, and obfuscate the code by removing unused code and renaming classes, fields, and methods with semantically obscure names. This property is initialized by the value of the <code>CMAKE_ANDROID_PROGUARD</code> variable if it is set when a target is created.</p>
<h3>Android_Proguard_Config_Path</h3>
<p>New in version 3.4. </p>
<p> Set the Android property that specifies the location of the ProGuard config file. Leave empty to use the default one. This a string property that contains the path to ProGuard config file. This property is initialized by the value of the <code>CMAKE_ANDROID_PROGUARD_CONFIG_PATH</code> variable if it is set when a target is created.</p>
<h3>Android_Secure_Props_Path</h3>
<p>New in version 3.4. </p>
<p> Set the Android property that states the location of the secure properties file. This is a string property that contains the file path. This property is initialized by the value of the <code>CMAKE_ANDROID_SECURE_PROPS_PATH</code> variable if it is set when a target is created.</p>
<h3>Android_Skip_Ant_Step</h3>
<p>New in version 3.4. </p>
<p> Set the Android property that defines whether or not to skip the Ant build step. This is a boolean property initialized by the value of the <code>CMAKE_ANDROID_SKIP_ANT_STEP</code> variable if it is set when a target is created.</p>
<h3>Android_Stl_Type</h3>
<p>New in version 3.4. </p>
<p> When Cross Compiling for Android with NVIDIA Nsight Tegra Visual Studio Edition, this property specifies the type of STL support for the project. This is a string property that could set to the one of the following values:</p>
<dl>
<dt><code>none</code></p>
<dd>No C++ Support</p>
<dt><code>system</code></p>
<dd>Minimal C++ without STL</p>
<dt><code>gabi++_static</code></p>
<dd>GAbi++ Static</p>
<dt><code>gabi++_shared</code></p>
<dd>GAbi++ Shared</p>
<dt><code>gnustl_static</code></p>
<dd>GNU libstdc++ Static</p>
<dt><code>gnustl_shared</code></p>
<dd>GNU libstdc++ Shared</p>
<dt><code>stlport_static</code></p>
<dd>STLport Static</p>
<dt><code>stlport_shared</code></p>
<dd>STLport Shared </p>
<p> This property is initialized by the value of the <code>CMAKE_ANDROID_STL_TYPE</code> variable if it is set when a target is created.</dl>
<h3>Archive_Output_Directory</h3>
<p>Output directory in which to build ARCHIVE target files. </p>
<p> This property specifies the directory into which archive target files should be built. The property value may use <code>generator expressions</code>. Multi-configuration generators (Visual Studio, <code>Xcode</code>, <code>Ninja Multi-Config</code>) append a per-configuration subdirectory to the specified directory unless a generator expression is used. </p>
<p> This property is initialized by the value of the <code>CMAKE_ARCHIVE_OUTPUT_DIRECTORY</code> variable if it is set when a target is created. </p>
<p> See also the <code>ARCHIVE_OUTPUT_DIRECTORY_&lt;CONFIG&gt;</code> target property.</p>
<h3>ARCHIVE_OUTPUT_DIRECTORY_&lt;CONFIG&gt;</h3>
<p>Per-configuration output directory for ARCHIVE target files. </p>
<p> This is a per-configuration version of the <code>ARCHIVE_OUTPUT_DIRECTORY</code> target property, but multi-configuration generators (VS, Xcode) do NOT append a per-configuration subdirectory to the specified directory. This property is initialized by the value of the <code>CMAKE_ARCHIVE_OUTPUT_DIRECTORY_&lt;CONFIG&gt;</code> variable if it is set when a target is created. </p>
<p> Contents of <code>ARCHIVE_OUTPUT_DIRECTORY_&lt;CONFIG&gt;</code> may use <code>generator expressions</code>.</p>
<h3>Archive_Output_Name</h3>
<p>Output name for ARCHIVE target files. </p>
<p> This property specifies the base name for archive target files. It overrides <code>OUTPUT_NAME</code> and <code>OUTPUT_NAME_&lt;CONFIG&gt;</code> properties. </p>
<p> See also the <code>ARCHIVE_OUTPUT_NAME_&lt;CONFIG&gt;</code> target property.</p>
<h3>ARCHIVE_OUTPUT_NAME_&lt;CONFIG&gt;</h3>
<p>Per-configuration output name for ARCHIVE target files. </p>
<p> This is the configuration-specific version of the <code>ARCHIVE_OUTPUT_NAME</code> target property.</p>
<h3>Autogen_Build_Dir</h3>
<p>New in version 3.9. </p>
<p> Directory where <code>AUTOMOC</code>, <code>AUTOUIC</code> and <code>AUTORCC</code> generate files for the target. </p>
<p> The directory is created on demand and automatically added to the <code>ADDITIONAL_CLEAN_FILES</code> target property. </p>
<p> When unset or empty the directory <code>&lt;dir&gt;/&lt;target-name&gt;_autogen</code> is used where <code>&lt;dir&gt;</code> is <code>CMAKE_CURRENT_BINARY_DIR</code> and <code>&lt;target-name&gt;</code> is <code>NAME</code>. </p>
<p> By default <i>AUTOGEN_BUILD_DIR</i> is unset. </p>
<p> See the <code><a href="/docs/linux/man/7-cmake-qt/">cmake-qt</a>(7)</code> manual for more information on using CMake with Qt.</p>
<h3>Autogen_Origin_Depends</h3>
<p>New in version 3.14. </p>
<p> Switch for forwarding origin target dependencies to the corresponding <code>_autogen</code> target. </p>
<p> Targets which have their <code>AUTOMOC</code> or <code>AUTOUIC</code> property <code>ON</code> have a corresponding <code>_autogen</code> target which generates <code>moc</code> and <code>uic</code> files. As this <code>_autogen</code> target is created at generate-time, it is not possible to define dependencies of it using e.g. <code>add_dependencies()</code>. Instead the <i>AUTOGEN_ORIGIN_DEPENDS</i> target property decides whether the origin target dependencies should be forwarded to the <code>_autogen</code> target or not. </p>
<p> By default <i>AUTOGEN_ORIGIN_DEPENDS</i> is initialized from <code>CMAKE_AUTOGEN_ORIGIN_DEPENDS</code> which is <code>ON</code> by default. </p>
<p> In total the dependencies of the <code>_autogen</code> target are composed from</p>
<dl>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd>forwarded origin target dependencies (enabled by default via <i>AUTOGEN_ORIGIN_DEPENDS</i>)</p>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd>additional user defined dependencies from <code>AUTOGEN_TARGET_DEPENDS</code> </p>
<p> See the <code><a href="/docs/linux/man/7-cmake-qt/">cmake-qt</a>(7)</code> manual for more information on using CMake with Qt.</dl>
<h3>Note</h3>
<p>Disabling <i>AUTOGEN_ORIGIN_DEPENDS</i> is useful to avoid building of origin target dependencies when building the <code>_autogen</code> target only. This is especially interesting when a <code>global autogen target</code> is enabled. </p>
<p> When the <code>_autogen</code> target doesn&#8217;t require all the origin target&#8217;s dependencies, and <i>AUTOGEN_ORIGIN_DEPENDS</i> is disabled, it might be necessary to extend <code>AUTOGEN_TARGET_DEPENDS</code> to add missing dependencies.</p>
<h3>Autogen_Parallel</h3>
<p>New in version 3.11. </p>
<p> Number of parallel <code>moc</code> or <code>uic</code> processes to start when using <code>AUTOMOC</code> and <code>AUTOUIC</code>. </p>
<p> The custom <code>&lt;origin&gt;_autogen</code> target starts a number of threads of which each one parses a source file and on demand starts a <code>moc</code> or <code>uic</code> process. <code>AUTOGEN_PARALLEL</code> controls how many parallel threads (and therefore <code>moc</code> or <code>uic</code> processes) are started.</p>
<dl>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd>An empty (or unset) value or the string <code>AUTO</code> sets the number of threads/processes to the number of physical CPUs on the host system.</p>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd>A positive non zero integer value sets the exact thread/process count.</p>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd>Otherwise a single thread/process is started. </p>
<p> By default <code>AUTOGEN_PARALLEL</code> is initialized from <code>CMAKE_AUTOGEN_PARALLEL</code>. </p>
<p> See the <code><a href="/docs/linux/man/7-cmake-qt/">cmake-qt</a>(7)</code> manual for more information on using CMake with Qt.</dl>
<h3>Autogen_Target_Depends</h3>
<p>Additional target dependencies of the corresponding <code>_autogen</code> target. </p>
<p> Targets which have their <code>AUTOMOC</code> or <code>AUTOUIC</code> property <code>ON</code> have a corresponding <code>_autogen</code> target which generates <code>moc</code> and <code>uic</code> files. As this <code>_autogen</code> target is created at generate-time, it is not possible to define dependencies of it using e.g. <code>add_dependencies()</code>. Instead the <i>AUTOGEN_TARGET_DEPENDS</i> target property can be set to a ;-list of additional dependencies for the <code>_autogen</code> target. Dependencies can be target names or file names. </p>
<p> In total the dependencies of the <code>_autogen</code> target are composed from</p>
<dl>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd>forwarded origin target dependencies (enabled by default via <code>AUTOGEN_ORIGIN_DEPENDS</code>)</p>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd>additional user defined dependencies from <i>AUTOGEN_TARGET_DEPENDS</i> </p>
<p> See the <code><a href="/docs/linux/man/7-cmake-qt/">cmake-qt</a>(7)</code> manual for more information on using CMake with Qt.</dl>
<h3>Use cases</h3>
<p>If <code>AUTOMOC</code> or <code>AUTOUIC</code> depends on a file that is either</p>
<dl>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd>a <code>GENERATED</code> non C++ file (e.g. a <code>GENERATED</code> <code>.json</code> or <code>.ui</code> file) or</p>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd>a <code>GENERATED</code> C++ file that isn&#8217;t recognized by <code>AUTOMOC</code> and <code>AUTOUIC</code> because it&#8217;s skipped by <code>SKIP_AUTOMOC</code>, <code>SKIP_AUTOUIC</code>, <code>SKIP_AUTOGEN</code> or <code>CMP0071</code> or</p>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd>a file that isn&#8217;t in the origin target&#8217;s sources </p>
<p> it must be added to <i>AUTOGEN_TARGET_DEPENDS</i>.</dl>
<h3>Automoc</h3>
<p>Should the target be processed with auto-moc (for Qt projects). </p>
<p> <i>AUTOMOC</i> is a boolean specifying whether CMake will handle the Qt <code>moc</code> preprocessor automatically, i.e. without having to use commands like <code>QT4_WRAP_CPP()</code>, <code>QT5_WRAP_CPP()</code>, etc. Currently, Qt versions 4 to 6 are supported. </p>
<p> This property is initialized by the value of the <code>CMAKE_AUTOMOC</code> variable if it is set when a target is created. </p>
<p> When this property is set <code>ON</code>, CMake will scan the header and source files at build time and invoke <code>moc</code> accordingly.</p>
<h3>Header file processing</h3>
<p>At configuration time, a list of header files that should be scanned by <i>AUTOMOC</i> is computed from the target&#8217;s sources.</p>
<dl>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd>All header files in the target&#8217;s sources are added to the scan list.</p>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd>For all C++ source files <code>&lt;source_base&gt;.&lt;source_extension&gt;</code> in the target&#8217;s sources, CMake searches for</p>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd>a regular header with the same base name (<code>&lt;source_base&gt;.&lt;header_extention&gt;</code>) and</p>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd>a private header with the same base name and a <code>_p</code> suffix (<code>&lt;source_base&gt;_p.&lt;header_extention&gt;</code>) </p>
<p> and adds these to the scan list. </p>
<p> At build time, CMake scans each unknown or modified header file from the list and searches for</p>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd>a Qt macro from <code>AUTOMOC_MACRO_NAMES</code>,</p>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd>additional file dependencies from the <code>FILE</code> argument of a <code>Q_PLUGIN_METADATA</code> macro and</p>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd>additional file dependencies detected by filters defined in <code>AUTOMOC_DEPEND_FILTERS</code>. </p>
<p> If a Qt macro is found, then the header will be compiled by the <code>moc</code> to the output file <code>moc_&lt;base_name&gt;.cpp</code>. The complete output file path is described in the section <i>Output file location</i>. </p>
<p> The header will be <code>moc</code> compiled again if a file from the additional file dependencies changes. </p>
<p> Header <code>moc</code> output files <code>moc_&lt;base_name&gt;.cpp</code> can be included in source files. In the section <i>Including header moc files in sources</i> there is more information on that topic.</dl>
<h3>Source file processing</h3>
<p>At build time, CMake scans each unknown or modified C++ source file from the target&#8217;s sources for</p>
<dl>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd>a Qt macro from <code>AUTOMOC_MACRO_NAMES</code>,</p>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd>includes of header <code>moc</code> files (see <i>Including header moc files in sources</i>),</p>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd>additional file dependencies from the <code>FILE</code> argument of a <code>Q_PLUGIN_METADATA</code> macro and</p>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd>additional file dependencies detected by filters defined in <code>AUTOMOC_DEPEND_FILTERS</code>. </p>
<p> If a Qt macro is found, then the C++ source file <code>&lt;base&gt;.&lt;source_extension&gt;</code> is expected to as well contain an include statement </p>
<pre>#include &lt;&lt;base&gt;.moc&gt; // or
#include &quot;&lt;base&gt;.moc&quot;</pre>
<p>The source file then will be compiled by the <code>moc</code> to the output file <code>&lt;base&gt;.moc</code>. A description of the complete output file path is in section <i>Output file location</i>. </p>
<p> The source will be <code>moc</code> compiled again if a file from the additional file dependencies changes.</dl>
<h3>Including header moc files in sources</h3>
<p>A source file can include the <code>moc</code> output file of a header <code>&lt;header_base&gt;.&lt;header_extension&gt;</code> by using an include statement of the form </p>
<pre>#include &lt;moc_&lt;header_base&gt;.cpp&gt; // or
#include &quot;moc_&lt;header_base&gt;.cpp&quot;</pre>
<p>If the <code>moc</code> output file of a header is included by a source, it will be generated in a different location than if it was not included. This is described in the section <i>Output file location</i>.</p>
<h3>Output file location</h3>
<h3>Included moc output files</h3>
<p><code>moc</code> output files that are included by a source file will be generated in</p>
<dl>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd><code>&lt;AUTOGEN_BUILD_DIR&gt;/include</code> for single configuration generators or in</p>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd><code>&lt;AUTOGEN_BUILD_DIR&gt;/include_&lt;CONFIG&gt;</code> for <code>multi configuration</code> generators. </p>
<p> Where <code>&lt;AUTOGEN_BUILD_DIR&gt;</code> is the value of the target property <code>AUTOGEN_BUILD_DIR</code>. </p>
<p> The include directory is automatically added to the target&#8217;s <code>INCLUDE_DIRECTORIES</code>.</dl>
<h3>Not included moc output files</h3>
<p><code>moc</code> output files that are not included in a source file will be generated in</p>
<dl>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd><code>&lt;AUTOGEN_BUILD_DIR&gt;/&lt;SOURCE_DIR_CHECKSUM&gt;</code> for single configuration generators or in,</p>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd><code>&lt;AUTOGEN_BUILD_DIR&gt;/include_&lt;CONFIG&gt;/&lt;SOURCE_DIR_CHECKSUM&gt;</code> for <code>multi configuration</code> generators. </p>
<p> Where <code>&lt;SOURCE_DIR_CHECKSUM&gt;</code> is a checksum computed from the relative parent directory path of the <code>moc</code> input file. This scheme allows to have <code>moc</code> input files with the same name in different directories. </p>
<p> All not included <code>moc</code> output files will be included automatically by the CMake generated file</p>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd><code>&lt;AUTOGEN_BUILD_DIR&gt;/mocs_compilation.cpp</code>, or</p>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd><code>&lt;AUTOGEN_BUILD_DIR&gt;/mocs_compilation_$&lt;CONFIG&gt;.cpp</code>, </p>
<p> which is added to the target&#8217;s sources.</dl>
<h3>Qt version detection</h3>
<p><i>AUTOMOC</i> enabled targets need to know the Qt major and minor version they&#8217;re working with. The major version usually is provided by the <code>INTERFACE_QT_MAJOR_VERSION</code> property of the <code>Qt[456]Core</code> library, that the target links to. To find the minor version, CMake builds a list of available Qt versions from</p>
<dl>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd><code>Qt6Core_VERSION_MAJOR</code> and <code>Qt6Core_VERSION_MINOR</code> variables (usually set by <code>find_package(Qt6&#8230;)</code>)</p>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd><code>Qt6Core_VERSION_MAJOR</code> and <code>Qt6Core_VERSION_MINOR</code> directory properties</p>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd><code>Qt5Core_VERSION_MAJOR</code> and <code>Qt5Core_VERSION_MINOR</code> variables (usually set by <code>find_package(Qt5&#8230;)</code>)</p>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd><code>Qt5Core_VERSION_MAJOR</code> and <code>Qt5Core_VERSION_MINOR</code> directory properties</p>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd><code>QT_VERSION_MAJOR</code> and <code>QT_VERSION_MINOR</code> variables (usually set by <code>find_package(Qt4&#8230;)</code>)</p>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd><code>QT_VERSION_MAJOR</code> and <code>QT_VERSION_MINOR</code> directory properties </p>
<p> in the context of the <code>add_executable()</code> or <code>add_library()</code> call. </p>
<p> Assumed <code>INTERFACE_QT_MAJOR_VERSION</code> is a valid number, the first entry in the list with a matching major version is taken. If no matching major version was found, an error is generated. If <code>INTERFACE_QT_MAJOR_VERSION</code> is not a valid number, the first entry in the list is taken. </p>
<p> A <code>find_package(Qt[456]&#8230;)</code> call sets the <code>QT/Qt[56]Core_VERSION_MAJOR/MINOR</code> variables. If the call is in a different context than the <code>add_executable()</code> or <code>add_library()</code> call, e.g. in a function, then the version variables might not be available to the <i>AUTOMOC</i> enabled target. In that case the version variables can be forwarded from the <code>find_package(Qt[456]&#8230;)</code> calling context to the <code>add_executable()</code> or <code>add_library()</code> calling context as directory properties. The following Qt5 example demonstrates the procedure. </p>
<pre>function (add_qt5_client)
 find_package(Qt5 REQUIRED QUIET COMPONENTS Core Widgets)
 ...
 set_property(DIRECTORY &quot;${CMAKE_CURRENT_SOURCE_DIR}&quot;
 PROPERTY Qt5Core_VERSION_MAJOR &quot;${Qt5Core_VERSION_MAJOR}&quot;)
 set_property(DIRECTORY &quot;${CMAKE_CURRENT_SOURCE_DIR}&quot;
 PROPERTY Qt5Core_VERSION_MINOR &quot;${Qt5Core_VERSION_MAJOR}&quot;)
 ...
endfunction ()
...
add_qt5_client()
add_executable(myTarget main.cpp)
target_link_libraries(myTarget Qt5::QtWidgets)
set_property(TARGET myTarget PROPERTY AUTOMOC ON)</pre>
</dl>
<h3>Modifiers</h3>
<p><code>AUTOMOC_EXECUTABLE</code>: The <code>moc</code> executable will be detected automatically, but can be forced to a certain binary using this target property. </p>
<p> <code>AUTOMOC_MOC_OPTIONS</code>: Additional command line options for <code>moc</code> can be set in this target property. </p>
<p> <code>AUTOMOC_MACRO_NAMES</code>: This list of Qt macro names can be extended to search for additional macros in headers and sources. </p>
<p> <code>AUTOMOC_DEPEND_FILTERS</code>: <code>moc</code> dependency file names can be extracted from headers or sources by defining file name filters in this target property. </p>
<p> <code>AUTOMOC_COMPILER_PREDEFINES</code>: Compiler pre definitions for <code>moc</code> are written to the <code>moc_predefs.h</code> file. The generation of this file can be enabled or disabled in this target property. </p>
<p> <code>SKIP_AUTOMOC</code>: Sources and headers can be excluded from <i>AUTOMOC</i> processing by setting this source file property. </p>
<p> <code>SKIP_AUTOGEN</code>: Source files can be excluded from <i>AUTOMOC</i>, <code>AUTOUIC</code> and <code>AUTORCC</code> processing by setting this source file property. </p>
<p> <code>AUTOGEN_SOURCE_GROUP</code>: This global property can be used to group files generated by <i>AUTOMOC</i> or <code>AUTORCC</code> together in an IDE, e.g. in MSVS. </p>
<p> <code>AUTOGEN_TARGETS_FOLDER</code>: This global property can be used to group <i>AUTOMOC</i>, <code>AUTOUIC</code> and <code>AUTORCC</code> targets together in an IDE, e.g. in MSVS. </p>
<p> <code>CMAKE_GLOBAL_AUTOGEN_TARGET</code>: A global <code>autogen</code> target, that depends on all <i>AUTOMOC</i> or <code>AUTOUIC</code> generated <code>&lt;ORIGIN&gt;_autogen</code> targets in the project, will be generated when this variable is <code>ON</code>. </p>
<p> <code>AUTOGEN_PARALLEL</code>: This target property controls the number of <code>moc</code> or <code>uic</code> processes to start in parallel during builds. </p>
<p> See the <code><a href="/docs/linux/man/7-cmake-qt/">cmake-qt</a>(7)</code> manual for more information on using CMake with Qt.</p>
<h3>Automoc_Compiler_Predefines</h3>
<p>New in version 3.10. </p>
<p> Boolean value used by <code>AUTOMOC</code> to determine if the compiler pre definitions file <code>moc_predefs.h</code> should be generated. </p>
<p> CMake generates a <code>moc_predefs.h</code> file with compiler pre definitions from the output of the command defined in <code>CMAKE_CXX_COMPILER_PREDEFINES_COMMAND</code> when</p>
<dl>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd><code>AUTOMOC</code> is enabled,</p>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd><i>AUTOMOC_COMPILER_PREDEFINES</i> is enabled,</p>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd><code>CMAKE_CXX_COMPILER_PREDEFINES_COMMAND</code> isn&#8217;t empty and</p>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd>the Qt version is greater or equal 5.8. </p>
<p> The <code>moc_predefs.h</code> file, which is generated in <code>AUTOGEN_BUILD_DIR</code>, is passed to <code>moc</code> as the argument to the <code>&#8211;include</code> option. </p>
<p> By default <i>AUTOMOC_COMPILER_PREDEFINES</i> is initialized from <code>CMAKE_AUTOMOC_COMPILER_PREDEFINES</code>, which is ON by default. </p>
<p> See the <code><a href="/docs/linux/man/7-cmake-qt/">cmake-qt</a>(7)</code> manual for more information on using CMake with Qt.</dl>
<h3>Automoc_Depend_Filters</h3>
<p>New in version 3.9. </p>
<p> Filter definitions used by <code>AUTOMOC</code> to extract file names from a source file that are registered as additional dependencies for the <code>moc</code> file of the source file. </p>
<p> Filters are defined as <code>KEYWORD;REGULAR_EXPRESSION</code> pairs. First the file content is searched for <code>KEYWORD</code>. If it is found at least once, then file names are extracted by successively searching for <code>REGULAR_EXPRESSION</code> and taking the first match group. </p>
<p> The file name found in the first match group is searched for</p>
<dl>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd>first in the vicinity of the source file</p>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd>and afterwards in the target&#8217;s <code>INCLUDE_DIRECTORIES</code>. </p>
<p> If any of the extracted files changes, then the <code>moc</code> file for the source file gets rebuilt even when the source file itself doesn&#8217;t change. </p>
<p> If any of the extracted files is <code>GENERATED</code> or if it is not in the target&#8217;s sources, then it might be necessary to add it to the <code>_autogen</code> target dependencies. See <code>AUTOGEN_TARGET_DEPENDS</code> for reference. </p>
<p> By default <i>AUTOMOC_DEPEND_FILTERS</i> is initialized from <code>CMAKE_AUTOMOC_DEPEND_FILTERS</code>, which is empty by default. </p>
<p> From Qt 5.15.0 on this variable is ignored as moc is able to output the correct dependencies. </p>
<p> See the <code><a href="/docs/linux/man/7-cmake-qt/">cmake-qt</a>(7)</code> manual for more information on using CMake with Qt.</dl>
<h3>Example 1</h3>
