<p>A header file <code>my_class.hpp</code> uses a custom macro <code>JSON_FILE_MACRO</code> which is defined in an other header <code>macros.hpp</code>. We want the <code>moc</code> file of <code>my_class.hpp</code> to depend on the file name argument of <code>JSON_FILE_MACRO</code>: </p>
<pre>// my_class.hpp
class My_Class : public QObject
{
 Q_OBJECT
 JSON_FILE_MACRO ( &quot;info.json&quot; )
...
};</pre>
<p>In <code>CMakeLists.txt</code> we add a filter to <code>CMAKE_AUTOMOC_DEPEND_FILTERS</code> like this: </p>
<pre>list( APPEND CMAKE_AUTOMOC_DEPEND_FILTERS
 &quot;JSON_FILE_MACRO&quot;
 &quot;[
][ 	]*JSON_FILE_MACRO[ 	]*\([ 	]*\&quot;([^\&quot;]+)\&quot;&quot;
)</pre>
<p>We assume <code>info.json</code> is a plain (not <code>GENERATED</code>) file that is listed in the target&#8217;s source. Therefore we do not need to add it to <code>AUTOGEN_TARGET_DEPENDS</code>.</p>
<h3>Example 2</h3>
<p>In the target <code>my_target</code> a header file <code>complex_class.hpp</code> uses a custom macro <code>JSON_BASED_CLASS</code> which is defined in an other header <code>macros.hpp</code>: </p>
<pre>// macros.hpp
...
#define JSON_BASED_CLASS(name, json) \
class name : public QObject \
{ \
 Q_OBJECT \
 Q_PLUGIN_METADATA(IID &quot;demo&quot; FILE json) \
 name() {} \
};
...</pre>
<pre>// complex_class.hpp
#pragma once
JSON_BASED_CLASS(Complex_Class, &quot;meta.json&quot;)
// end of file</pre>
<p>Since <code>complex_class.hpp</code> doesn&#8217;t contain a <code>Q_OBJECT</code> macro it would be ignored by <code>AUTOMOC</code>. We change this by adding <code>JSON_BASED_CLASS</code> to <code>CMAKE_AUTOMOC_MACRO_NAMES</code>: </p>
<pre>list(APPEND CMAKE_AUTOMOC_MACRO_NAMES &quot;JSON_BASED_CLASS&quot;)</pre>
<p>We want the <code>moc</code> file of <code>complex_class.hpp</code> to depend on <code>meta.json</code>. So we add a filter to <code>CMAKE_AUTOMOC_DEPEND_FILTERS</code>: </p>
<pre>list(APPEND CMAKE_AUTOMOC_DEPEND_FILTERS
 &quot;JSON_BASED_CLASS&quot;
 &quot;[
^][ 	]*JSON_BASED_CLASS[ 	]*\([^,]*,[ 	]*\&quot;([^\&quot;]+)\&quot;&quot;
)</pre>
<p>Additionally we assume <code>meta.json</code> is <code>GENERATED</code> which is why we have to add it to <code>AUTOGEN_TARGET_DEPENDS</code>: </p>
<pre>set_property(TARGET my_target APPEND PROPERTY AUTOGEN_TARGET_DEPENDS &quot;meta.json&quot;)</pre>
<h3>Automoc_Executable</h3>
<p>New in version 3.14. </p>
<p> <i>AUTOMOC_EXECUTABLE</i> is file path pointing to the <code>moc</code> executable to use for <code>AUTOMOC</code> enabled files. Setting this property will make CMake skip the automatic detection of the <code>moc</code> binary as well as the sanity-tests normally run to ensure that the binary is available and working as expected. </p>
<p> Usually this property does not need to be set. Only consider this property if auto-detection of <code>moc</code> can not work &#8212; e.g. because you are building the <code>moc</code> binary as part of your project. </p>
<p> See the <code><a href="/docs/linux/man/7-cmake-qt/">cmake-qt</a>(7)</code> manual for more information on using CMake with Qt.</p>
<h3>Automoc_Macro_Names</h3>
<p>New in version 3.10. </p>
<p> A semicolon-separated list list of macro names used by <code>AUTOMOC</code> to determine if a C++ file needs to be processed by <code>moc</code>. </p>
<p> This property is only used if the <code>AUTOMOC</code> property is <code>ON</code> for this target. </p>
<p> When running <code>AUTOMOC</code>, CMake searches for the strings listed in <i>AUTOMOC_MACRO_NAMES</i> in C++ source and header files. If any of the strings is found</p>
<dl>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd>as the first non space string on a new line or</p>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd>as the first non space string after a <code>{</code> on a new line, </p>
<p> then the file will be processed by <code>moc</code>. </p>
<p> By default <i>AUTOMOC_MACRO_NAMES</i> is initialized from <code>CMAKE_AUTOMOC_MACRO_NAMES</code>. </p>
<p> See the <code><a href="/docs/linux/man/7-cmake-qt/">cmake-qt</a>(7)</code> manual for more information on using CMake with Qt.</dl>
<h3>Example</h3>
<p>In this case the <code>Q_OBJECT</code> macro is hidden inside another macro called <code>CUSTOM_MACRO</code>. To let CMake know that source files that contain <code>CUSTOM_MACRO</code> need to be <code>moc</code> processed, we call: </p>
<pre>set_property(TARGET tgt APPEND PROPERTY AUTOMOC_MACRO_NAMES &quot;CUSTOM_MACRO&quot;)</pre>
<h3>Automoc_Moc_Options</h3>
<p>Additional options for <code>moc</code> when using <code>AUTOMOC</code> </p>
<p> This property is only used if the <code>AUTOMOC</code> property is <code>ON</code> for this target. In this case, it holds additional command line options which will be used when <code>moc</code> is executed during the build, i.e. it is equivalent to the optional <code>OPTIONS</code> argument of the <code>qt4_wrap_cpp()</code> macro. </p>
<p> This property is initialized by the value of the <code>CMAKE_AUTOMOC_MOC_OPTIONS</code> variable if it is set when a target is created, or an empty string otherwise. </p>
<p> See the <code><a href="/docs/linux/man/7-cmake-qt/">cmake-qt</a>(7)</code> manual for more information on using CMake with Qt.</p>
<h3>Automoc_Path_Prefix</h3>
<p>New in version 3.16. </p>
<p> When this property is <code>ON</code>, CMake will generate the <code>-p</code> path prefix option for <code>moc</code> on <code>AUTOMOC</code> enabled Qt targets. </p>
<p> To generate the path prefix, CMake tests if the header compiled by <code>moc</code> is in any of the target <code>include directories</code>. If so, CMake will compute the relative path accordingly. If the header is not in the <code>include directories</code>, CMake will omit the <code>-p</code> path prefix option. <code>moc</code> usually generates a relative include path in that case. </p>
<p> <i>AUTOMOC_PATH_PREFIX</i> is initialized from the variable <code>CMAKE_AUTOMOC_PATH_PREFIX</code>, which is <code>OFF</code> by default. </p>
<p> See the <code><a href="/docs/linux/man/7-cmake-qt/">cmake-qt</a>(7)</code> manual for more information on using CMake with Qt.</p>
<h3>Reproducible builds</h3>
<p>For reproducible builds it is recommended to keep headers that are <code>moc</code> compiled in one of the target <code>include directories</code> and set <i>AUTOMOC_PATH_PREFIX</i> to <code>ON</code>. This ensures that:</p>
<dl>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd><code>moc</code> output files are identical on different build setups,</p>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd><code>moc</code> output files will compile correctly when the source and/or build directory is a symbolic link.</dl>
<h3>Autorcc</h3>
<p>Should the target be processed with auto-rcc (for Qt projects). </p>
<p> <i>AUTORCC</i> is a boolean specifying whether CMake will handle the Qt <code>rcc</code> code generator automatically, i.e. without having to use commands like <code>QT4_ADD_RESOURCES()</code>, <code>QT5_ADD_RESOURCES()</code>, etc. Currently, Qt versions 4 to 6 are supported. </p>
<p> When this property is <code>ON</code>, CMake will handle <code>.qrc</code> files added as target sources at build time and invoke <code>rcc</code> accordingly. This property is initialized by the value of the <code>CMAKE_AUTORCC</code> variable if it is set when a target is created. </p>
<p> By default <i>AUTORCC</i> is processed by a <code>custom command</code>. If the <code>.qrc</code> file is <code>GENERATED</code>, a <code>custom target</code> is used instead. </p>
<p> When there are multiple <code>.qrc</code> files with the same name, CMake will generate unspecified unique output file names for <code>rcc</code>. Therefore, if <code>Q_INIT_RESOURCE()</code> or <code>Q_CLEANUP_RESOURCE()</code> need to be used, the <code>.qrc</code> file name must be unique.</p>
<h3>Modifiers</h3>
<p><code>AUTORCC_EXECUTABLE</code>: The <code>rcc</code> executable will be detected automatically, but can be forced to a certain binary by setting this target property. </p>
<p> <code>AUTORCC_OPTIONS</code>: Additional command line options for <code>rcc</code> can be set via this target property. The corresponding <code>AUTORCC_OPTIONS</code> source file property can be used to specify options to be applied only to a specific <code>.qrc</code> file. </p>
<p> <code>SKIP_AUTORCC</code>: <code>.qrc</code> files can be excluded from <i>AUTORCC</i> processing by setting this source file property. </p>
<p> <code>SKIP_AUTOGEN</code>: Source files can be excluded from <code>AUTOMOC</code>, <code>AUTOUIC</code> and <i>AUTORCC</i> processing by setting this source file property. </p>
<p> <code>AUTOGEN_SOURCE_GROUP</code>: This global property can be used to group files generated by <code>AUTOMOC</code> or <i>AUTORCC</i> together in an IDE, e.g. in MSVS. </p>
<p> <code>AUTOGEN_TARGETS_FOLDER</code>: This global property can be used to group <code>AUTOMOC</code>, <code>AUTOUIC</code> and <i>AUTORCC</i> targets together in an IDE, e.g. in MSVS. </p>
<p> <code>CMAKE_GLOBAL_AUTORCC_TARGET</code>: A global <code>autorcc</code> target that depends on all <i>AUTORCC</i> targets in the project will be generated when this variable is <code>ON</code>. </p>
<p> See the <code><a href="/docs/linux/man/7-cmake-qt/">cmake-qt</a>(7)</code> manual for more information on using CMake with Qt.</p>
<h3>Autorcc_Executable</h3>
<p>New in version 3.14. </p>
<p> <i>AUTORCC_EXECUTABLE</i> is file path pointing to the <code>rcc</code> executable to use for <code>AUTORCC</code> enabled files. Setting this property will make CMake skip the automatic detection of the <code>rcc</code> binary as well as the sanity-tests normally run to ensure that the binary is available and working as expected. </p>
<p> Usually this property does not need to be set. Only consider this property if auto-detection of <code>rcc</code> can not work &#8212; e.g. because you are building the <code>rcc</code> binary as part of your project. </p>
<p> See the <code><a href="/docs/linux/man/7-cmake-qt/">cmake-qt</a>(7)</code> manual for more information on using CMake with Qt.</p>
<h3>Autorcc_Options</h3>
<p>Additional options for <code>rcc</code> when using <code>AUTORCC</code> </p>
<p> This property holds additional command line options which will be used when <code>rcc</code> is executed during the build via <code>AUTORCC</code>, i.e. it is equivalent to the optional <code>OPTIONS</code> argument of the <code>qt4_add_resources()</code> macro. </p>
<p> This property is initialized by the value of the <code>CMAKE_AUTORCC_OPTIONS</code> variable if it is set when a target is created, or an empty string otherwise. </p>
<p> The options set on the target may be overridden by <code>AUTORCC_OPTIONS</code> set on the <code>.qrc</code> source file. </p>
<p> See the <code><a href="/docs/linux/man/7-cmake-qt/">cmake-qt</a>(7)</code> manual for more information on using CMake with Qt.</p>
<h3>Example</h3>
<pre># ...
set_property(TARGET tgt PROPERTY AUTORCC_OPTIONS &quot;--compress;9&quot;)
# ...</pre>
<h3>Autouic</h3>
<p>Should the target be processed with auto-uic (for Qt projects). </p>
<p> <i>AUTOUIC</i> is a boolean specifying whether CMake will handle the Qt <code>uic</code> code generator automatically, i.e. without having to use commands like <code>QT4_WRAP_UI()</code>, <code>QT5_WRAP_UI()</code>, etc. Currently, Qt versions 4 to 6 are supported. </p>
<p> This property is initialized by the value of the <code>CMAKE_AUTOUIC</code> variable if it is set when a target is created. </p>
<p> When this property is <code>ON</code>, CMake will scan the header and source files at build time and invoke <code>uic</code> accordingly.</p>
<h3>Header and source file processing</h3>
<p>At build time, CMake scans each header and source file from the target&#8217;s sources for include statements of the form </p>
<pre>#include &quot;ui_&lt;ui_base&gt;.h&quot;</pre>
<p>Once such an include statement is found in a file, CMake searches for the <code>uic</code> input file <code>&lt;ui_base&gt;.ui</code></p>
<dl>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd>in the vicinity of the file and</p>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd>in the <code>AUTOUIC_SEARCH_PATHS</code> of the target. </p>
<p> If the <code>&lt;ui_base&gt;.ui</code> file was found, <code>uic</code> is called on it to generate <code>ui_&lt;ui_base&gt;.h</code> in the directory</p>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd><code>&lt;AUTOGEN_BUILD_DIR&gt;/include</code> for single configuration generators or in</p>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd><code>&lt;AUTOGEN_BUILD_DIR&gt;/include_&lt;CONFIG&gt;</code> for <code>multi configuration</code> generators. </p>
<p> Where <code>&lt;AUTOGEN_BUILD_DIR&gt;</code> is the value of the target property <code>AUTOGEN_BUILD_DIR</code>. </p>
<p> The include directory is automatically added to the target&#8217;s <code>INCLUDE_DIRECTORIES</code>.</dl>
<h3>Modifiers</h3>
<p><code>AUTOUIC_EXECUTABLE</code>: The <code>uic</code> executable will be detected automatically, but can be forced to a certain binary using this target property. </p>
<p> <code>AUTOUIC_OPTIONS</code>: Additional command line options for <code>uic</code> can be set via this target property. The corresponding <code>AUTOUIC_OPTIONS</code> source file property can be used to specify options to be applied only to a specific <code>&lt;base_name&gt;.ui</code> file. </p>
<p> <code>SKIP_AUTOUIC</code>: Source files can be excluded from <i>AUTOUIC</i> processing by setting this source file property. </p>
<p> <code>SKIP_AUTOGEN</code>: Source files can be excluded from <code>AUTOMOC</code>, <i>AUTOUIC</i> and <code>AUTORCC</code> processing by setting this source file property. </p>
<p> <code>AUTOGEN_TARGETS_FOLDER</code>: This global property can be used to group <code>AUTOMOC</code>, <i>AUTOUIC</i> and <code>AUTORCC</code> targets together in an IDE, e.g. in MSVS. </p>
<p> <code>CMAKE_GLOBAL_AUTOGEN_TARGET</code>: A global <code>autogen</code> target, that depends on all <code>AUTOMOC</code> or <i>AUTOUIC</i> generated <code>&lt;ORIGIN&gt;_autogen</code> targets in the project, will be generated when this variable is <code>ON</code>. </p>
<p> <code>AUTOGEN_PARALLEL</code>: This target property controls the number of <code>moc</code> or <code>uic</code> processes to start in parallel during builds. </p>
<p> See the <code><a href="/docs/linux/man/7-cmake-qt/">cmake-qt</a>(7)</code> manual for more information on using CMake with Qt.</p>
<h3>Autouic_Executable</h3>
<p>New in version 3.14. </p>
<p> <i>AUTOUIC_EXECUTABLE</i> is file path pointing to the <code>uic</code> executable to use for <code>AUTOUIC</code> enabled files. Setting this property will make CMake skip the automatic detection of the <code>uic</code> binary as well as the sanity-tests normally run to ensure that the binary is available and working as expected. </p>
<p> Usually this property does not need to be set. Only consider this property if auto-detection of <code>uic</code> can not work &#8212; e.g. because you are building the <code>uic</code> binary as part of your project. </p>
<p> See the <code><a href="/docs/linux/man/7-cmake-qt/">cmake-qt</a>(7)</code> manual for more information on using CMake with Qt.</p>
<h3>Autouic_Options</h3>
<p>Additional options for <code>uic</code> when using <code>AUTOUIC</code> </p>
<p> This property holds additional command line options which will be used when <code>uic</code> is executed during the build via <code>AUTOUIC</code>, i.e. it is equivalent to the optional <code>OPTIONS</code> argument of the <code>qt4_wrap_ui()</code> macro. </p>
<p> This property is initialized by the value of the <code>CMAKE_AUTOUIC_OPTIONS</code> variable if it is set when a target is created, or an empty string otherwise. </p>
<p> The options set on the target may be overridden by <code>AUTOUIC_OPTIONS</code> set on the <code>.ui</code> source file. </p>
<p> This property may use &quot;generator expressions&quot; with the syntax <code>$&lt;&#8230;&gt;</code>. See the <code><a href="/docs/linux/man/7-cmake-generator-expressions/">cmake-generator-expressions</a>(7)</code> manual for available expressions. </p>
<p> See the <code><a href="/docs/linux/man/7-cmake-qt/">cmake-qt</a>(7)</code> manual for more information on using CMake with Qt.</p>
<h3>Example</h3>
<pre># ...
set_property(TARGET tgt PROPERTY AUTOUIC_OPTIONS &quot;--no-protection&quot;)
# ...</pre>
<h3>Autouic_Search_Paths</h3>
<p>New in version 3.9. </p>
<p> Search path list used by <code>AUTOUIC</code> to find included <code>.ui</code> files. </p>
<p> This property is initialized by the value of the <code>CMAKE_AUTOUIC_SEARCH_PATHS</code> variable if it is set when a target is created. Otherwise it is empty. </p>
<p> See the <code><a href="/docs/linux/man/7-cmake-qt/">cmake-qt</a>(7)</code> manual for more information on using CMake with Qt.</p>
<h3>Binary_Dir</h3>
<p>New in version 3.4. </p>
<p> This read-only property reports the value of the <code>CMAKE_CURRENT_BINARY_DIR</code> variable in the directory in which the target was defined.</p>
<h3>Build_Rpath</h3>
<p>New in version 3.8. </p>
<p> A semicolon-separated list specifying runtime path (<code>RPATH</code>) entries to add to binaries linked in the build tree (for platforms that support it). The entries will <i>not</i> be used for binaries in the install tree. See also the <code>INSTALL_RPATH</code> target property. </p>
<p> This property is initialized by the value of the variable <code>CMAKE_BUILD_RPATH</code> if it is set when a target is created. </p>
<p> This property supports <code>generator expressions</code>.</p>
<h3>Build_Rpath_Use_Origin</h3>
<p>New in version 3.14. </p>
<p> Whether to use relative paths for the build <code>RPATH</code>. </p>
<p> This property is initialized by the value of the variable <code>CMAKE_BUILD_RPATH_USE_ORIGIN</code>. </p>
<p> On platforms that support runtime paths (<code>RPATH</code>) with the <code>$ORIGIN</code> token, setting this property to <code>TRUE</code> enables relative paths in the build <code>RPATH</code> for executables and shared libraries that point to shared libraries in the same build tree. </p>
<p> Normally the build <code>RPATH</code> of a binary contains absolute paths to the directory of each shared library it links to. The <code>RPATH</code> entries for directories contained within the build tree can be made relative to enable relocatable builds and to help achieve reproducible builds by omitting the build directory from the build environment. </p>
<p> This property has no effect on platforms that do not support the <code>$ORIGIN</code> token in <code>RPATH</code>, or when the <code>CMAKE_SKIP_RPATH</code> variable is set. The runtime path set through the <code>BUILD_RPATH</code> target property is also unaffected by this property.</p>
<h3>Build_With_Install_Name_Dir</h3>
<p>New in version 3.9. </p>
<p> <code>BUILD_WITH_INSTALL_NAME_DIR</code> is a boolean specifying whether the macOS <code>install_name</code> of a target in the build tree uses the directory given by <code>INSTALL_NAME_DIR</code>. This setting only applies to targets on macOS. </p>
<p> This property is initialized by the value of the variable <code>CMAKE_BUILD_WITH_INSTALL_NAME_DIR</code> if it is set when a target is created. </p>
<p> If this property is not set and policy <code>CMP0068</code> is not <code>NEW</code>, the value of <code>BUILD_WITH_INSTALL_RPATH</code> is used in its place.</p>
<h3>Build_With_Install_Rpath</h3>
<p><code>BUILD_WITH_INSTALL_RPATH</code> is a boolean specifying whether to link the target in the build tree with the <code>INSTALL_RPATH</code>. This takes precedence over <code>SKIP_BUILD_RPATH</code> and avoids the need for relinking before installation. </p>
<p> This property is initialized by the value of the <code>CMAKE_BUILD_WITH_INSTALL_RPATH</code> variable if it is set when a target is created. </p>
<p> If policy <code>CMP0068</code> is not <code>NEW</code>, this property also controls use of <code>INSTALL_NAME_DIR</code> in the build tree on macOS. Either way, the <code>BUILD_WITH_INSTALL_NAME_DIR</code> target property takes precedence.</p>
<h3>Bundle</h3>
<p>This target is a <code>CFBundle</code> on the macOS. </p>
<p> If a module library target has this property set to true it will be built as a <code>CFBundle</code> when built on the mac. It will have the directory structure required for a <code>CFBundle</code> and will be suitable to be used for creating Browser Plugins or other application resources.</p>
<h3>Bundle_Extension</h3>
<p>The file extension used to name a <code>BUNDLE</code>, a <code>FRAMEWORK</code>, or a <code>MACOSX_BUNDLE</code> target on the macOS and iOS. </p>
<p> The default value is <code>bundle</code>, <code>framework</code>, or <code>app</code> for the respective target types.</p>
<h3>C_Extensions</h3>
<p>New in version 3.1. </p>
<p> Boolean specifying whether compiler specific extensions are requested. </p>
<p> This property specifies whether compiler specific extensions should be used. For some compilers, this results in adding a flag such as <code>-std=gnu11</code> instead of <code>-std=c11</code> to the compile line. This property is <code>ON</code> by default. The basic C standard level is controlled by the <code>C_STANDARD</code> target property. </p>
<p> See the <code><a href="/docs/linux/man/7-cmake-compile-features/">cmake-compile-features</a>(7)</code> manual for information on compile features and a list of supported compilers. </p>
<p> This property is initialized by the value of the <code>CMAKE_C_EXTENSIONS</code> variable if set when a target is created and otherwise by the value of <code>CMAKE_C_EXTENSIONS_DEFAULT</code> (see <code>CMP0128</code>).</p>
<h3>C_Standard</h3>
<p>New in version 3.1. </p>
<p> The C standard whose features are requested to build this target. </p>
<p> This property specifies the C standard whose features are requested to build this target. For some compilers, this results in adding a flag such as <code>-std=gnu11</code> to the compile line. For compilers that have no notion of a C standard level, such as Microsoft Visual C++ before VS 16.7, this property has no effect. </p>
<p> Supported values are:</p>
<dl>
<dt><code>90</code></p>
<dd>C89/C90</p>
<dt><code>99</code></p>
<dd>C99</p>
<dt><code>11</code></p>
<dd>C11</p>
<dt><code>17</code></p>
<dd>New in version 3.21. </p>
<p> C17</p>
<dt><code>23</code></p>
<dd>New in version 3.21. </p>
<p> C23 </p>
<p> If the value requested does not result in a compile flag being added for the compiler in use, a previous standard flag will be added instead. This means that using: </p>
<pre>set_property(TARGET tgt PROPERTY C_STANDARD 11)</pre>
<p>with a compiler which does not support <code>-std=gnu11</code> or an equivalent flag will not result in an error or warning, but will instead add the <code>-std=gnu99</code> or <code>-std=gnu90</code> flag if supported. This &quot;decay&quot; behavior may be controlled with the <code>C_STANDARD_REQUIRED</code> target property. Additionally, the <code>C_EXTENSIONS</code> target property may be used to control whether compiler-specific extensions are enabled on a per-target basis. </p>
<p> See the <code><a href="/docs/linux/man/7-cmake-compile-features/">cmake-compile-features</a>(7)</code> manual for information on compile features and a list of supported compilers. </p>
<p> This property is initialized by the value of the <code>CMAKE_C_STANDARD</code> variable if it is set when a target is created.</dl>
<h3>C_Standard_Required</h3>
<p>New in version 3.1. </p>
<p> Boolean describing whether the value of <code>C_STANDARD</code> is a requirement. </p>
<p> If this property is set to <code>ON</code>, then the value of the <code>C_STANDARD</code> target property is treated as a requirement. If this property is <code>OFF</code> or unset, the <code>C_STANDARD</code> target property is treated as optional and may &quot;decay&quot; to a previous standard if the requested is not available. For compilers that have no notion of a C standard level, such as Microsoft Visual C++ before VS 16.7, this property has no effect. </p>
<p> See the <code><a href="/docs/linux/man/7-cmake-compile-features/">cmake-compile-features</a>(7)</code> manual for information on compile features and a list of supported compilers. </p>
<p> This property is initialized by the value of the <code>CMAKE_C_STANDARD_REQUIRED</code> variable if it is set when a target is created.</p>
<h3>Common_Language_Runtime</h3>
<p>New in version 3.12. </p>
<p> By setting this target property, the target is configured to build with <code>C++/CLI</code> support. </p>
<p> The Visual Studio generator defines the <code>clr</code> parameter depending on the value of <code>COMMON_LANGUAGE_RUNTIME</code>:</p>
<dl>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd>property not set: native C++ (i.e. default)</p>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd>property set but empty: mixed unmanaged/managed C++</p>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd>property set to any non empty value: managed C++ </p>
<p> Supported values: <code>&quot;&quot;</code>, <code>&quot;pure&quot;</code>, <code>&quot;safe&quot;</code> </p>
<p> This property is only evaluated Visual Studio Generators for VS 2010 and above. </p>
<p> To be able to build managed C++ targets with VS 2017 and above the component <code>C++/CLI support</code> must be installed, which may not be done by default. </p>
<p> See also <code>IMPORTED_COMMON_LANGUAGE_RUNTIME</code></dl>
<h3>Compatible_Interface_Bool</h3>
<p>Properties which must be compatible with their link interface </p>
<p> The <code>COMPATIBLE_INTERFACE_BOOL</code> property may contain a list of properties for this target which must be consistent when evaluated as a boolean with the <code>INTERFACE</code> variant of the property in all linked dependees. For example, if a property <code>FOO</code> appears in the list, then for each dependee, the <code>INTERFACE_FOO</code> property content in all of its dependencies must be consistent with each other, and with the <code>FOO</code> property in the depender. </p>
<p> Consistency in this sense has the meaning that if the property is set, then it must have the same boolean value as all others, and if the property is not set, then it is ignored. </p>
<p> Note that for each dependee, the set of properties specified in this property must not intersect with the set specified in any of the other Compatible Interface Properties.</p>
<h3>Compatible_Interface_Number_Max</h3>
<p>Properties whose maximum value from the link interface will be used. </p>
<p> The <code>COMPATIBLE_INTERFACE_NUMBER_MAX</code> property may contain a list of properties for this target whose maximum value may be read at generate time when evaluated in the <code>INTERFACE</code> variant of the property in all linked dependees. For example, if a property <code>FOO</code> appears in the list, then for each dependee, the <code>INTERFACE_FOO</code> property content in all of its dependencies will be compared with each other and with the <code>FOO</code> property in the depender. When reading the <code>FOO</code> property at generate time, the maximum value will be returned. If the property is not set, then it is ignored. </p>
<p> Note that for each dependee, the set of properties specified in this property must not intersect with the set specified in any of the other Compatible Interface Properties.</p>
<h3>Compatible_Interface_Number_Min</h3>
<p>Properties whose maximum value from the link interface will be used. </p>
<p> The <code>COMPATIBLE_INTERFACE_NUMBER_MIN</code> property may contain a list of properties for this target whose minimum value may be read at generate time when evaluated in the <code>INTERFACE</code> variant of the property of all linked dependees. For example, if a property <code>FOO</code> appears in the list, then for each dependee, the <code>INTERFACE_FOO</code> property content in all of its dependencies will be compared with each other and with the <code>FOO</code> property in the depender. When reading the <code>FOO</code> property at generate time, the minimum value will be returned. If the property is not set, then it is ignored. </p>
<p> Note that for each dependee, the set of properties specified in this property must not intersect with the set specified in any of the other Compatible Interface Properties.</p>
<h3>Compatible_Interface_String</h3>
<p>Properties which must be string-compatible with their link interface </p>
<p> The <code>COMPATIBLE_INTERFACE_STRING</code> property may contain a list of properties for this target which must be the same when evaluated as a string in the <code>INTERFACE</code> variant of the property all linked dependees. For example, if a property <code>FOO</code> appears in the list, then for each dependee, the <code>INTERFACE_FOO</code> property content in all of its dependencies must be equal with each other, and with the <code>FOO</code> property in the depender. If the property is not set, then it is ignored. </p>
<p> Note that for each dependee, the set of properties specified in this property must not intersect with the set specified in any of the other Compatible Interface Properties.</p>
<h3>Compile_Definitions</h3>
<p>Preprocessor definitions for compiling a target&#8217;s sources. </p>
<p> The <code>COMPILE_DEFINITIONS</code> property may be set to a semicolon-separated list of preprocessor definitions using the syntax <code>VAR</code> or <code>VAR=value</code>. Function-style definitions are not supported. CMake will automatically escape the value correctly for the native build system (note that CMake language syntax may require escapes to specify some values). </p>
<p> CMake will automatically drop some definitions that are not supported by the native build tool. </p>
<p> Disclaimer: Most native build tools have poor support for escaping certain values. CMake has work-arounds for many cases but some values may just not be possible to pass correctly. If a value does not seem to be escaped correctly, do not attempt to work-around the problem by adding escape sequences to the value. Your work-around may break in a future version of CMake that has improved escape support. Instead consider defining the macro in a (configured) header file. Then report the limitation. Known limitations include: </p>
<pre># - broken almost everywhere
; - broken in VS IDE 7.0 and Borland Makefiles
, - broken in VS IDE
% - broken in some cases in NMake
&amp; | - broken in some cases on MinGW
^ &lt; &gt; \&quot; - broken in most Make tools on Windows</pre>
<p>CMake does not reject these values outright because they do work in some cases. Use with caution. </p>
<p> Contents of <code>COMPILE_DEFINITIONS</code> may use &quot;generator expressions&quot; with the syntax <code>$&lt;&#8230;&gt;</code>. See the <code><a href="/docs/linux/man/7-cmake-generator-expressions/">cmake-generator-expressions</a>(7)</code> manual for available expressions. See the <code><a href="/docs/linux/man/7-cmake-buildsystem/">cmake-buildsystem</a>(7)</code> manual for more on defining buildsystem properties. </p>
<p> The corresponding <code>COMPILE_DEFINITIONS_&lt;CONFIG&gt;</code> property may be set to specify per-configuration definitions. Generator expressions should be preferred instead of setting the alternative property.</p>
<h3>Compile_Features</h3>
<p>New in version 3.1. </p>
<p> Compiler features enabled for this target. </p>
<p> The list of features in this property are a subset of the features listed in the <code>CMAKE_C_COMPILE_FEATURES</code>, <code>CMAKE_CUDA_COMPILE_FEATURES</code>, and <code>CMAKE_CXX_COMPILE_FEATURES</code> variables. </p>
<p> Contents of <code>COMPILE_FEATURES</code> may use &quot;generator expressions&quot; with the syntax <code>$&lt;&#8230;&gt;</code>. See the <code><a href="/docs/linux/man/7-cmake-generator-expressions/">cmake-generator-expressions</a>(7)</code> manual for available expressions. See the <code><a href="/docs/linux/man/7-cmake-compile-features/">cmake-compile-features</a>(7)</code> manual for information on compile features and a list of supported compilers.</p>
<h3>Compile_Flags</h3>
<p>Additional flags to use when compiling this target&#8217;s sources. </p>
<p> The <code>COMPILE_FLAGS</code> property sets additional compiler flags used to build sources within the target. Use <code>COMPILE_DEFINITIONS</code> to pass additional preprocessor definitions. </p>
<p> This property is deprecated. Use the <code>COMPILE_OPTIONS</code> property or the <code>target_compile_options()</code> command instead.</p>
<h3>Compile_Options</h3>
<p>List of options to pass to the compiler. </p>
<p> This property holds a semicolon-separated list of options specified so far for its target. Use the <code>target_compile_options()</code> command to append more options. The options will be added after after flags in the <code>CMAKE_&lt;LANG&gt;_FLAGS</code> and <code>CMAKE_&lt;LANG&gt;_FLAGS_&lt;CONFIG&gt;</code> variables, but before those propagated from dependencies by the <code>INTERFACE_COMPILE_OPTIONS</code> property. </p>
<p> This property is initialized by the <code>COMPILE_OPTIONS</code> directory property when a target is created, and is used by the generators to set the options for the compiler. </p>
<p> Contents of <code>COMPILE_OPTIONS</code> may use &quot;generator expressions&quot; with the syntax <code>$&lt;&#8230;&gt;</code>. See the <code><a href="/docs/linux/man/7-cmake-generator-expressions/">cmake-generator-expressions</a>(7)</code> manual for available expressions. See the <code><a href="/docs/linux/man/7-cmake-buildsystem/">cmake-buildsystem</a>(7)</code> manual for more on defining buildsystem properties.</p>
<h3>Option De-duplication</h3>
<p>The final set of options used for a target is constructed by accumulating options from the current target and the usage requirements of its dependencies. The set of options is de-duplicated to avoid repetition. </p>
<p> New in version 3.12: While beneficial for individual options, the de-duplication step can break up option groups. For example, <code>-option A -option B</code> becomes <code>-option A B</code>. One may specify a group of options using shell-like quoting along with a <code>SHELL:</code> prefix. The <code>SHELL:</code> prefix is dropped, and the rest of the option string is parsed using the <code>separate_arguments()</code> <code>UNIX_COMMAND</code> mode. For example, <code>&quot;SHELL:-option A&quot; &quot;SHELL:-option B&quot;</code> becomes <code>-option A -option B</code>.</p>
<h3>Compile_Pdb_Name</h3>
<p>New in version 3.1. </p>
<p> Output name for the MS debug symbol <code>.pdb</code> file generated by the compiler while building source files. </p>
<p> This property specifies the base name for the debug symbols file. If not set, the default is unspecified. </p>
<p> <code>NOTE:</code> The compiler-generated program database files are specified by the <code>/Fd</code> compiler flag and are not the same as linker-generated program database files specified by the <code>/pdb</code> linker flag. Use the <code>PDB_NAME</code> property to specify the latter.</p>
<h3>COMPILE_PDB_NAME_&lt;CONFIG&gt;</h3>
<p>New in version 3.1. </p>
<p> Per-configuration output name for the MS debug symbol <code>.pdb</code> file generated by the compiler while building source files. </p>
<p> This is the configuration-specific version of <code>COMPILE_PDB_NAME</code>. </p>
<p> <code>NOTE:</code> The compiler-generated program database files are specified by the <code>/Fd</code> compiler flag and are not the same as linker-generated program database files specified by the <code>/pdb</code> linker flag. Use the <code>PDB_NAME_&lt;CONFIG&gt;</code> property to specify the latter.</p>
<h3>Compile_Pdb_Output_Directory</h3>
<p>New in version 3.1. </p>
<p> Output directory for the MS debug symbol <code>.pdb</code> file generated by the compiler while building source files. </p>
<p> This property specifies the directory into which the MS debug symbols will be placed by the compiler. This property is initialized by the value of the <code>CMAKE_COMPILE_PDB_OUTPUT_DIRECTORY</code> variable if it is set when a target is created. </p>
<p> <code>NOTE:</code> The compiler-generated program database files are specified by the <code>/Fd</code> compiler flag and are not the same as linker-generated program database files specified by the <code>/pdb</code> linker flag. Use the <code>PDB_OUTPUT_DIRECTORY</code> property to specify the latter.</p>
<h3>COMPILE_PDB_OUTPUT_DIRECTORY_&lt;CONFIG&gt;</h3>
<p>New in version 3.1. </p>
<p> Per-configuration output directory for the MS debug symbol <code>.pdb</code> file generated by the compiler while building source files. </p>
<p> This is a per-configuration version of <code>COMPILE_PDB_OUTPUT_DIRECTORY</code>, but multi-configuration generators (Visual Studio, Xcode) do NOT append a per-configuration subdirectory to the specified directory. This property is initialized by the value of the <code>CMAKE_COMPILE_PDB_OUTPUT_DIRECTORY_&lt;CONFIG&gt;</code> variable if it is set when a target is created. </p>
<p> <code>NOTE:</code> The compiler-generated program database files are specified by the <code>/Fd</code> compiler flag and are not the same as linker-generated program database files specified by the <code>/pdb</code> linker flag. Use the <code>PDB_OUTPUT_DIRECTORY_&lt;CONFIG&gt;</code> property to specify the latter.</p>
<h3>&lt;CONFIG&gt;_OUTPUT_NAME</h3>
<p>Old per-configuration target file base name. Use <code>OUTPUT_NAME_&lt;CONFIG&gt;</code> instead. </p>
<p> This is a configuration-specific version of the <code>OUTPUT_NAME</code> target property.</p>
<h3>&lt;CONFIG&gt;_POSTFIX</h3>
<p>Postfix to append to the target file name for configuration &lt;CONFIG&gt;. </p>
<p> When building with configuration &lt;CONFIG&gt; the value of this property is appended to the target file name built on disk. For non-executable targets, this property is initialized by the value of the variable CMAKE_&lt;CONFIG&gt;_POSTFIX if it is set when a target is created. This property is ignored on the Mac for Frameworks and App Bundles. </p>
<p> For macOS see also the <code>FRAMEWORK_MULTI_CONFIG_POSTFIX_&lt;CONFIG&gt;</code> target property.</p>
<h3>Crosscompiling_Emulator</h3>
<p>New in version 3.3. </p>
<p> Use the given emulator to run executables created when crosscompiling. This command will be added as a prefix to <code>add_test()</code>, <code>add_custom_command()</code>, and <code>add_custom_target()</code> commands for built target system executables. </p>
<p> If this property contains a semicolon-separated list, then the first value is the command and remaining values are its arguments. </p>
<p> This property is initialized by the value of the <code>CMAKE_CROSSCOMPILING_EMULATOR</code> variable if it is set when a target is created.</p>
<h3>Cuda_Architectures</h3>
<p>New in version 3.18. </p>
<p> List of architectures to generate device code for. </p>
<p> An architecture can be suffixed by either <code>-real</code> or <code>-virtual</code> to specify the kind of architecture to generate code for. If no suffix is given then code is generated for both real and virtual architectures. </p>
<p> A non-empty false value (e.g. <code>OFF</code>) disables adding architectures. This is intended to support packagers and rare cases where full control over the passed flags is required. </p>
<p> This property is initialized by the value of the <code>CMAKE_CUDA_ARCHITECTURES</code> variable if it is set when a target is created. </p>
<p> The <code>CUDA_ARCHITECTURES</code> target property must be set to a non-empty value on targets that compile CUDA sources, or it is an error. See policy <code>CMP0104</code>.</p>
<h3>Examples</h3>
<pre>set_target_properties(tgt PROPERTIES CUDA_ARCHITECTURES &quot;35;50;72&quot;)</pre>
<p>Generates code for real and virtual architectures <code>30</code>, <code>50</code> and <code>72</code>. </p>
<pre>set_property(TARGET tgt PROPERTY CUDA_ARCHITECTURES 70-real 72-virtual)</pre>
<p>Generates code for real architecture <code>70</code> and virtual architecture <code>72</code>. </p>
<pre>set_property(TARGET tgt PROPERTY CUDA_ARCHITECTURES OFF)</pre>
<p>CMake will not pass any architecture flags to the compiler.</p>
<h3>Cuda_Extensions</h3>
<p>New in version 3.8. </p>
<p> Boolean specifying whether compiler specific extensions are requested. </p>
<p> This property specifies whether compiler specific extensions should be used. For some compilers, this results in adding a flag such as <code>-std=gnu++11</code> instead of <code>-std=c++11</code> to the compile line. This property is <code>ON</code> by default. The basic CUDA/C++ standard level is controlled by the <code>CUDA_STANDARD</code> target property. </p>
<p> See the <code><a href="/docs/linux/man/7-cmake-compile-features/">cmake-compile-features</a>(7)</code> manual for information on compile features and a list of supported compilers. </p>
<p> This property is initialized by the value of the <code>CMAKE_CUDA_EXTENSIONS</code> variable if set when a target is created and otherwise by the value of <code>CMAKE_CUDA_EXTENSIONS_DEFAULT</code> (see <code>CMP0128</code>).</p>
<h3>Cuda_Ptx_Compilation</h3>
<p>New in version 3.9. </p>
<p> Compile CUDA sources to <code>.ptx</code> files instead of <code>.obj</code> files within Object Libraries. </p>
<p> For example: </p>
<pre>add_library(myptx OBJECT a.cu b.cu)
set_property(TARGET myptx PROPERTY CUDA_PTX_COMPILATION ON)</pre>
<h3>Cuda_Resolve_Device_Symbols</h3>
<p>New in version 3.9. </p>
<p> CUDA only: Enables device linking for the specific library target where required. </p>
<p> If set, this will tell the required compilers to enable device linking on the library target. Device linking is an additional link step required by some CUDA compilers when <code>CUDA_SEPARABLE_COMPILATION</code> is enabled. Normally device linking is deferred until a shared library or executable is generated, allowing for multiple static libraries to resolve device symbols at the same time when they are used by a shared library or executable. </p>
<p> By default static library targets have this property is disabled, while shared, module, and executable targets have this property enabled. </p>
<p> Note that device linking is not supported for Object Libraries. </p>
<p> For instance: </p>
<pre>set_property(TARGET mystaticlib PROPERTY CUDA_RESOLVE_DEVICE_SYMBOLS ON)</pre>
<h3>Cuda_Runtime_Library</h3>
<p>New in version 3.17. </p>
<p> Select the CUDA runtime library for use by compilers targeting the CUDA language. </p>
<p> The allowed case insensitive values are:</p>
<dl>
<dt><code>None</code></p>
<dd>Link with <code>-cudart=none</code> or equivalent flag(s) to use no CUDA runtime library.</p>
<dt><code>Shared</code></p>
<dd>Link with <code>-cudart=shared</code> or equivalent flag(s) to use a dynamically-linked CUDA runtime library.</p>
<dt><code>Static</code></p>
<dd>Link with <code>-cudart=static</code> or equivalent flag(s) to use a statically-linked CUDA runtime library. </p>
<p> Contents of <code>CUDA_RUNTIME_LIBRARY</code> may use <code>generator expressions</code>. </p>
<p> If that property is not set then CMake uses an appropriate default value based on the compiler to select the CUDA runtime library. </p>
<p> <code>NOTE:</code> This property has effect only when the <code>CUDA</code> language is enabled. To control the CUDA runtime linking when only using the CUDA SDK with the <code>C</code> or <code>C++</code> language we recommend using the <code>FindCUDAToolkit</code> module.</dl>
<h3>Cuda_Separable_Compilation</h3>
<p>New in version 3.8. </p>
<p> CUDA only: Enables separate compilation of device code </p>
<p> If set this will enable separable compilation for all CUDA files for the given target. </p>
<p> For instance: </p>
<pre>set_property(TARGET myexe PROPERTY CUDA_SEPARABLE_COMPILATION ON)</pre>
<p>This property is initialized by the value of the <code>CMAKE_CUDA_SEPARABLE_COMPILATION</code> variable if it is set when a target is created.</p>
<h3>Cuda_Standard</h3>
<p>New in version 3.8. </p>
<p> The CUDA/C++ standard whose features are requested to build this target. </p>
<p> This property specifies the CUDA/C++ standard whose features are requested to build this target. For some compilers, this results in adding a flag such as <code>-std=gnu++11</code> to the compile line. </p>
<p> Supported values are:</p>
<dl>
<dt><code>98</code></p>
<dd>CUDA C++98. Note that this maps to the same as <code>03</code> internally.</p>
<dt><code>03</code></p>
<dd>CUDA C++03</p>
<dt><code>11</code></p>
<dd>CUDA C++11</p>
<dt><code>14</code></p>
<dd>CUDA C++14. While CMake 3.8 and later <i>recognize</i> <code>14</code> as a valid value, CMake 3.9 was the first version to include support for any compiler.</p>
<dt><code>17</code></p>
<dd>CUDA C++17. While CMake 3.8 and later <i>recognize</i> <code>17</code> as a valid value, CMake 3.18 was the first version to include support for any compiler.</p>
<dt><code>20</code></p>
<dd>New in version 3.12. </p>
<p> CUDA C++20. While CMake 3.12 and later <i>recognize</i> <code>20</code> as a valid value, CMake 3.18 was the first version to include support for any compiler.</p>
<dt><code>23</code></p>
<dd>New in version 3.20. </p>
<p> CUDA C++23 </p>
<p> If the value requested does not result in a compile flag being added for the compiler in use, a previous standard flag will be added instead. This means that using: </p>
<pre>set_property(TARGET tgt PROPERTY CUDA_STANDARD 11)</pre>
<p>with a compiler which does not support <code>-std=gnu++11</code> or an equivalent flag will not result in an error or warning, but will instead add the <code>-std=gnu++03</code> flag if supported. This &quot;decay&quot; behavior may be controlled with the <code>CUDA_STANDARD_REQUIRED</code> target property. Additionally, the <code>CUDA_EXTENSIONS</code> target property may be used to control whether compiler-specific extensions are enabled on a per-target basis. </p>
<p> See the <code><a href="/docs/linux/man/7-cmake-compile-features/">cmake-compile-features</a>(7)</code> manual for information on compile features and a list of supported compilers. </p>
<p> This property is initialized by the value of the <code>CMAKE_CUDA_STANDARD</code> variable if it is set when a target is created.</dl>
<h3>Cuda_Standard_Required</h3>
<p>New in version 3.8. </p>
<p> Boolean describing whether the value of <code>CUDA_STANDARD</code> is a requirement. </p>
<p> If this property is set to <code>ON</code>, then the value of the <code>CUDA_STANDARD</code> target property is treated as a requirement. If this property is <code>OFF</code> or unset, the <code>CUDA_STANDARD</code> target property is treated as optional and may &quot;decay&quot; to a previous standard if the requested is not available. For compilers that have no notion of a standard level, such as MSVC 1800 (Visual Studio 2013) and lower, this has no effect. </p>
<p> See the <code><a href="/docs/linux/man/7-cmake-compile-features/">cmake-compile-features</a>(7)</code> manual for information on compile features and a list of supported compilers. </p>
<p> This property is initialized by the value of the <code>CMAKE_CUDA_STANDARD_REQUIRED</code> variable if it is set when a target is created.</p>
<h3>Cxx_Extensions</h3>
<p>New in version 3.1. </p>
<p> Boolean specifying whether compiler specific extensions are requested. </p>
<p> This property specifies whether compiler specific extensions should be used. For some compilers, this results in adding a flag such as <code>-std=gnu++11</code> instead of <code>-std=c++11</code> to the compile line. This property is <code>ON</code> by default. The basic C++ standard level is controlled by the <code>CXX_STANDARD</code> target property. </p>
<p> See the <code><a href="/docs/linux/man/7-cmake-compile-features/">cmake-compile-features</a>(7)</code> manual for information on compile features and a list of supported compilers. </p>
<p> This property is initialized by the value of the <code>CMAKE_CXX_EXTENSIONS</code> variable if set when a target is created and otherwise by the value of <code>CMAKE_CXX_EXTENSIONS_DEFAULT</code> (see <code>CMP0128</code>).</p>
<h3>Cxx_Standard</h3>
<p>New in version 3.1. </p>
<p> The C++ standard whose features are requested to build this target. </p>
<p> This property specifies the C++ standard whose features are requested to build this target. For some compilers, this results in adding a flag such as <code>-std=gnu++11</code> to the compile line. For compilers that have no notion of a standard level, such as Microsoft Visual C++ before 2015 Update 3, this has no effect. </p>
<p> Supported values are:</p>
<dl>
<dt><code>98</code></p>
<dd>C++98</p>
<dt><code>11</code></p>
<dd>C++11</p>
<dt><code>14</code></p>
<dd>C++14</p>
<dt><code>17</code></p>
<dd>New in version 3.8. </p>
<p> C++17</p>
<dt><code>20</code></p>
<dd>New in version 3.12. </p>
<p> C++20</p>
<dt><code>23</code></p>
<dd>New in version 3.20. </p>
<p> C++23 </p>
<p> If the value requested does not result in a compile flag being added for the compiler in use, a previous standard flag will be added instead. This means that using: </p>
<pre>set_property(TARGET tgt PROPERTY CXX_STANDARD 11)</pre>
<p>with a compiler which does not support <code>-std=gnu++11</code> or an equivalent flag will not result in an error or warning, but will instead add the <code>-std=gnu++98</code> flag if supported. This &quot;decay&quot; behavior may be controlled with the <code>CXX_STANDARD_REQUIRED</code> target property. Additionally, the <code>CXX_EXTENSIONS</code> target property may be used to control whether compiler-specific extensions are enabled on a per-target basis. </p>
<p> See the <code><a href="/docs/linux/man/7-cmake-compile-features/">cmake-compile-features</a>(7)</code> manual for information on compile features and a list of supported compilers. </p>
<p> This property is initialized by the value of the <code>CMAKE_CXX_STANDARD</code> variable if it is set when a target is created.</dl>
<h3>Cxx_Standard_Required</h3>
<p>New in version 3.1. </p>
<p> Boolean describing whether the value of <code>CXX_STANDARD</code> is a requirement. </p>
<p> If this property is set to <code>ON</code>, then the value of the <code>CXX_STANDARD</code> target property is treated as a requirement. If this property is <code>OFF</code> or unset, the <code>CXX_STANDARD</code> target property is treated as optional and may &quot;decay&quot; to a previous standard if the requested is not available. For compilers that have no notion of a standard level, such as MSVC 1800 (Visual Studio 2013) and lower, this has no effect. </p>
<p> See the <code><a href="/docs/linux/man/7-cmake-compile-features/">cmake-compile-features</a>(7)</code> manual for information on compile features and a list of supported compilers. </p>
<p> This property is initialized by the value of the <code>CMAKE_CXX_STANDARD_REQUIRED</code> variable if it is set when a target is created.</p>
<h3>Debug_Postfix</h3>
<p>See target property <code>&lt;CONFIG&gt;_POSTFIX</code>. </p>
<p> This property is a special case of the more-general <code>&lt;CONFIG&gt;_POSTFIX</code> property for the <code>DEBUG</code> configuration.</p>
<h3>Define_Symbol</h3>
<p>Define a symbol when compiling this target&#8217;s sources. </p>
<p> <code>DEFINE_SYMBOL</code> sets the name of the preprocessor symbol defined when compiling sources in a shared library. If not set here then it is set to <code>target_EXPORTS</code> by default (with some substitutions if the target is not a valid C identifier). This is useful for headers to know whether they are being included from inside their library or outside to properly setup dllexport/dllimport decorations.</p>
<h3>Deployment_Additional_Files</h3>
<p>New in version 3.13. </p>
<p> Set the WinCE project <code>AdditionalFiles</code> in <code>DeploymentTool</code> in <code>.vcproj</code> files generated by the <code>Visual Studio 9 2008</code> generator. This is useful when you want to debug on remote WinCE device. Specify additional files that will be copied to the device. For example: </p>
<pre>set_property(TARGET ${TARGET} PROPERTY
 DEPLOYMENT_ADDITIONAL_FILES &quot;english.lng|local_folder|remote_folder|0&quot;
 &quot;german.lng|local_folder|remote_folder|0&quot;)</pre>
<p>produces: </p>
<pre>&lt;DeploymentTool AdditionalFiles=&quot;english.lng|local_folder|remote_folder|0;german.lng|local_folder|remote_folder|0&quot; ... /&gt;</pre>
<h3>Deployment_Remote_Directory</h3>
<p>New in version 3.6. </p>
<p> Set the WinCE project <code>RemoteDirectory</code> in <code>DeploymentTool</code> and <code>RemoteExecutable</code> in <code>DebuggerTool</code> in <code>.vcproj</code> files generated by the <code>Visual Studio 9 2008</code> generator. This is useful when you want to debug on remote WinCE device. For example: </p>
<pre>set_property(TARGET ${TARGET} PROPERTY
 DEPLOYMENT_REMOTE_DIRECTORY &quot;\FlashStorage&quot;)</pre>
<p>produces: </p>
<pre>&lt;DeploymentTool RemoteDirectory=&quot;\FlashStorage&quot; ... /&gt;
&lt;DebuggerTool RemoteExecutable=&quot;\FlashStorage	arget_file&quot; ... /&gt;</pre>
<h3>Deprecation</h3>
<p>New in version 3.17. </p>
<p> Deprecation message from imported target&#8217;s developer. </p>
<p> <code>DEPRECATION</code> is the message regarding a deprecation status to be displayed to downstream users of a target.</p>
<h3>Disable_Precompile_Headers</h3>
<p>New in version 3.16. </p>
<p> Disables the precompilation of header files specified by <code>PRECOMPILE_HEADERS</code> property. </p>
<p> If the property is not set, CMake will use the value provided by <code>CMAKE_DISABLE_PRECOMPILE_HEADERS</code>.</p>
<h3>Dotnet_Target_Framework</h3>
<p>New in version 3.17. </p>
<p> Specify the .NET target framework. </p>
<p> Used to specify the .NET target framework for C++/CLI and C#. For example: <code>netcoreapp2.1</code>. </p>
<p> This property is only evaluated for Visual Studio Generators VS 2010 and above. </p>
<p> Can be initialized for all targets using the variable <code>CMAKE_DOTNET_TARGET_FRAMEWORK</code>.</p>
<h3>Dotnet_Target_Framework_Version</h3>
<p>New in version 3.12. </p>
<p> Specify the .NET target framework version. </p>
<p> Used to specify the .NET target framework version for C++/CLI and C#. For example: <code>v4.5</code>. </p>
<p> This property is only evaluated for Visual Studio Generators VS 2010 and above. </p>
<p> To initialize this variable for all targets set <code>CMAKE_DOTNET_TARGET_FRAMEWORK</code> or <code>CMAKE_DOTNET_TARGET_FRAMEWORK_VERSION</code>. If both are set, the latter is ignored.</p>
<h3>EchoString</h3>
<p>A message to be displayed when the target is built. </p>
<p> A message to display on some generators (such as Makefile Generators) when the target is built.</p>
<h3>Enable_Exports</h3>
<p>Specify whether an executable exports symbols for loadable modules. </p>
<p> Normally an executable does not export any symbols because it is the final program. It is possible for an executable to export symbols to be used by loadable modules. When this property is set to true CMake will allow other targets to &quot;link&quot; to the executable with the <code>target_link_libraries()</code> command. On all platforms a target-level dependency on the executable is created for targets that link to it. Handling of the executable on the link lines of the loadable modules varies by platform:</p>
<dl>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd>On Windows-based systems (including Cygwin) an &quot;import library&quot; is created along with the executable to list the exported symbols. Loadable modules link to the import library to get the symbols.</p>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd>On macOS, loadable modules link to the executable itself using the <code>-bundle_loader</code> flag.</p>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd>On AIX, a linker &quot;import file&quot; is created along with the executable to list the exported symbols for import when linking other targets. Loadable modules link to the import file to get the symbols.</p>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd>On other platforms, loadable modules are simply linked without referencing the executable since the dynamic loader will automatically bind symbols when the module is loaded. </p>
<p> This property is initialized by the value of the variable <code>CMAKE_ENABLE_EXPORTS</code> if it is set when a target is created.</dl>
<h3>Exclude_From_All</h3>
<p>Set this target property to a true (or false) value to exclude (or include) the target from the &quot;all&quot; target of the containing directory and its ancestors. If excluded, running e.g. <code>make</code> in the containing directory or its ancestors will not build the target by default. </p>
<p> If this target property is not set then the target will be included in the &quot;all&quot; target of the containing directory. Furthermore, it will be included in the &quot;all&quot; target of its ancestor directories unless the <code>EXCLUDE_FROM_ALL</code> directory property is set. </p>
<p> With <code>EXCLUDE_FROM_ALL</code> set to false or not set at all, the target will be brought up to date as part of doing a <code>make install</code> or its equivalent for the CMake generator being used. </p>
<p> If a target has <code>EXCLUDE_FROM_ALL</code> set to true, it may still be listed in an <code>install(TARGETS)</code> command, but the user is responsible for ensuring that the target&#8217;s build artifacts are not missing or outdated when an install is performed. </p>
<p> This property may use &quot;generator expressions&quot; with the syntax <code>$&lt;&#8230;&gt;</code>. See the <code><a href="/docs/linux/man/7-cmake-generator-expressions/">cmake-generator-expressions</a>(7)</code> manual for available expressions. </p>
<p> Only the &quot;Ninja Multi-Config&quot; generator supports a property value that varies by configuration. For all other generators the value of this property must be the same for all configurations.</p>
<h3>Exclude_From_Default_Build</h3>
<p>Exclude target from <code>Build Solution</code>. </p>
<p> This property is only used by Visual Studio generators. When set to <code>TRUE</code>, the target will not be built when you press <code>Build Solution</code>.</p>
<h3>EXCLUDE_FROM_DEFAULT_BUILD_&lt;CONFIG&gt;</h3>
<p>Per-configuration version of target exclusion from <code>Build Solution</code>. </p>
<p> This is the configuration-specific version of <code>EXCLUDE_FROM_DEFAULT_BUILD</code>. If the generic <code>EXCLUDE_FROM_DEFAULT_BUILD</code> is also set on a target, <code>EXCLUDE_FROM_DEFAULT_BUILD_&lt;CONFIG&gt;</code> takes precedence in configurations for which it has a value.</p>
<h3>Export_Compile_Commands</h3>
<p>New in version 3.20. </p>
<p> Enable/Disable output of compile commands during generation for a target. </p>
<p> This property is initialized by the value of the variable <code>CMAKE_EXPORT_COMPILE_COMMANDS</code> if it is set when a target is created.</p>
<h3>Export_Name</h3>
<p>Exported name for target files. </p>
<p> This sets the name for the <code>IMPORTED</code> target generated by the <code>install(EXPORT)</code> and <code>export()</code> commands. If not set, the logical target name is used by default.</p>
<h3>Export_Properties</h3>
<p>New in version 3.12. </p>
<p> List additional properties to export for a target. </p>
<p> This property contains a list of property names that should be exported by the <code>install(EXPORT)</code> and <code>export()</code> commands. By default only a limited number of properties are exported. This property can be used to additionally export other properties as well. </p>
<p> Properties starting with <code>INTERFACE_</code> or <code>IMPORTED_</code> are not allowed as they are reserved for internal CMake use. </p>
<p> Properties containing generator expressions are also not allowed. </p>
<p> <code>NOTE:</code> Since CMake 3.19, Interface Libraries may have arbitrary target properties. If a project exports an interface library with custom properties, the resulting package may not work with dependents configured by older versions of CMake that reject the custom properties.</p>
<h3>Folder</h3>
<p>Set the folder name. Use to organize targets in an IDE. </p>
<p> Targets with no <code>FOLDER</code> property will appear as top level entities in IDEs like Visual Studio. Targets with the same <code>FOLDER</code> property value will appear next to each other in a folder of that name. To nest folders, use <code>FOLDER</code> values such as &#8216;GUI/Dialogs&#8217; with &#8216;/&#8217; characters separating folder levels. </p>
<p> This property is initialized by the value of the variable <code>CMAKE_FOLDER</code> if it is set when a target is created.</p>
<h3>Fortran_BUILDING_INSTRINSIC_MODULES</h3>
<p>New in version 3.22. </p>
<p> Instructs the CMake Fortran preprocessor that the target is building Fortran intrinsics for building a Fortran compiler. </p>
<p> This property is off by default and should be turned only on projects that build a Fortran compiler. It should not be turned on for projects that use a Fortran compiler. </p>
<p> Turning this property on will correctly add dependencies for building Fortran intrinsic modules whereas turning the property off will ignore Fortran intrinsic modules in the dependency graph as they are supplied by the compiler itself.</p>
<h3>Fortran_FORMAT</h3>
<p>Set to <code>FIXED</code> or <code>FREE</code> to indicate the Fortran source layout. </p>
<p> This property tells CMake whether the Fortran source files in a target use fixed-format or free-format. CMake will pass the corresponding format flag to the compiler. Use the source-specific <code>Fortran_FORMAT</code> property to change the format of a specific source file. If the variable <code>CMAKE_Fortran_FORMAT</code> is set when a target is created its value is used to initialize this property.</p>
<h3>Fortran_MODULE_DIRECTORY</h3>
<p>Specify output directory for Fortran modules provided by the target. </p>
<p> If the target contains Fortran source files that provide modules and the compiler supports a module output directory this specifies the directory in which the modules will be placed. When this property is not set the modules will be placed in the build directory corresponding to the target&#8217;s source directory. If the variable <code>CMAKE_Fortran_MODULE_DIRECTORY</code> is set when a target is created its value is used to initialize this property. </p>
<p> When using one of the Visual Studio Generators with the Intel Fortran plugin installed in Visual Studio, a subdirectory named after the configuration will be appended to the path where modules are created. For example, if <code>Fortran_MODULE_DIRECTORY</code> is set to <code>C:/some/path</code>, modules will end up in <code>C:/some/path/Debug</code> (or <code>C:/some/path/Release</code> etc.) when an Intel Fortran <code>.vfproj</code> file is generated, and in <code>C:/some/path</code> when any other generator is used. </p>
<p> Note that some compilers will automatically search the module output directory for modules USEd during compilation but others will not. If your sources USE modules their location must be specified by <code>INCLUDE_DIRECTORIES</code> regardless of this property.</p>
<h3>Fortran_PREPROCESS</h3>
<p>New in version 3.18. </p>
<p> Control whether the Fortran source file should be unconditionally preprocessed. </p>
<p> If unset or empty, rely on the compiler to determine whether the file should be preprocessed. If explicitly set to <code>OFF</code> then the file does not need to be preprocessed. If explicitly set to <code>ON</code>, then the file does need to be preprocessed as part of the compilation step. </p>
<p> When using the <code>Ninja</code> generator, all source files are first preprocessed in order to generate module dependency information. Setting this property to <code>OFF</code> will make <code>Ninja</code> skip this step. </p>
<p> Use the source-specific <code>Fortran_PREPROCESS</code> property if a single file needs to be preprocessed. If the variable <code>CMAKE_Fortran_PREPROCESS</code> is set when a target is created its value is used to initialize this property. </p>
<p> <code>NOTE:</code> For some compilers, <code>NAG</code>, <code>PGI</code> and <code>Solaris Studio</code>, setting this to <code>OFF</code> will have no effect.</p>
<h3>Framework</h3>
<p>Build <code>SHARED</code> or <code>STATIC</code> library as Framework Bundle on the macOS and iOS. </p>
<p> If such a library target has this property set to <code>TRUE</code> it will be built as a framework when built on the macOS and iOS. It will have the directory structure required for a framework and will be suitable to be used with the <code>-framework</code> option. This property is initialized by the value of the <code>CMAKE_FRAMEWORK</code> variable if it is set when a target is created. </p>
<p> To customize <code>Info.plist</code> file in the framework, use <code>MACOSX_FRAMEWORK_INFO_PLIST</code> target property. </p>
<p> For macOS see also the <code>FRAMEWORK_VERSION</code> target property. </p>
<p> Example of creation <code>dynamicFramework</code>: </p>
<pre>add_library(dynamicFramework SHARED
 dynamicFramework.c
 dynamicFramework.h
)
set_target_properties(dynamicFramework PROPERTIES
 FRAMEWORK TRUE
 FRAMEWORK_VERSION C
 MACOSX_FRAMEWORK_IDENTIFIER com.cmake.dynamicFramework
 MACOSX_FRAMEWORK_INFO_PLIST Info.plist
 # &quot;current version&quot; in semantic format in Mach-O binary file
 VERSION 16.4.0
 # &quot;compatibility version&quot; in semantic format in Mach-O binary file
 SOVERSION 1.0.0
 PUBLIC_HEADER dynamicFramework.h
 XCODE_ATTRIBUTE_CODE_SIGN_IDENTITY &quot;iPhone Developer&quot;
)</pre>
<h3>FRAMEWORK_MULTI_CONFIG_POSTFIX_&lt;CONFIG&gt;</h3>
<p>New in version 3.18. </p>
<p> Postfix to append to the framework file name for configuration <code>&lt;CONFIG&gt;</code>, when using a multi-config generator (like Xcode and Ninja Multi-Config). </p>
<p> When building with configuration <code>&lt;CONFIG&gt;</code> the value of this property is appended to the framework file name built on disk. </p>
<p> For example, given a framework called <code>my_fw</code>, a value of <code>_debug</code> for the <code>FRAMEWORK_MULTI_CONFIG_POSTFIX_DEBUG</code> property, and <code>Debug;Release</code> in <code>CMAKE_CONFIGURATION_TYPES</code>, the following relevant files would be created for the <code>Debug</code> and <code>Release</code> configurations:</p>
<dl>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd><code>Release/my_fw.framework/my_fw</code></p>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd><code>Release/my_fw.framework/Versions/A/my_fw</code></p>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd><code>Debug/my_fw.framework/my_fw_debug</code></p>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd><code>Debug/my_fw.framework/Versions/A/my_fw_debug</code> </p>
<p> For framework targets, this property is initialized by the value of the <code>CMAKE_FRAMEWORK_MULTI_CONFIG_POSTFIX_&lt;CONFIG&gt;</code> variable if it is set when a target is created. </p>
<p> This property is ignored for non-framework targets, and when using single config generators.</dl>
<h3>Framework_Version</h3>
<p>New in version 3.4. </p>
<p> Version of a framework created using the <code>FRAMEWORK</code> target property (e.g. <code>A</code>). </p>
<p> This property only affects macOS, as iOS doesn&#8217;t have versioned directory structure.</p>
<h3>Generator_File_Name</h3>
<p>Generator&#8217;s file for this target. </p>
<p> An internal property used by some generators to record the name of the project or dsp file associated with this target. Note that at configure time, this property is only set for targets created by <code>include_external_msproject()</code>.</p>
<h3>Ghs_Integrity_App</h3>
<p>New in version 3.14. </p>
<p> <code>ON</code> / <code>OFF</code> boolean to determine if an executable target should be treated as an <i>Integrity Application</i>. </p>
<p> If no value is set and if a <code>.int</code> file is added as a source file to the executable target it will be treated as an <i>Integrity Application</i>. </p>
<p> Supported on <code>Green Hills MULTI</code>.</p>
<h3>Ghs_No_Source_Group_File</h3>
<p>New in version 3.14. </p>
<p> <code>ON</code> / <code>OFF</code> boolean to control if the project file for a target should be one single file or multiple files. </p>
<p> The default behavior or when the property is <code>OFF</code> is to generate a project file for the target and then a sub-project file for each source group. </p>
<p> When this property is <code>ON</code> or if <code>CMAKE_GHS_NO_SOURCE_GROUP_FILE</code> is <code>ON</code> then only a single project file is generated for the target. </p>
<p> Supported on <code>Green Hills MULTI</code>.</p>
<h3>GNUtoMS</h3>
<p>Convert GNU import library (<code>.dll.a</code>) to MS format (<code>.lib</code>). </p>
<p> When linking a shared library or executable that exports symbols using GNU tools on Windows (MinGW/MSYS) with Visual Studio installed convert the import library (<code>.dll.a</code>) from GNU to MS format (<code>.lib</code>). Both import libraries will be installed by <code>install(TARGETS)</code> and exported by <code>install(EXPORT)</code> and <code>export()</code> to be linked by applications with either GNU- or MS-compatible tools. </p>
<p> If the variable <code>CMAKE_GNUtoMS</code> is set when a target is created its value is used to initialize this property. The variable must be set prior to the first command that enables a language such as <code>project()</code> or <code>enable_language()</code>. CMake provides the variable as an option to the user automatically when configuring on Windows with GNU tools.</p>
<h3>Has_Cxx</h3>
<p>Link the target using the C++ linker tool (obsolete). </p>
<p> This is equivalent to setting the <code>LINKER_LANGUAGE</code> property to <code>CXX</code>.</p>
<h3>Hip_Architectures</h3>
<p>New in version 3.21. </p>
<p> List of AMD GPU architectures to generate device code for. </p>
<p> A non-empty false value (e.g. <code>OFF</code>) disables adding architectures. This is intended to support packagers and rare cases where full control over the passed flags is required. </p>
<p> This property is initialized by the value of the <code>CMAKE_HIP_ARCHITECTURES</code> variable if it is set when a target is created. </p>
<p> The HIP compilation model has two modes: whole and separable. Whole compilation generates device code at compile time. Separable compilation generates device code at link time. Therefore the <code>HIP_ARCHITECTURES</code> target property should be set on targets that compile or link with any HIP sources.</p>
<h3>Examples</h3>
<pre>set_property(TARGET tgt PROPERTY HIP_ARCHITECTURES gfx801 gfx900)</pre>
<p>Generates code for both <code>gfx801</code> and <code>gfx900</code>.</p>
<h3>Hip_Extensions</h3>
<p>New in version 3.21. </p>
<p> Boolean specifying whether compiler specific extensions are requested. </p>
<p> This property specifies whether compiler specific extensions should be used. For some compilers, this results in adding a flag such as <code>-std=gnu++11</code> instead of <code>-std=c++11</code> to the compile line. This property is <code>ON</code> by default. The basic HIP/C++ standard level is controlled by the <code>HIP_STANDARD</code> target property. </p>
<p> See the <code><a href="/docs/linux/man/7-cmake-compile-features/">cmake-compile-features</a>(7)</code> manual for information on compile features and a list of supported compilers. </p>
<p> This property is initialized by the value of the <code>CMAKE_HIP_EXTENSIONS</code> variable if set when a target is created and otherwise by the value of <code>CMAKE_HIP_EXTENSIONS_DEFAULT</code> (see <code>CMP0128</code>).</p>
<h3>Hip_Standard</h3>
<p>New in version 3.21. </p>
<p> The HIP/C++ standard requested to build this target. </p>
<p> Supported values are:</p>
<dl>
<dt><code>98</code></p>
<dd>HIP C++98</p>
<dt><code>11</code></p>
<dd>HIP C++11</p>
<dt><code>14</code></p>
<dd>HIP C++14</p>
<dt><code>17</code></p>
<dd>HIP C++17</p>
<dt><code>20</code></p>
<dd>HIP C++20</p>
<dt><code>23</code></p>
<dd>HIP C++23 </p>
<p> If the value requested does not result in a compile flag being added for the compiler in use, a previous standard flag will be added instead. This means that using: </p>
<pre>set_property(TARGET tgt PROPERTY HIP_STANDARD 11)</pre>
<p>with a compiler which does not support <code>-std=gnu++11</code> or an equivalent flag will not result in an error or warning, but will instead add the <code>-std=gnu++98</code> flag if supported. This &quot;decay&quot; behavior may be controlled with the <code>HIP_STANDARD_REQUIRED</code> target property. Additionally, the <code>HIP_EXTENSIONS</code> target property may be used to control whether compiler-specific extensions are enabled on a per-target basis. </p>
<p> See the <code><a href="/docs/linux/man/7-cmake-compile-features/">cmake-compile-features</a>(7)</code> manual for information on compile features and a list of supported compilers. </p>
<p> This property is initialized by the value of the <code>CMAKE_HIP_STANDARD</code> variable if it is set when a target is created.</dl>
<h3>Hip_Standard_Required</h3>
<p>New in version 3.21. </p>
<p> Boolean describing whether the value of <code>HIP_STANDARD</code> is a requirement. </p>
<p> If this property is set to <code>ON</code>, then the value of the <code>HIP_STANDARD</code> target property is treated as a requirement. If this property is <code>OFF</code> or unset, the <code>HIP_STANDARD</code> target property is treated as optional and may &quot;decay&quot; to a previous standard if the requested is not available. </p>
<p> See the <code><a href="/docs/linux/man/7-cmake-compile-features/">cmake-compile-features</a>(7)</code> manual for information on compile features and a list of supported compilers. </p>
<p> This property is initialized by the value of the <code>CMAKE_HIP_STANDARD_REQUIRED</code> variable if it is set when a target is created.</p>
<h3>Implicit_Depends_Include_Transform</h3>
<p>Specify <code>#include</code> line transforms for dependencies in a target. </p>
<p> This property specifies rules to transform macro-like <code>#include</code> lines during implicit dependency scanning of C and C++ source files. The list of rules must be semicolon-separated with each entry of the form <code>A_MACRO(%)=value-with-%</code> (the <code>%</code> must be literal). During dependency scanning occurrences of <code>A_MACRO(&#8230;)</code> on <code>#include</code> lines will be replaced by the value given with the macro argument substituted for <code>%</code>. For example, the entry </p>
<pre>MYDIR(%)=&lt;mydir/%&gt;</pre>
<p>will convert lines of the form </p>
<pre>#include MYDIR(myheader.h)</pre>
<p>to </p>
<pre>#include &lt;mydir/myheader.h&gt;</pre>
<p>allowing the dependency to be followed. </p>
<p> This property applies to sources in the target on which it is set.</p>
<h3>Imported</h3>
<p>Read-only indication of whether a target is <code>IMPORTED</code>. </p>
<p> The boolean value of this property is <code>True</code> for targets created with the <code>IMPORTED</code> option to <code>add_executable()</code> or <code>add_library()</code>. It is <code>False</code> for targets built within the project.</p>
<h3>Imported_Common_Language_Runtime</h3>
<p>New in version 3.12. </p>
<p> Property to define if the target uses <code>C++/CLI</code>. </p>
<p> Ignored for non-imported targets. </p>
<p> See also the <code>COMMON_LANGUAGE_RUNTIME</code> target property.</p>
<h3>Imported_Configurations</h3>
<p>Configurations provided for an IMPORTED target. </p>
<p> Set this to the list of configuration names available for an IMPORTED target. The names correspond to configurations defined in the project from which the target is imported. If the importing project uses a different set of configurations the names may be mapped using the MAP_IMPORTED_CONFIG_&lt;CONFIG&gt; property. Ignored for non-imported targets.</p>
<h3>Imported_Global</h3>
<p>New in version 3.11. </p>
<p> Indication of whether an IMPORTED target is globally visible. </p>
<p> The boolean value of this property is True for targets created with the <code>IMPORTED</code> <code>GLOBAL</code> options to <code>add_executable()</code> or <code>add_library()</code>. It is always False for targets built within the project. </p>
<p> For targets created with the <code>IMPORTED</code> option to <code>add_executable()</code> or <code>add_library()</code> but without the additional option <code>GLOBAL</code> this is False, too. However, setting this property for such a locally <code>IMPORTED</code> target to True promotes that target to global scope. This promotion can only be done in the same directory where that <code>IMPORTED</code> target was created in the first place. </p>
<p> <code>NOTE:</code> Once an imported target has been made global, it cannot be changed back to non-global. Therefore, if a project sets this property, it may only provide a value of True. CMake will issue an error if the project tries to set the property to a non-True value, even if the value was already False. </p>
<p> <code>NOTE:</code> Local ALIAS targets created before promoting an IMPORTED target from <code>LOCAL</code> to <code>GLOBAL</code>, keep their initial scope (see <code>ALIAS_GLOBAL</code> target property).</p>
<h3>Imported_Implib</h3>
<p>Full path to the import library for an <code>IMPORTED</code> target. </p>
<p> Set this to the location of the <code>.lib</code> part of a Windows DLL, or on AIX set it to an import file created for executables that export symbols (see the <code>ENABLE_EXPORTS</code> target property). Ignored for non-imported targets.</p>
<h3>IMPORTED_IMPLIB_&lt;CONFIG&gt;</h3>
<p>&lt;CONFIG&gt;-specific version of <code>IMPORTED_IMPLIB</code> property. </p>
<p> Configuration names correspond to those provided by the project from which the target is imported.</p>
<h3>Imported_Libname</h3>
<p>New in version 3.8. </p>
<p> Specify the link library name for an imported Interface Library. </p>
<p> An interface library builds no library file itself but does specify usage requirements for its consumers. The <code>IMPORTED_LIBNAME</code> property may be set to specify a single library name to be placed on the link line in place of the interface library target name as a requirement for using the interface. </p>
<p> This property is intended for use in naming libraries provided by a platform SDK for which the full path to a library file may not be known. The value may be a plain library name such as <code>foo</code> but may <i>not</i> be a path (e.g. <code>/usr/lib/libfoo.so</code>) or a flag (e.g. <code>-Wl,&#8230;</code>). The name is never treated as a library target name even if it happens to name one. </p>
<p> The <code>IMPORTED_LIBNAME</code> property is allowed only on imported Interface Libraries and is rejected on targets of other types (for which the <code>IMPORTED_LOCATION</code> target property may be used).</p>
<h3>IMPORTED_LIBNAME_&lt;CONFIG&gt;</h3>
<p>New in version 3.8. </p>
<p> &lt;CONFIG&gt;-specific version of <code>IMPORTED_LIBNAME</code> property. </p>
<p> Configuration names correspond to those provided by the project from which the target is imported.</p>
<h3>Imported_Link_Dependent_Libraries</h3>
<p>Dependent shared libraries of an imported shared library. </p>
<p> Shared libraries may be linked to other shared libraries as part of their implementation. On some platforms the linker searches for the dependent libraries of shared libraries they are including in the link. Set this property to the list of dependent shared libraries of an imported library. The list should be disjoint from the list of interface libraries in the <code>INTERFACE_LINK_LIBRARIES</code> property. On platforms requiring dependent shared libraries to be found at link time CMake uses this list to add appropriate files or paths to the link command line. Ignored for non-imported targets.</p>
<h3>IMPORTED_LINK_DEPENDENT_LIBRARIES_&lt;CONFIG&gt;</h3>
<p>&lt;CONFIG&gt;-specific version of <code>IMPORTED_LINK_DEPENDENT_LIBRARIES</code>. </p>
