<p> Configuration names correspond to those provided by the project from which the target is imported. If set, this property completely overrides the generic property for the named configuration.</p>
<h3>Imported_Link_Interface_Languages</h3>
<p>Languages compiled into an <code>IMPORTED</code> static library. </p>
<p> Set this to the list of languages of source files compiled to produce a <code>STATIC IMPORTED</code> library (such as <code>C</code> or <code>CXX</code>). CMake accounts for these languages when computing how to link a target to the imported library. For example, when a C executable links to an imported C++ static library CMake chooses the C++ linker to satisfy language runtime dependencies of the static library. </p>
<p> This property is ignored for targets that are not <code>STATIC</code> libraries. This property is ignored for non-imported targets.</p>
<h3>IMPORTED_LINK_INTERFACE_LANGUAGES_&lt;CONFIG&gt;</h3>
<p>&lt;CONFIG&gt;-specific version of <code>IMPORTED_LINK_INTERFACE_LANGUAGES</code>. </p>
<p> Configuration names correspond to those provided by the project from which the target is imported. If set, this property completely overrides the generic property for the named configuration.</p>
<h3>Imported_Link_Interface_Libraries</h3>
<p>Transitive link interface of an <code>IMPORTED</code> target. </p>
<p> Set this to the list of libraries whose interface is included when an <code>IMPORTED</code> library target is linked to another target. The libraries will be included on the link line for the target. Unlike the <code>LINK_INTERFACE_LIBRARIES</code> property, this property applies to all imported target types, including <code>STATIC</code> libraries. This property is ignored for non-imported targets. </p>
<p> This property is ignored if the target also has a non-empty <code>INTERFACE_LINK_LIBRARIES</code> property. </p>
<p> This property is deprecated. Use <code>INTERFACE_LINK_LIBRARIES</code> instead.</p>
<h3>IMPORTED_LINK_INTERFACE_LIBRARIES_&lt;CONFIG&gt;</h3>
<p>&lt;CONFIG&gt;-specific version of <code>IMPORTED_LINK_INTERFACE_LIBRARIES</code>. </p>
<p> Configuration names correspond to those provided by the project from which the target is imported. If set, this property completely overrides the generic property for the named configuration. </p>
<p> This property is ignored if the target also has a non-empty <code>INTERFACE_LINK_LIBRARIES</code> property. </p>
<p> This property is deprecated. Use <code>INTERFACE_LINK_LIBRARIES</code> instead.</p>
<h3>Imported_Link_Interface_Multiplicity</h3>
<p>Repetition count for cycles of <code>IMPORTED</code> static libraries. </p>
<p> This is <code>LINK_INTERFACE_MULTIPLICITY</code> for <code>IMPORTED</code> targets.</p>
<h3>IMPORTED_LINK_INTERFACE_MULTIPLICITY_&lt;CONFIG&gt;</h3>
<p>&lt;CONFIG&gt;-specific version of <code>IMPORTED_LINK_INTERFACE_MULTIPLICITY</code>. </p>
<p> If set, this property completely overrides the generic property for the named configuration.</p>
<h3>Imported_Location</h3>
<p>Full path to the main file on disk for an <code>IMPORTED</code> target. </p>
<p> Set this to the location of an <code>IMPORTED</code> target file on disk. For executables this is the location of the executable file. For <code>STATIC</code> libraries and modules this is the location of the library or module. For <code>SHARED</code> libraries on non-DLL platforms this is the location of the shared library. For application bundles on macOS this is the location of the executable file inside <code>Contents/MacOS</code> within the bundle folder. For frameworks on macOS this is the location of the library file symlink just inside the framework folder. For DLLs this is the location of the <code>.dll</code> part of the library. For <code>UNKNOWN</code> libraries this is the location of the file to be linked. Ignored for non-imported targets. </p>
<p> The <code>IMPORTED_LOCATION</code> target property may be overridden for a given configuration <code>&lt;CONFIG&gt;</code> by the configuration-specific <code>IMPORTED_LOCATION_&lt;CONFIG&gt;</code> target property. Furthermore, the <code>MAP_IMPORTED_CONFIG_&lt;CONFIG&gt;</code> target property may be used to map between a project&#8217;s configurations and those of an imported target. If none of these is set then the name of any other configuration listed in the <code>IMPORTED_CONFIGURATIONS</code> target property may be selected and its <code>IMPORTED_LOCATION_&lt;CONFIG&gt;</code> value used. </p>
<p> To get the location of an imported target read one of the <code>LOCATION</code> or <code>LOCATION_&lt;CONFIG&gt;</code> properties. </p>
<p> For platforms with import libraries (e.g. Windows) see also <code>IMPORTED_IMPLIB</code>.</p>
<h3>IMPORTED_LOCATION_&lt;CONFIG&gt;</h3>
<p>&lt;CONFIG&gt;-specific version of <code>IMPORTED_LOCATION</code> property. </p>
<p> Configuration names correspond to those provided by the project from which the target is imported.</p>
<h3>Imported_No_Soname</h3>
<p>Specifies that an <code>IMPORTED</code> shared library target has no <code>soname</code>. </p>
<p> Set this property to true for an imported shared library file that has no <code>soname</code> field. CMake may adjust generated link commands for some platforms to prevent the linker from using the path to the library in place of its missing <code>soname</code>. Ignored for non-imported targets.</p>
<h3>IMPORTED_NO_SONAME_&lt;CONFIG&gt;</h3>
<p>&lt;CONFIG&gt;-specific version of <code>IMPORTED_NO_SONAME</code> property. </p>
<p> Configuration names correspond to those provided by the project from which the target is imported.</p>
<h3>Imported_Objects</h3>
<p>New in version 3.9. </p>
<p> A semicolon-separated list of absolute paths to the object files on disk for an imported object library. </p>
<p> Ignored for non-imported targets. </p>
<p> Projects may skip <code>IMPORTED_OBJECTS</code> if the configuration-specific property <code>IMPORTED_OBJECTS_&lt;CONFIG&gt;</code> is set instead, except in situations as noted in the section below.</p>
<h3>Xcode Generator Considerations</h3>
<p>New in version 3.20. </p>
<p> For Apple platforms, a project may be built for more than one architecture. This is controlled by the <code>CMAKE_OSX_ARCHITECTURES</code> variable. For all but the <code>Xcode</code> generator, CMake invokes compilers once per source file and passes multiple <code>-arch</code> flags, leading to a single object file which will be a universal binary. Such object files work well when listed in the <code>IMPORTED_OBJECTS</code> of a separate CMake build, even for the <code>Xcode</code> generator. But producing such object files with the <code>Xcode</code> generator is more difficult, since it invokes the compiler once per architecture for each source file. Unlike the other generators, it does not generate universal object file binaries. </p>
<p> A further complication with the <code>Xcode</code> generator is that when targeting device platforms (iOS, tvOS or watchOS), the <code>Xcode</code> generator has the ability to use either the device or simulator SDK without needing CMake to be re-run. The SDK can be selected at build time. But since some architectures can be supported by both the device and the simulator SDKs (e.g. <code>arm64</code> with Xcode 12 or later), not all combinations can be represented in a single universal binary. The only solution in this case is to have multiple object files. </p>
<p> <code>IMPORTED_OBJECTS</code> doesn&#8217;t support generator expressions, so every file it lists needs to be valid for every architecture and SDK. If incorporating object files that are not universal binaries, the path and/or file name of each object file has to somehow encapsulate the different architectures and SDKs. With the <code>Xcode</code> generator, Xcode variables of the form <code>$(&#8230;)</code> can be used to represent these aspects and Xcode will substitute the appropriate values at build time. CMake doesn&#8217;t interpret these variables and embeds them unchanged in the Xcode project file. <code>$(CURRENT_ARCH)</code> can be used to represent the architecture, while <code>$(EFFECTIVE_PLATFORM_NAME)</code> can be used to differentiate between SDKs. </p>
<p> The following shows one example of how these two variables can be used to refer to an object file whose location depends on both the SDK and the architecture: </p>
<pre>add_library(someObjs OBJECT IMPORTED)
set_property(TARGET someObjs PROPERTY IMPORTED_OBJECTS
 # Quotes are required because of the ()
 &quot;/path/to/somewhere/objects$(EFFECTIVE_PLATFORM_NAME)/$(CURRENT_ARCH)/func.o&quot;
)
# Example paths:
# /path/to/somewhere/objects-iphoneos/arm64/func.o
# /path/to/somewhere/objects-iphonesimulator/x86_64/func.o</pre>
<p>In some cases, you may want to have configuration-specific object files as well. The <code>$(CONFIGURATION)</code> Xcode variable is often used for this and can be used in conjunction with the others mentioned above: </p>
<pre>add_library(someObjs OBJECT IMPORTED)
set_property(TARGET someObjs PROPERTY IMPORTED_OBJECTS
 &quot;/path/to/somewhere/$(CONFIGURATION)$(EFFECTIVE_PLATFORM_NAME)/$(CURRENT_ARCH)/func.o&quot;
)
# Example paths:
# /path/to/somewhere/Release-iphoneos/arm64/func.o
# /path/to/somewhere/Debug-iphonesimulator/x86_64/func.o</pre>
<p>When any Xcode variable is used, CMake is not able to fully evaluate the path(s) at configure time. One consequence of this is that the configuration-specific <code>IMPORTED_OBJECTS_&lt;CONFIG&gt;</code> properties cannot be used, since CMake cannot determine whether an object file exists at a particular <code>&lt;CONFIG&gt;</code> location. The <code>IMPORTED_OBJECTS</code> property must be used for these situations and the configuration-specific aspects of the path should be handled by the <code>$(CONFIGURATION)</code> Xcode variable.</p>
<h3>IMPORTED_OBJECTS_&lt;CONFIG&gt;</h3>
<p>New in version 3.9. </p>
<p> <code>&lt;CONFIG&gt;</code>-specific version of <code>IMPORTED_OBJECTS</code> property. </p>
<p> Configuration names correspond to those provided by the project from which the target is imported.</p>
<h3>Xcode Generator Considerations</h3>
<p>Do not use this <code>&lt;CONFIG&gt;</code>-specific property if you need to use Xcode variables like <code>$(CURRENT_ARCH)</code> or <code>$(EFFECTIVE_PLATFORM_NAME)</code> in the value. The <code>&lt;CONFIG&gt;</code>-specific properties will be ignored in such cases because CMake cannot determine whether a file exists at the configuration-specific path at configuration time. For such cases, use <code>IMPORTED_OBJECTS</code> instead.</p>
<h3>Imported_Soname</h3>
<p>The <code>soname</code> of an <code>IMPORTED</code> target of shared library type. </p>
<p> Set this to the <code>soname</code> embedded in an imported shared library. This is meaningful only on platforms supporting the feature. Ignored for non-imported targets.</p>
<h3>IMPORTED_SONAME_&lt;CONFIG&gt;</h3>
<p>&lt;CONFIG&gt;-specific version of <code>IMPORTED_SONAME</code> property. </p>
<p> Configuration names correspond to those provided by the project from which the target is imported.</p>
<h3>Import_Prefix</h3>
<p>What comes before the import library name. </p>
<p> Similar to the target property <code>PREFIX</code>, but used for import libraries (typically corresponding to a <code>DLL</code>) instead of regular libraries. A target property that can be set to override the prefix (such as <code>lib</code>) on an import library name.</p>
<h3>Import_Suffix</h3>
<p>What comes after the import library name. </p>
<p> Similar to the target property <code>SUFFIX</code>, but used for import libraries (typically corresponding to a <code>DLL</code>) instead of regular libraries. A target property that can be set to override the suffix (such as <code>.lib</code>) on an import library name.</p>
<h3>Include_Directories</h3>
<p>List of preprocessor include file search directories. </p>
<p> This property specifies the list of directories given so far to the <code>target_include_directories()</code> command. In addition to accepting values from that command, values may be set directly on any target using the <code>set_property()</code> command. A target gets its initial value for this property from the value of the <code>INCLUDE_DIRECTORIES</code> directory property. Both directory and target property values are adjusted by calls to the <code>include_directories()</code> command. </p>
<p> The value of this property is used by the generators to set the include paths for the compiler. </p>
<p> Relative paths should not be added to this property directly. Use one of the commands above instead to handle relative paths. </p>
<p> Contents of <code>INCLUDE_DIRECTORIES</code> may use <code><a href="/docs/linux/man/7-cmake-generator-expressions/">cmake-generator-expressions</a>(7)</code> with the syntax <code>$&lt;&#8230;&gt;</code>. See the <code><a href="/docs/linux/man/7-cmake-generator-expressions/">cmake-generator-expressions</a>(7)</code> manual for available expressions. See the <code><a href="/docs/linux/man/7-cmake-buildsystem/">cmake-buildsystem</a>(7)</code> manual for more on defining buildsystem properties.</p>
<h3>Install_Name_Dir</h3>
<p>Directory name for installed targets on Apple platforms. </p>
<p> <code>INSTALL_NAME_DIR</code> is a string specifying the directory portion of the &quot;install_name&quot; field of shared libraries on Apple platforms for installed targets. When not set, the default directory used is determined by <code>MACOSX_RPATH</code>. Policies <code>CMP0068</code> and <code>CMP0042</code> are also relevant. </p>
<p> This property is initialized by the value of the variable <code>CMAKE_INSTALL_NAME_DIR</code> if it is set when a target is created. </p>
<p> This property supports <code>generator expressions</code>. In particular, the <code>$&lt;INSTALL_PREFIX&gt;</code> generator expression can be used to set the directory relative to the install-time prefix.</p>
<h3>Install_Remove_Environment_Rpath</h3>
<p>New in version 3.16. </p>
<p> Controls whether toolchain-defined rpaths should be removed during installation. </p>
<p> When a target is being installed, CMake may need to rewrite its rpath information. This occurs when the install rpath (as specified by the <code>INSTALL_RPATH</code> target property) has different contents to the rpath that the target was built with. Some toolchains insert their own rpath contents into the binary as part of the build. By default, CMake will preserve those extra inserted contents in the install rpath. For those scenarios where such toolchain-inserted entries need to be discarded during install, set the <code>INSTALL_REMOVE_ENVIRONMENT_RPATH</code> target property to true. </p>
<p> This property is initialized by the value of <code>CMAKE_INSTALL_REMOVE_ENVIRONMENT_RPATH</code> when the target is created.</p>
<h3>Install_Rpath</h3>
<p>The rpath to use for installed targets. </p>
<p> A semicolon-separated list specifying the rpath to use in installed targets (for platforms that support it). This property is initialized by the value of the variable <code>CMAKE_INSTALL_RPATH</code> if it is set when a target is created. </p>
<p> Because the rpath may contain <code>${ORIGIN}</code>, which coincides with CMake syntax, the contents of <code>INSTALL_RPATH</code> are properly escaped in the <code>cmake_install.cmake</code> script (see policy <code>CMP0095</code>.) </p>
<p> This property supports <code>generator expressions</code>.</p>
<h3>Install_Rpath_Use_Link_Path</h3>
<p>Add paths to linker search and installed rpath. </p>
<p> <code>INSTALL_RPATH_USE_LINK_PATH</code> is a boolean that if set to <code>True</code> will append to the runtime search path (rpath) of installed binaries any directories outside the project that are in the linker search path or contain linked library files. The directories are appended after the value of the <code>INSTALL_RPATH</code> target property. </p>
<p> This property is initialized by the value of the variable <code>CMAKE_INSTALL_RPATH_USE_LINK_PATH</code> if it is set when a target is created.</p>
<h3>Interface_Autouic_Options</h3>
<p>List of interface options to pass to uic. </p>
<p> Targets may populate this property to publish the options required to use when invoking <code>uic</code>. Consuming targets can add entries to their own <code>AUTOUIC_OPTIONS</code> property such as <code>$&lt;TARGET_PROPERTY:foo,INTERFACE_AUTOUIC_OPTIONS&gt;</code> to use the uic options specified in the interface of <code>foo</code>. This is done automatically by the <code>target_link_libraries()</code> command. </p>
<p> This property supports generator expressions. See the <code><a href="/docs/linux/man/7-cmake-generator-expressions/">cmake-generator-expressions</a>(7)</code> manual for available expressions.</p>
<h3>Interface_Compile_Definitions</h3>
<p>List of public compile definitions requirements for a library. </p>
<p> Targets may populate this property to publish the compile definitions required to compile against the headers for the target. The <code>target_compile_definitions()</code> command populates this property with values given to the <code>PUBLIC</code> and <code>INTERFACE</code> keywords. Projects may also get and set the property directly. </p>
<p> When target dependencies are specified using <code>target_link_libraries()</code>, CMake will read this property from all target dependencies to determine the build properties of the consumer. </p>
<p> Contents of <code>INTERFACE_COMPILE_DEFINITIONS</code> may use &quot;generator expressions&quot; with the syntax <code>$&lt;&#8230;&gt;</code>. See the <code><a href="/docs/linux/man/7-cmake-generator-expressions/">cmake-generator-expressions</a>(7)</code> manual for available expressions. See the <code><a href="/docs/linux/man/7-cmake-buildsystem/">cmake-buildsystem</a>(7)</code> -manual for more on defining buildsystem properties.</p>
<h3>Interface_Compile_Features</h3>
<p>New in version 3.1. </p>
<p> List of public compile features requirements for a library. </p>
<p> Targets may populate this property to publish the compile features required to compile against the headers for the target. The <code>target_compile_features()</code> command populates this property with values given to the <code>PUBLIC</code> and <code>INTERFACE</code> keywords. Projects may also get and set the property directly. </p>
<p> When target dependencies are specified using <code>target_link_libraries()</code>, CMake will read this property from all target dependencies to determine the build properties of the consumer. </p>
<p> Contents of <code>INTERFACE_COMPILE_FEATURES</code> may use &quot;generator expressions&quot; with the syntax <code>$&lt;&#8230;&gt;</code>. See the <code><a href="/docs/linux/man/7-cmake-generator-expressions/">cmake-generator-expressions</a>(7)</code> manual for available expressions. See the <code><a href="/docs/linux/man/7-cmake-buildsystem/">cmake-buildsystem</a>(7)</code> -manual for more on defining buildsystem properties. </p>
<p> See the <code><a href="/docs/linux/man/7-cmake-compile-features/">cmake-compile-features</a>(7)</code> manual for information on compile features and a list of supported compilers.</p>
<h3>Interface_Compile_Options</h3>
<p>List of public compile options requirements for a library. </p>
<p> Targets may populate this property to publish the compile options required to compile against the headers for the target. The <code>target_compile_options()</code> command populates this property with values given to the <code>PUBLIC</code> and <code>INTERFACE</code> keywords. Projects may also get and set the property directly. </p>
<p> When target dependencies are specified using <code>target_link_libraries()</code>, CMake will read this property from all target dependencies to determine the build properties of the consumer. </p>
<p> Contents of <code>INTERFACE_COMPILE_OPTIONS</code> may use &quot;generator expressions&quot; with the syntax <code>$&lt;&#8230;&gt;</code>. See the <code><a href="/docs/linux/man/7-cmake-generator-expressions/">cmake-generator-expressions</a>(7)</code> manual for available expressions. See the <code><a href="/docs/linux/man/7-cmake-buildsystem/">cmake-buildsystem</a>(7)</code> -manual for more on defining buildsystem properties.</p>
<h3>Interface_Include_Directories</h3>
<p>List of public include directories requirements for a library. </p>
<p> Targets may populate this property to publish the include directories required to compile against the headers for the target. The <code>target_include_directories()</code> command populates this property with values given to the <code>PUBLIC</code> and <code>INTERFACE</code> keywords. Projects may also get and set the property directly. </p>
<p> When target dependencies are specified using <code>target_link_libraries()</code>, CMake will read this property from all target dependencies to determine the build properties of the consumer. </p>
<p> Contents of <code>INTERFACE_INCLUDE_DIRECTORIES</code> may use &quot;generator expressions&quot; with the syntax <code>$&lt;&#8230;&gt;</code>. See the <code><a href="/docs/linux/man/7-cmake-generator-expressions/">cmake-generator-expressions</a>(7)</code> manual for available expressions. See the <code><a href="/docs/linux/man/7-cmake-buildsystem/">cmake-buildsystem</a>(7)</code> -manual for more on defining buildsystem properties. </p>
<p> Include directories usage requirements commonly differ between the build-tree and the install-tree. The <code>BUILD_INTERFACE</code> and <code>INSTALL_INTERFACE</code> generator expressions can be used to describe separate usage requirements based on the usage location. Relative paths are allowed within the <code>INSTALL_INTERFACE</code> expression and are interpreted relative to the installation prefix. For example: </p>
<pre>target_include_directories(mylib INTERFACE
 $&lt;BUILD_INTERFACE:${CMAKE_CURRENT_SOURCE_DIR}/include/mylib&gt;
 $&lt;INSTALL_INTERFACE:include/mylib&gt; # &lt;prefix&gt;/include/mylib
)</pre>
<h3>Creating Relocatable Packages</h3>
<p>Note that it is not advisable to populate the <code>INSTALL_INTERFACE</code> of the <code>INTERFACE_INCLUDE_DIRECTORIES</code> of a target with absolute paths to the include directories of dependencies. That would hard-code into installed packages the include directory paths for dependencies <code>as found on the machine the package was made on</code>. </p>
<p> The <code>INSTALL_INTERFACE</code> of the <code>INTERFACE_INCLUDE_DIRECTORIES</code> is only suitable for specifying the required include directories for headers provided with the target itself, not those provided by the transitive dependencies listed in its <code>INTERFACE_LINK_LIBRARIES</code> target property. Those dependencies should themselves be targets that specify their own header locations in <code>INTERFACE_INCLUDE_DIRECTORIES</code>. </p>
<p> See the Creating Relocatable Packages section of the <code><a href="/docs/linux/man/7-cmake-packages/">cmake-packages</a>(7)</code> manual for discussion of additional care that must be taken when specifying usage requirements while creating packages for redistribution.</p>
<h3>Interface_Link_Depends</h3>
<p>New in version 3.13. </p>
<p> Additional public interface files on which a target binary depends for linking. </p>
<p> This property is supported only by <code>Ninja</code> and Makefile Generators. It is intended to specify dependencies on &quot;linker scripts&quot; for custom Makefile link rules. </p>
<p> When target dependencies are specified using <code>target_link_libraries()</code>, CMake will read this property from all target dependencies to determine the build properties of the consumer. </p>
<p> Contents of <code>INTERFACE_LINK_DEPENDS</code> may use &quot;generator expressions&quot; with the syntax <code>$&lt;&#8230;&gt;</code>. See the <code><a href="/docs/linux/man/7-cmake-generator-expressions/">cmake-generator-expressions</a>(7)</code> manual for available expressions. See the <code><a href="/docs/linux/man/7-cmake-buildsystem/">cmake-buildsystem</a>(7)</code> -manual for more on defining buildsystem properties. </p>
<p> Link dependency files usage requirements commonly differ between the build-tree and the install-tree. The <code>BUILD_INTERFACE</code> and <code>INSTALL_INTERFACE</code> generator expressions can be used to describe separate usage requirements based on the usage location. Relative paths are allowed within the <code>INSTALL_INTERFACE</code> expression and are interpreted relative to the installation prefix. For example: </p>
<pre>set_property(TARGET mylib PROPERTY INTERFACE_LINK_DEPENDS
 $&lt;BUILD_INTERFACE:${CMAKE_CURRENT_SOURCE_DIR}/mylinkscript&gt;
 $&lt;INSTALL_INTERFACE:mylinkscript&gt; # &lt;prefix&gt;/mylinkscript
)</pre>
<h3>Interface_Link_Directories</h3>
<p>New in version 3.13. </p>
<p> List of public link directories requirements for a library. </p>
<p> Targets may populate this property to publish the link directories required to compile against the headers for the target. The <code>target_link_directories()</code> command populates this property with values given to the <code>PUBLIC</code> and <code>INTERFACE</code> keywords. Projects may also get and set the property directly. </p>
<p> When target dependencies are specified using <code>target_link_libraries()</code>, CMake will read this property from all target dependencies to determine the build properties of the consumer. </p>
<p> Contents of <code>INTERFACE_LINK_DIRECTORIES</code> may use &quot;generator expressions&quot; with the syntax <code>$&lt;&#8230;&gt;</code>. See the <code><a href="/docs/linux/man/7-cmake-generator-expressions/">cmake-generator-expressions</a>(7)</code> manual for available expressions. See the <code><a href="/docs/linux/man/7-cmake-buildsystem/">cmake-buildsystem</a>(7)</code> -manual for more on defining buildsystem properties.</p>
<h3>Interface_Link_Libraries</h3>
<p>List public interface libraries for a library. </p>
<p> This property contains the list of transitive link dependencies. When the target is linked into another target using the <code>target_link_libraries()</code> command, the libraries listed (and recursively their link interface libraries) will be provided to the other target also. This property is overridden by the <code>LINK_INTERFACE_LIBRARIES</code> or <code>LINK_INTERFACE_LIBRARIES_&lt;CONFIG&gt;</code> property if policy <code>CMP0022</code> is <code>OLD</code> or unset. </p>
<p> Contents of <code>INTERFACE_LINK_LIBRARIES</code> may use &quot;generator expressions&quot; with the syntax <code>$&lt;&#8230;&gt;</code>. See the <code><a href="/docs/linux/man/7-cmake-generator-expressions/">cmake-generator-expressions</a>(7)</code> manual for available expressions. See the <code><a href="/docs/linux/man/7-cmake-buildsystem/">cmake-buildsystem</a>(7)</code> manual for more on defining buildsystem properties. </p>
<p> <code>NOTE:</code> A call to <code>target_link_libraries(&lt;target&gt; &#8230;)</code> may update this property on <code>&lt;target&gt;</code>. If <code>&lt;target&gt;</code> was not created in the same directory as the call then <code>target_link_libraries()</code> will wrap each entry with the form <code>::@(directory-id);&#8230;;::@</code>, where the <code>::@</code> is literal and the <code>(directory-id)</code> is unspecified. This tells the generators that the named libraries must be looked up in the scope of the caller rather than in the scope in which the <code>&lt;target&gt;</code> was created. Valid directory ids are stripped on export by the <code>install(EXPORT)</code> and <code>export()</code> commands.</p>
<h3>Creating Relocatable Packages</h3>
<p>Note that it is not advisable to populate the <code>INTERFACE_LINK_LIBRARIES</code> of a target with absolute paths to dependencies. That would hard-code into installed packages the library file paths for dependencies <code>as found on the machine the package was made on</code>. </p>
<p> See the Creating Relocatable Packages section of the <code><a href="/docs/linux/man/7-cmake-packages/">cmake-packages</a>(7)</code> manual for discussion of additional care that must be taken when specifying usage requirements while creating packages for redistribution.</p>
<h3>Interface_Link_Options</h3>
<p>New in version 3.13. </p>
<p> List of public link options requirements for a library. </p>
<p> Targets may populate this property to publish the link options required to compile against the headers for the target. The <code>target_link_options()</code> command populates this property with values given to the <code>PUBLIC</code> and <code>INTERFACE</code> keywords. Projects may also get and set the property directly. </p>
<p> When target dependencies are specified using <code>target_link_libraries()</code>, CMake will read this property from all target dependencies to determine the build properties of the consumer. </p>
<p> Contents of <code>INTERFACE_LINK_OPTIONS</code> may use &quot;generator expressions&quot; with the syntax <code>$&lt;&#8230;&gt;</code>. See the <code><a href="/docs/linux/man/7-cmake-generator-expressions/">cmake-generator-expressions</a>(7)</code> manual for available expressions. See the <code><a href="/docs/linux/man/7-cmake-buildsystem/">cmake-buildsystem</a>(7)</code> -manual for more on defining buildsystem properties.</p>
<h3>Interface_Position_Independent_Code</h3>
<p>Whether consumers need to create a position-independent target </p>
<p> The <code>INTERFACE_POSITION_INDEPENDENT_CODE</code> property informs consumers of this target whether they must set their <code>POSITION_INDEPENDENT_CODE</code> property to <code>ON</code>. If this property is set to <code>ON</code>, then the <code>POSITION_INDEPENDENT_CODE</code> property on all consumers will be set to <code>ON</code>. Similarly, if this property is set to <code>OFF</code>, then the <code>POSITION_INDEPENDENT_CODE</code> property on all consumers will be set to <code>OFF</code>. If this property is undefined, then consumers will determine their <code>POSITION_INDEPENDENT_CODE</code> property by other means. Consumers must ensure that the targets that they link to have a consistent requirement for their <code>INTERFACE_POSITION_INDEPENDENT_CODE</code> property. </p>
<p> Contents of <code>INTERFACE_POSITION_INDEPENDENT_CODE</code> may use &quot;generator expressions&quot; with the syntax <code>$&lt;&#8230;&gt;</code>. See the <code><a href="/docs/linux/man/7-cmake-generator-expressions/">cmake-generator-expressions</a>(7)</code> manual for available expressions. See the <code><a href="/docs/linux/man/7-cmake-buildsystem/">cmake-buildsystem</a>(7)</code> manual for more on defining buildsystem properties.</p>
<h3>Interface_Precompile_Headers</h3>
<p>New in version 3.16. </p>
<p> List of interface header files to precompile into consuming targets. </p>
<p> Targets may populate this property to publish the header files for consuming targets to precompile. The <code>target_precompile_headers()</code> command populates this property with values given to the <code>PUBLIC</code> and <code>INTERFACE</code> keywords. Projects may also get and set the property directly. See the discussion in <code>target_precompile_headers()</code> for guidance on appropriate use of this property for installed or exported targets. </p>
<p> Contents of <code>INTERFACE_PRECOMPILE_HEADERS</code> may use &quot;generator expressions&quot; with the syntax <code>$&lt;&#8230;&gt;</code>. See the <code><a href="/docs/linux/man/7-cmake-generator-expressions/">cmake-generator-expressions</a>(7)</code> manual for available expressions. See the <code><a href="/docs/linux/man/7-cmake-buildsystem/">cmake-buildsystem</a>(7)</code> manual for more on defining buildsystem properties.</p>
<h3>Interface_Sources</h3>
<p>New in version 3.1. </p>
<p> List of interface sources to compile into consuming targets. </p>
<p> Targets may populate this property to publish the sources for consuming targets to compile. The <code>target_sources()</code> command populates this property with values given to the <code>PUBLIC</code> and <code>INTERFACE</code> keywords. Projects may also get and set the property directly. </p>
<p> When target dependencies are specified using <code>target_link_libraries()</code>, CMake will read this property from all target dependencies to determine the sources of the consumer. </p>
<p> Contents of <code>INTERFACE_SOURCES</code> may use &quot;generator expressions&quot; with the syntax <code>$&lt;&#8230;&gt;</code>. See the <code><a href="/docs/linux/man/7-cmake-generator-expressions/">cmake-generator-expressions</a>(7)</code> manual for available expressions. See the <code><a href="/docs/linux/man/7-cmake-buildsystem/">cmake-buildsystem</a>(7)</code> manual for more on defining buildsystem properties.</p>
<h3>Interface_System_Include_Directories</h3>
<p>List of public system include directories for a library. </p>
<p> Targets may populate this property to publish the include directories which contain system headers, and therefore should not result in compiler warnings. The <code>target_include_directories(SYSTEM)</code> command signature populates this property with values given to the <code>PUBLIC</code> and <code>INTERFACE</code> keywords. </p>
<p> Projects may also get and set the property directly, but must be aware that adding directories to this property does not make those directories used during compilation. Adding directories to this property marks directories as <code>SYSTEM</code> which otherwise would be used in a non-<code>SYSTEM</code> manner. This can appear similar to &#8216;duplication&#8217;, so prefer the high-level <code>target_include_directories(SYSTEM)</code> command and avoid setting the property by low-level means. </p>
<p> When target dependencies are specified using <code>target_link_libraries()</code>, CMake will read this property from all target dependencies to mark the same include directories as containing system headers. </p>
<p> Contents of <code>INTERFACE_SYSTEM_INCLUDE_DIRECTORIES</code> may use &quot;generator expressions&quot; with the syntax <code>$&lt;&#8230;&gt;</code>. See the <code><a href="/docs/linux/man/7-cmake-generator-expressions/">cmake-generator-expressions</a>(7)</code> manual for available expressions. See the <code><a href="/docs/linux/man/7-cmake-buildsystem/">cmake-buildsystem</a>(7)</code> manual for more on defining buildsystem properties.</p>
<h3>Interprocedural_Optimization</h3>
<p>Enable interprocedural optimization for a target. </p>
<p> If set to true, enables interprocedural optimizations if they are known <code>to be supported</code> by the compiler. Depending on value of policy <code>CMP0069</code>, the error will be reported or ignored, if interprocedural optimization is enabled but not supported. </p>
<p> This property is initialized by the <code>CMAKE_INTERPROCEDURAL_OPTIMIZATION</code> variable if it is set when a target is created.</p>
<h3>INTERPROCEDURAL_OPTIMIZATION_&lt;CONFIG&gt;</h3>
<p>Per-configuration interprocedural optimization for a target. </p>
<p> This is a per-configuration version of <code>INTERPROCEDURAL_OPTIMIZATION</code>. If set, this property overrides the generic property for the named configuration. </p>
<p> This property is initialized by the <code>CMAKE_INTERPROCEDURAL_OPTIMIZATION_&lt;CONFIG&gt;</code> variable if it is set when a target is created.</p>
<h3>Ios_Install_Combined</h3>
<p>New in version 3.5. </p>
<p> Build a combined (device and simulator) target when installing. </p>
<p> When this property is set to set to false (which is the default) then it will either be built with the device SDK or the simulator SDK depending on the SDK set. But if this property is set to true then the target will at install time also be built for the corresponding SDK and combined into one library. </p>
<p> <code>NOTE:</code> If a selected architecture is available for both: device SDK and simulator SDK it will be built for the SDK selected by <code>CMAKE_OSX_SYSROOT</code> and removed from the corresponding SDK. </p>
<p> This feature requires at least Xcode version 6.</p>
<h3>Ispc_Header_Directory</h3>
<p>New in version 3.19. </p>
<p> Specify relative output directory for ISPC headers provided by the target. </p>
<p> If the target contains ISPC source files, this specifies the directory in which the generated headers will be placed. Relative paths are treated with respect to the value of <code>CMAKE_CURRENT_BINARY_DIR</code>. When this property is not set, the headers will be placed a generator defined build directory. If the variable <code>CMAKE_ISPC_HEADER_DIRECTORY</code> is set when a target is created its value is used to initialize this property.</p>
<h3>Ispc_Header_Suffix</h3>
<p>New in version 3.19.2. </p>
<p> Specify output suffix to be used for ISPC generated headers provided by the target. </p>
<p> This property is initialized by the value of the <code>CMAKE_ISPC_HEADER_SUFFIX</code> variable if it is set when a target is created. </p>
<p> If the target contains ISPC source files, this specifies the header suffix to be used for the generated headers. </p>
<p> The default value is <code>_ispc.h</code>.</p>
<h3>Ispc_Instruction_Sets</h3>
<p>New in version 3.19. </p>
<p> List of instruction set architectures to generate code for. </p>
<p> This property is initialized by the value of the <code>CMAKE_ISPC_INSTRUCTION_SETS</code> variable if it is set when a target is created. </p>
<p> The <code>ISPC_INSTRUCTION_SETS</code> target property must be used when generating for multiple instruction sets so that CMake can track what object files will be generated.</p>
<h3>Examples</h3>
<pre>set_property(TARGET tgt PROPERTY ISPC_INSTRUCTION_SETS avx2-i32x4 avx512skx-i32x835)</pre>
<p>Generates code for avx2 and avx512skx target architectures.</p>
<h3>Job_Pool_Compile</h3>
<p>Ninja only: Pool used for compiling. </p>
<p> The number of parallel compile processes could be limited by defining pools with the global <code>JOB_POOLS</code> property and then specifying here the pool name. </p>
<p> For instance: </p>
<pre>set_property(TARGET myexe PROPERTY JOB_POOL_COMPILE ten_jobs)</pre>
<p>This property is initialized by the value of <code>CMAKE_JOB_POOL_COMPILE</code>.</p>
<h3>Job_Pool_Link</h3>
<p>Ninja only: Pool used for linking. </p>
<p> The number of parallel link processes could be limited by defining pools with the global <code>JOB_POOLS</code> property and then specifying here the pool name. </p>
<p> For instance: </p>
<pre>set_property(TARGET myexe PROPERTY JOB_POOL_LINK two_jobs)</pre>
<p>This property is initialized by the value of <code>CMAKE_JOB_POOL_LINK</code>.</p>
<h3>Job_Pool_Precompile_Header</h3>
<p>New in version 3.17. </p>
<p> Ninja only: Pool used for generating pre-compiled headers. </p>
<p> The number of parallel compile processes could be limited by defining pools with the global <code>JOB_POOLS</code> property and then specifying here the pool name. </p>
<p> For instance: </p>
<pre>set_property(TARGET myexe PROPERTY JOB_POOL_PRECOMPILE_HEADER two_jobs)</pre>
<p>This property is initialized by the value of <code>CMAKE_JOB_POOL_PRECOMPILE_HEADER</code>. </p>
<p> If neither <i>JOB_POOL_PRECOMPILE_HEADER</i> nor <code>CMAKE_JOB_POOL_PRECOMPILE_HEADER</code> are set then <code>JOB_POOL_COMPILE</code> will be used for this task.</p>
<h3>Labels</h3>
<p>Specify a list of text labels associated with a target. </p>
<p> Target label semantics are currently unspecified.</p>
<h3>&lt;LANG&gt;_CLANG_TIDY</h3>
<p>New in version 3.6. </p>
<p> This property is implemented only when <code>&lt;LANG&gt;</code> is <code>C</code>, <code>CXX</code>, <code>OBJC</code> or <code>OBJCXX</code>. </p>
<p> Specify a semicolon-separated list containing a command line for the <code>clang-tidy</code> tool. The Makefile Generators and the <code>Ninja</code> generator will run this tool along with the compiler and report a warning if the tool reports any problems. </p>
<p> This property is initialized by the value of the <code>CMAKE_&lt;LANG&gt;_CLANG_TIDY</code> variable if it is set when a target is created.</p>
<h3>&lt;LANG&gt;_COMPILER_LAUNCHER</h3>
<p>New in version 3.4. </p>
<p> This property is implemented only when <code>&lt;LANG&gt;</code> is <code>C</code>, <code>CXX</code>, <code>Fortran</code>, <code>HIP</code>, <code>ISPC</code>, <code>OBJC</code>, <code>OBJCXX</code>, or <code>CUDA</code>. </p>
<p> Specify a semicolon-separated list containing a command line for a compiler launching tool. The Makefile Generators and the <code>Ninja</code> generator will run this tool and pass the compiler and its arguments to the tool. Some example tools are distcc and ccache. </p>
<p> This property is initialized by the value of the <code>CMAKE_&lt;LANG&gt;_COMPILER_LAUNCHER</code> variable if it is set when a target is created.</p>
<h3>&lt;LANG&gt;_CPPCHECK</h3>
<p>New in version 3.10. </p>
<p> This property is supported only when <code>&lt;LANG&gt;</code> is <code>C</code> or <code>CXX</code>. </p>
<p> Specify a semicolon-separated list containing a command line for the <code>cppcheck</code> static analysis tool. The Makefile Generators and the <code>Ninja</code> generator will run <code>cppcheck</code> along with the compiler and report any problems. If the command-line specifies the exit code options to <code>cppcheck</code> then the build will fail if the tool returns non-zero. </p>
<p> This property is initialized by the value of the <code>CMAKE_&lt;LANG&gt;_CPPCHECK</code> variable if it is set when a target is created.</p>
<h3>&lt;LANG&gt;_CPPLINT</h3>
<p>New in version 3.8. </p>
<p> This property is supported only when <code>&lt;LANG&gt;</code> is <code>C</code> or <code>CXX</code>. </p>
<p> Specify a semicolon-separated list containing a command line for the <code>cpplint</code> style checker. The Makefile Generators and the <code>Ninja</code> generator will run <code>cpplint</code> along with the compiler and report any problems. </p>
<p> This property is initialized by the value of the <code>CMAKE_&lt;LANG&gt;_CPPLINT</code> variable if it is set when a target is created.</p>
<h3>&lt;LANG&gt;_EXTENSIONS</h3>
<p>The variations are:</p>
<dl>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd><code>C_EXTENSIONS</code></p>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd><code>CXX_EXTENSIONS</code></p>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd><code>CUDA_EXTENSIONS</code></p>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd><code>HIP_EXTENSIONS</code></p>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd><code>OBJC_EXTENSIONS</code></p>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd><code>OBJCXX_EXTENSIONS</code> </p>
<p> These properties specify whether compiler-specific extensions are requested. </p>
<p> These properties are initialized by the value of the <code>CMAKE_&lt;LANG&gt;_EXTENSIONS</code> variable if it is set when a target is created and otherwise by the value of <code>CMAKE_&lt;LANG&gt;_EXTENSIONS_DEFAULT</code> (see <code>CMP0128</code>). </p>
<p> For supported CMake versions see the respective pages. To control language standard versions see <code>&lt;LANG&gt;_STANDARD</code>. </p>
<p> See the <code><a href="/docs/linux/man/7-cmake-compile-features/">cmake-compile-features</a>(7)</code> manual for information on compile features and a list of supported compilers.</dl>
<h3>&lt;LANG&gt;_INCLUDE_WHAT_YOU_USE</h3>
<p>New in version 3.3. </p>
<p> This property is implemented only when <code>&lt;LANG&gt;</code> is <code>C</code> or <code>CXX</code>. </p>
<p> Specify a semicolon-separated list containing a command line for the <code>include-what-you-use</code> tool. The Makefile Generators and the <code>Ninja</code> generator will run this tool along with the compiler and report a warning if the tool reports any problems. </p>
<p> This property is initialized by the value of the <code>CMAKE_&lt;LANG&gt;_INCLUDE_WHAT_YOU_USE</code> variable if it is set when a target is created.</p>
<h3>&lt;LANG&gt;_LINKER_LAUNCHER</h3>
<p>New in version 3.21. </p>
<p> This property is implemented only when <code>&lt;LANG&gt;</code> is <code>C</code>, <code>CXX</code>, <code>OBJC</code>, or <code>OBJCXX</code> </p>
<p> Specify a semicolon-separated list containing a command line for a linker launching tool. The Makefile Generators and the <code>Ninja</code> generator will run this tool and pass the linker and its arguments to the tool. This is useful for tools such as static analyzers. </p>
<p> This property is initialized by the value of the <code>CMAKE_&lt;LANG&gt;_LINKER_LAUNCHER</code> variable if it is set when a target is created.</p>
<h3>&lt;LANG&gt;_STANDARD</h3>
<p>The variations are:</p>
<dl>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd><code>C_STANDARD</code></p>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd><code>CXX_STANDARD</code></p>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd><code>CUDA_STANDARD</code></p>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd><code>HIP_STANDARD</code></p>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd><code>OBJC_STANDARD</code></p>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd><code>OBJCXX_STANDARD</code> </p>
<p> These properties specify language standard versions which are requested. When a newer standard is specified than is supported by the compiler, then it will fallback to the latest supported standard. This &quot;decay&quot; behavior may be controlled with the <code>&lt;LANG&gt;_STANDARD_REQUIRED</code> target property. </p>
<p> These properties are initialized by the value of the <code>CMAKE_&lt;LANG&gt;_STANDARD</code> variable if it is set when a target is created. </p>
<p> For supported values and CMake versions see the respective pages. To control compiler-specific extensions see <code>&lt;LANG&gt;_EXTENSIONS</code>. </p>
<p> See the <code><a href="/docs/linux/man/7-cmake-compile-features/">cmake-compile-features</a>(7)</code> manual for information on compile features and a list of supported compilers.</dl>
<h3>&lt;LANG&gt;_STANDARD_REQUIRED</h3>
<p>The variations are:</p>
<dl>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd><code>C_STANDARD_REQUIRED</code></p>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd><code>CXX_STANDARD_REQUIRED</code></p>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd><code>CUDA_STANDARD_REQUIRED</code></p>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd><code>HIP_STANDARD_REQUIRED</code></p>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd><code>OBJC_STANDARD_REQUIRED</code></p>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd><code>OBJCXX_STANDARD_REQUIRED</code> </p>
<p> These properties specify whether the value of <code>&lt;LANG&gt;_STANDARD</code> is a requirement. When <code>OFF</code> or unset, the <code>&lt;LANG&gt;_STANDARD</code> target property is treated as optional and may &quot;decay&quot; to a previous standard if the requested is not available. </p>
<p> These properties are initialized by the value of the <code>CMAKE_&lt;LANG&gt;_STANDARD_REQUIRED</code> variable if it is set when a target is created. </p>
<p> For supported CMake versions see the respective pages. To control language standard versions see <code>&lt;LANG&gt;_STANDARD</code>. </p>
<p> See the <code><a href="/docs/linux/man/7-cmake-compile-features/">cmake-compile-features</a>(7)</code> manual for information on compile features and a list of supported compilers.</dl>
<h3>&lt;LANG&gt;_VISIBILITY_PRESET</h3>
<p>Value for symbol visibility compile flags </p>
<p> The <code>&lt;LANG&gt;_VISIBILITY_PRESET</code> property determines the value passed in a visibility related compile option, such as <code>-fvisibility=</code> for <code>&lt;LANG&gt;</code>. This property affects compilation in sources of all types of targets (subject to policy <code>CMP0063</code>). </p>
<p> This property is initialized by the value of the <code>CMAKE_&lt;LANG&gt;_VISIBILITY_PRESET</code> variable if it is set when a target is created.</p>
<h3>Library_Output_Directory</h3>
<p>Output directory in which to build LIBRARY target files. </p>
<p> This property specifies the directory into which library target files should be built. The property value may use <code>generator expressions</code>. Multi-configuration generators (Visual Studio, <code>Xcode</code>, <code>Ninja Multi-Config</code>) append a per-configuration subdirectory to the specified directory unless a generator expression is used. </p>
<p> This property is initialized by the value of the <code>CMAKE_LIBRARY_OUTPUT_DIRECTORY</code> variable if it is set when a target is created. </p>
<p> See also the <code>LIBRARY_OUTPUT_DIRECTORY_&lt;CONFIG&gt;</code> target property.</p>
<h3>LIBRARY_OUTPUT_DIRECTORY_&lt;CONFIG&gt;</h3>
<p>Per-configuration output directory for LIBRARY target files. </p>
<p> This is a per-configuration version of the <code>LIBRARY_OUTPUT_DIRECTORY</code> target property, but multi-configuration generators (Visual Studio Generators, <code>Xcode</code>) do NOT append a per-configuration subdirectory to the specified directory. This property is initialized by the value of the <code>CMAKE_LIBRARY_OUTPUT_DIRECTORY_&lt;CONFIG&gt;</code> variable if it is set when a target is created. </p>
<p> Contents of <code>LIBRARY_OUTPUT_DIRECTORY_&lt;CONFIG&gt;</code> may use <code>generator expressions</code>.</p>
<h3>Library_Output_Name</h3>
<p>Output name for LIBRARY target files. </p>
<p> This property specifies the base name for library target files. It overrides <code>OUTPUT_NAME</code> and <code>OUTPUT_NAME_&lt;CONFIG&gt;</code> properties. </p>
<p> See also the <code>LIBRARY_OUTPUT_NAME_&lt;CONFIG&gt;</code> target property.</p>
<h3>LIBRARY_OUTPUT_NAME_&lt;CONFIG&gt;</h3>
<p>Per-configuration output name for LIBRARY target files. </p>
<p> This is the configuration-specific version of the <code>LIBRARY_OUTPUT_NAME</code> target property.</p>
<h3>Link_Depends</h3>
<p>Additional files on which a target binary depends for linking. </p>
<p> Specifies a semicolon-separated list of full-paths to files on which the link rule for this target depends. The target binary will be linked if any of the named files is newer than it. </p>
<p> This property is supported only by <code>Ninja</code> and Makefile Generators. It is intended to specify dependencies on &quot;linker scripts&quot; for custom Makefile link rules. </p>
<p> Contents of <code>LINK_DEPENDS</code> may use &quot;generator expressions&quot; with the syntax <code>$&lt;&#8230;&gt;</code>. See the <code><a href="/docs/linux/man/7-cmake-generator-expressions/">cmake-generator-expressions</a>(7)</code> manual for available expressions. See the <code><a href="/docs/linux/man/7-cmake-buildsystem/">cmake-buildsystem</a>(7)</code> manual for more on defining buildsystem properties.</p>
<h3>Link_Depends_No_Shared</h3>
<p>Do not depend on linked shared library files. </p>
<p> Set this property to true to tell CMake generators not to add file-level dependencies on the shared library files linked by this target. Modification to the shared libraries will not be sufficient to re-link this target. Logical target-level dependencies will not be affected so the linked shared libraries will still be brought up to date before this target is built. </p>
<p> This property is initialized by the value of the <code>CMAKE_LINK_DEPENDS_NO_SHARED</code> variable if it is set when a target is created.</p>
<h3>Link_Directories</h3>
<p>New in version 3.13. </p>
<p> List of directories to use for the link step of shared library, module and executable targets. </p>
<p> This property holds a semicolon-separated list of directories specified so far for its target. Use the <code>target_link_directories()</code> command to append more search directories. </p>
<p> This property is initialized by the <code>LINK_DIRECTORIES</code> directory property when a target is created, and is used by the generators to set the search directories for the linker. </p>
<p> Contents of <code>LINK_DIRECTORIES</code> may use &quot;generator expressions&quot; with the syntax <code>$&lt;&#8230;&gt;</code>. See the <code><a href="/docs/linux/man/7-cmake-generator-expressions/">cmake-generator-expressions</a>(7)</code> manual for available expressions. See the <code><a href="/docs/linux/man/7-cmake-buildsystem/">cmake-buildsystem</a>(7)</code> manual for more on defining buildsystem properties.</p>
<h3>Link_Flags</h3>
<p>Additional flags to use when linking this target if it is a shared library, module library, or an executable. Static libraries need to use <code>STATIC_LIBRARY_OPTIONS</code> or <code>STATIC_LIBRARY_FLAGS</code> properties. </p>
<p> The <code>LINK_FLAGS</code> property, managed as a string, can be used to add extra flags to the link step of a target. <code>LINK_FLAGS_&lt;CONFIG&gt;</code> will add to the configuration <code>&lt;CONFIG&gt;</code>, for example, <code>DEBUG</code>, <code>RELEASE</code>, <code>MINSIZEREL</code>, <code>RELWITHDEBINFO</code>, &#8230; </p>
<p> <code>NOTE:</code> This property has been superseded by <code>LINK_OPTIONS</code> property.</p>
<h3>LINK_FLAGS_&lt;CONFIG&gt;</h3>
<p>Per-configuration linker flags for a <code>SHARED</code> library, <code>MODULE</code> or <code>EXECUTABLE</code> target. </p>
<p> This is the configuration-specific version of <code>LINK_FLAGS</code>. </p>
<p> <code>NOTE:</code> This property has been superseded by <code>LINK_OPTIONS</code> property.</p>
<h3>Link_Interface_Libraries</h3>
<p>List public interface libraries for a shared library or executable. </p>
<p> By default linking to a shared library target transitively links to targets with which the library itself was linked. For an executable with exports (see the <code>ENABLE_EXPORTS</code> target property) no default transitive link dependencies are used. This property replaces the default transitive link dependencies with an explicit list. When the target is linked into another target using the <code>target_link_libraries()</code> command, the libraries listed (and recursively their link interface libraries) will be provided to the other target also. If the list is empty then no transitive link dependencies will be incorporated when this target is linked into another target even if the default set is non-empty. This property is initialized by the value of the <code>CMAKE_LINK_INTERFACE_LIBRARIES</code> variable if it is set when a target is created. This property is ignored for <code>STATIC</code> libraries. </p>
<p> This property is overridden by the <code>INTERFACE_LINK_LIBRARIES</code> property if policy <code>CMP0022</code> is <code>NEW</code>. </p>
<p> This property is deprecated. Use <code>INTERFACE_LINK_LIBRARIES</code> instead.</p>
<h3>Creating Relocatable Packages</h3>
<p>Note that it is not advisable to populate the <code>LINK_INTERFACE_LIBRARIES</code> of a target with absolute paths to dependencies. That would hard-code into installed packages the library file paths for dependencies <code>as found on the machine the package was made on</code>. </p>
<p> See the Creating Relocatable Packages section of the <code><a href="/docs/linux/man/7-cmake-packages/">cmake-packages</a>(7)</code> manual for discussion of additional care that must be taken when specifying usage requirements while creating packages for redistribution.</p>
<h3>LINK_INTERFACE_LIBRARIES_&lt;CONFIG&gt;</h3>
<p>Per-configuration list of public interface libraries for a target. </p>
<p> This is the configuration-specific version of <code>LINK_INTERFACE_LIBRARIES</code>. If set, this property completely overrides the generic property for the named configuration. </p>
<p> This property is overridden by the <code>INTERFACE_LINK_LIBRARIES</code> property if policy <code>CMP0022</code> is <code>NEW</code>. </p>
<p> This property is deprecated. Use <code>INTERFACE_LINK_LIBRARIES</code> instead.</p>
<h3>Creating Relocatable Packages</h3>
<p>Note that it is not advisable to populate the <code>LINK_INTERFACE_LIBRARIES_&lt;CONFIG&gt;</code> of a target with absolute paths to dependencies. That would hard-code into installed packages the library file paths for dependencies <code>as found on the machine the package was made on</code>. </p>
<p> See the Creating Relocatable Packages section of the <code><a href="/docs/linux/man/7-cmake-packages/">cmake-packages</a>(7)</code> manual for discussion of additional care that must be taken when specifying usage requirements while creating packages for redistribution.</p>
<h3>Link_Interface_Multiplicity</h3>
<p>Repetition count for <code>STATIC</code> libraries with cyclic dependencies. </p>
<p> When linking to a <code>STATIC</code> library target with cyclic dependencies the linker may need to scan more than once through the archives in the strongly connected component of the dependency graph. CMake by default constructs the link line so that the linker will scan through the component at least twice. This property specifies the minimum number of scans if it is larger than the default. CMake uses the largest value specified by any target in a component.</p>
<h3>LINK_INTERFACE_MULTIPLICITY_&lt;CONFIG&gt;</h3>
<p>Per-configuration repetition count for cycles of <code>STATIC</code> libraries. </p>
<p> This is the configuration-specific version of <code>LINK_INTERFACE_MULTIPLICITY</code>. If set, this property completely overrides the generic property for the named configuration.</p>
<h3>Link_Libraries</h3>
<p>List of direct link dependencies. </p>
<p> This property specifies the list of libraries or targets which will be used for linking. In addition to accepting values from the <code>target_link_libraries()</code> command, values may be set directly on any target using the <code>set_property()</code> command. </p>
<p> The value of this property is used by the generators to set the link libraries for the compiler. </p>
<p> Contents of <code>LINK_LIBRARIES</code> may use &quot;generator expressions&quot; with the syntax <code>$&lt;&#8230;&gt;</code>. See the <code><a href="/docs/linux/man/7-cmake-generator-expressions/">cmake-generator-expressions</a>(7)</code> manual for available expressions. See the <code><a href="/docs/linux/man/7-cmake-buildsystem/">cmake-buildsystem</a>(7)</code> manual for more on defining buildsystem properties. </p>
<p> <code>NOTE:</code> A call to <code>target_link_libraries(&lt;target&gt; &#8230;)</code> may update this property on <code>&lt;target&gt;</code>. If <code>&lt;target&gt;</code> was not created in the same directory as the call then <code>target_link_libraries()</code> will wrap each entry with the form <code>::@(directory-id);&#8230;;::@</code>, where the <code>::@</code> is literal and the <code>(directory-id)</code> is unspecified. This tells the generators that the named libraries must be looked up in the scope of the caller rather than in the scope in which the <code>&lt;target&gt;</code> was created. Valid directory ids are stripped on export by the <code>install(EXPORT)</code> and <code>export()</code> commands.</p>
<h3>Link_Options</h3>
<p>New in version 3.13. </p>
<p> List of options to use for the link step of shared library, module and executable targets as well as the device link step. Targets that are static libraries need to use the <code>STATIC_LIBRARY_OPTIONS</code> target property. </p>
<p> These options are used for both normal linking and device linking (see policy <code>CMP0105</code>). To control link options for normal and device link steps, <code>$&lt;HOST_LINK&gt;</code> and <code>$&lt;DEVICE_LINK&gt;</code> <code>generator expressions</code> can be used. </p>
<p> This property holds a semicolon-separated list of options specified so far for its target. Use the <code>target_link_options()</code> command to append more options. </p>
<p> This property is initialized by the <code>LINK_OPTIONS</code> directory property when a target is created, and is used by the generators to set the options for the compiler. </p>
<p> Contents of <code>LINK_OPTIONS</code> may use &quot;generator expressions&quot; with the syntax <code>$&lt;&#8230;&gt;</code>. See the <code><a href="/docs/linux/man/7-cmake-generator-expressions/">cmake-generator-expressions</a>(7)</code> manual for available expressions. See the <code><a href="/docs/linux/man/7-cmake-buildsystem/">cmake-buildsystem</a>(7)</code> manual for more on defining buildsystem properties. </p>
<p> <code>NOTE:</code> This property must be used in preference to <code>LINK_FLAGS</code> property.</p>
<h3>Host And Device Specific Link Options</h3>
<p>New in version 3.18: When a device link step is involved, which is controlled by <code>CUDA_SEPARABLE_COMPILATION</code> and <code>CUDA_RESOLVE_DEVICE_SYMBOLS</code> properties and policy <code>CMP0105</code>, the raw options will be delivered to the host and device link steps (wrapped in <code>-Xcompiler</code> or equivalent for device link). Options wrapped with <code>$&lt;DEVICE_LINK:&#8230;&gt;</code> <code>generator expression</code> will be used only for the device link step. Options wrapped with <code>$&lt;HOST_LINK:&#8230;&gt;</code> <code>generator expression</code> will be used only for the host link step.</p>
<h3>Option De-duplication</h3>
<p>The final set of options used for a target is constructed by accumulating options from the current target and the usage requirements of its dependencies. The set of options is de-duplicated to avoid repetition. </p>
<p> New in version 3.12: While beneficial for individual options, the de-duplication step can break up option groups. For example, <code>-option A -option B</code> becomes <code>-option A B</code>. One may specify a group of options using shell-like quoting along with a <code>SHELL:</code> prefix. The <code>SHELL:</code> prefix is dropped, and the rest of the option string is parsed using the <code>separate_arguments()</code> <code>UNIX_COMMAND</code> mode. For example, <code>&quot;SHELL:-option A&quot; &quot;SHELL:-option B&quot;</code> becomes <code>-option A -option B</code>.</p>
<h3>Handling Compiler Driver Differences</h3>
<p>To pass options to the linker tool, each compiler driver has its own syntax. The <code>LINKER:</code> prefix and <code>,</code> separator can be used to specify, in a portable way, options to pass to the linker tool. <code>LINKER:</code> is replaced by the appropriate driver option and <code>,</code> by the appropriate driver separator. The driver prefix and driver separator are given by the values of the <code>CMAKE_&lt;LANG&gt;_LINKER_WRAPPER_FLAG</code> and <code>CMAKE_&lt;LANG&gt;_LINKER_WRAPPER_FLAG_SEP</code> variables. </p>
<p> For example, <code>&quot;LINKER:-z,defs&quot;</code> becomes <code>-Xlinker -z -Xlinker defs</code> for <code>Clang</code> and <code>-Wl,-z,defs</code> for <code>GNU GCC</code>. </p>
<p> The <code>LINKER:</code> prefix can be specified as part of a <code>SHELL:</code> prefix expression. </p>
<p> The <code>LINKER:</code> prefix supports, as an alternative syntax, specification of arguments using the <code>SHELL:</code> prefix and space as separator. The previous example then becomes <code>&quot;LINKER:SHELL:-z defs&quot;</code>. </p>
<p> <code>NOTE:</code> Specifying the <code>SHELL:</code> prefix anywhere other than at the beginning of the <code>LINKER:</code> prefix is not supported.</p>
<h3>Link_Search_End_Static</h3>
<p>End a link line such that static system libraries are used. </p>
<p> Some linkers support switches such as <code>-Bstatic</code> and <code>-Bdynamic</code> to determine whether to use static or shared libraries for <code>-lXXX</code> options. CMake uses these options to set the link type for libraries whose full paths are not known or (in some cases) are in implicit link directories for the platform. By default CMake adds an option at the end of the library list (if necessary) to set the linker search type back to its starting type. This property switches the final linker search type to <code>-Bstatic</code> regardless of how it started. </p>
<p> This property is initialized by the value of the variable <code>CMAKE_LINK_SEARCH_END_STATIC</code> if it is set when a target is created. </p>
<p> See also <code>LINK_SEARCH_START_STATIC</code>.</p>
<h3>Link_Search_Start_Static</h3>
<p>Assume the linker looks for static libraries by default. </p>
<p> Some linkers support switches such as <code>-Bstatic</code> and <code>-Bdynamic</code> to determine whether to use static or shared libraries for <code>-lXXX</code> options. CMake uses these options to set the link type for libraries whose full paths are not known or (in some cases) are in implicit link directories for the platform. By default the linker search type is assumed to be <code>-Bdynamic</code> at the beginning of the library list. This property switches the assumption to <code>-Bstatic</code>. It is intended for use when linking an executable statically (e.g. with the GNU <code>-static</code> option).</p>
<dl>
<dt><code>This property is initialized by the value of the variable</code></p>
<dd><code>CMAKE_LINK_SEARCH_START_STATIC</code> if it is set when a target is created. </p>
<p> See also <code>LINK_SEARCH_END_STATIC</code>.</dl>
<h3>Link_What_You_Use</h3>
<p>New in version 3.7. </p>
<p> This is a boolean option that, when set to <code>TRUE</code>, will automatically run contents of variable <code>CMAKE_LINK_WHAT_YOU_USE_CHECK</code> on the target after it is linked. In addition, the linker flag specified by variable <code>CMAKE_&lt;LANG&gt;_LINK_WHAT_YOU_USE_FLAG</code> will be passed to the target with the link command so that all libraries specified on the command line will be linked into the target. This will result in the link producing a list of libraries that provide no symbols used by this target but are being linked to it. </p>
<p> <code>NOTE:</code> For now, it is only supported for <code>ELF</code> platforms and is only applicable to executable and shared or module library targets. This property will be ignored for any other targets and configurations. </p>
<p> This property is initialized by the value of the <code>CMAKE_LINK_WHAT_YOU_USE</code> variable if it is set when a target is created.</p>
<h3>Linker_Language</h3>
<p>Specifies language whose compiler will invoke the linker. </p>
<p> For executables, shared libraries, and modules, this sets the language whose compiler is used to link the target (such as &quot;C&quot; or &quot;CXX&quot;). A typical value for an executable is the language of the source file providing the program entry point (main). If not set, the language with the highest linker preference value is the default. See documentation of <code>CMAKE_&lt;LANG&gt;_LINKER_PREFERENCE</code> variables. </p>
<p> If this property is not set by the user, it will be calculated at generate-time by CMake.</p>
<h3>Location</h3>
<p>Read-only location of a target on disk. </p>
<p> For an imported target, this read-only property returns the value of the <code>LOCATION_&lt;CONFIG&gt;</code> property for an unspecified configuration <code>&lt;CONFIG&gt;</code> provided by the target. </p>
<p> For a non-imported target, this property is provided for compatibility with CMake 2.4 and below. It was meant to get the location of an executable target&#8217;s output file for use in <code>add_custom_command()</code>. The path may contain a build-system-specific portion that is replaced at build time with the configuration getting built (such as <code>$(ConfigurationName)</code> in VS). In CMake 2.6 and above <code>add_custom_command()</code> automatically recognizes a target name in its <code>COMMAND</code> and <code>DEPENDS</code> options and computes the target location. In CMake 2.8.4 and above <code>add_custom_command()</code> recognizes <code>generator expressions</code> to refer to target locations anywhere in the command. Therefore this property is not needed for creating custom commands. </p>
<p> Do not set properties that affect the location of a target after reading this property. These include properties whose names match <code>(RUNTIME|LIBRARY|ARCHIVE)_OUTPUT_(NAME|DIRECTORY)(_&lt;CONFIG&gt;)?</code>, <code>(IMPLIB_)?(PREFIX|SUFFIX)</code>, or &quot;LINKER_LANGUAGE&quot;. Failure to follow this rule is not diagnosed and leaves the location of the target undefined.</p>
<h3>LOCATION_&lt;CONFIG&gt;</h3>
<p>Read-only property providing a target location on disk. </p>
<p> A read-only property that indicates where a target&#8217;s main file is located on disk for the configuration <code>&lt;CONFIG&gt;</code>. The property is defined only for library and executable targets. An imported target may provide a set of configurations different from that of the importing project. By default CMake looks for an exact-match but otherwise uses an arbitrary available configuration. Use the <code>MAP_IMPORTED_CONFIG_&lt;CONFIG&gt;</code> property to map imported configurations explicitly. </p>
<p> Do not set properties that affect the location of a target after reading this property. These include properties whose names match <code>(RUNTIME|LIBRARY|ARCHIVE)_OUTPUT_(NAME|DIRECTORY)(_&lt;CONFIG&gt;)?</code>, <code>(IMPLIB_)?(PREFIX|SUFFIX)</code>, or <code>LINKER_LANGUAGE</code>. Failure to follow this rule is not diagnosed and leaves the location of the target undefined.</p>
<h3>Macho_Compatibility_Version</h3>
<p>New in version 3.17. </p>
<p> What compatibility version number is this target for Mach-O binaries. </p>
<p> For shared libraries on Mach-O systems (e.g. macOS, iOS) the <code>MACHO_COMPATIBILITY_VERSION</code> property corresponds to the <i>compatibility version</i> and <code>MACHO_CURRENT_VERSION</code> corresponds to the <i>current version</i>. These are both embedded in the shared library binary and can be checked with the <code>otool -L &lt;binary&gt;</code> command. </p>
<p> It should be noted that the <code>MACHO_CURRENT_VERSION</code> and <code>MACHO_COMPATIBILITY_VERSION</code> properties do not affect the file names or version-related symlinks that CMake generates for the library. The <code>VERSION</code> and <code>SOVERSION</code> target properties still control the file and symlink names. The <code>install_name</code> is also still controlled by <code>SOVERSION</code>. </p>
<p> When <code>MACHO_CURRENT_VERSION</code> and <code>MACHO_COMPATIBILITY_VERSION</code> are not given, <code>VERSION</code> and <code>SOVERSION</code> are used for the version details to be embedded in the binaries respectively. The <code>MACHO_CURRENT_VERSION</code> and <code>MACHO_COMPATIBILITY_VERSION</code> properties only need to be given if the project needs to decouple the file and symlink naming from the version details embedded in the binaries (e.g. to match libtool conventions).</p>
<h3>Macho_Current_Version</h3>
<p>New in version 3.17. </p>
<p> What current version number is this target for Mach-O binaries. </p>
<p> For shared libraries on Mach-O systems (e.g. macOS, iOS) the <code>MACHO_COMPATIBILITY_VERSION</code> property corresponds to the <i>compatibility version</i> and <code>MACHO_CURRENT_VERSION</code> corresponds to the <i>current version</i>. These are both embedded in the shared library binary and can be checked with the <code>otool -L &lt;binary&gt;</code> command. </p>
<p> It should be noted that the <code>MACHO_CURRENT_VERSION</code> and <code>MACHO_COMPATIBILITY_VERSION</code> properties do not affect the file names or version-related symlinks that CMake generates for the library. The <code>VERSION</code> and <code>SOVERSION</code> target properties still control the file and symlink names. The <code>install_name</code> is also still controlled by <code>SOVERSION</code>. </p>
<p> When <code>MACHO_CURRENT_VERSION</code> and <code>MACHO_COMPATIBILITY_VERSION</code> are not given, <code>VERSION</code> and <code>SOVERSION</code> are used for the version details to be embedded in the binaries respectively. The <code>MACHO_CURRENT_VERSION</code> and <code>MACHO_COMPATIBILITY_VERSION</code> properties only need to be given if the project needs to decouple the file and symlink naming from the version details embedded in the binaries (e.g. to match libtool conventions).</p>
<h3>Macosx_Bundle</h3>
<p>Build an executable as an Application Bundle on macOS or iOS. </p>
<p> When this property is set to <code>TRUE</code> the executable when built on macOS or iOS will be created as an application bundle. This makes it a GUI executable that can be launched from the Finder. See the <code>MACOSX_BUNDLE_INFO_PLIST</code> target property for information about creation of the <code>Info.plist</code> file for the application bundle. This property is initialized by the value of the variable <code>CMAKE_MACOSX_BUNDLE</code> if it is set when a target is created.</p>
<h3>Macosx_Bundle_Info_Plist</h3>
<p>Specify a custom <code>Info.plist</code> template for a macOS and iOS Application Bundle. </p>
<p> An executable target with <code>MACOSX_BUNDLE</code> enabled will be built as an application bundle on macOS. By default its <code>Info.plist</code> file is created by configuring a template called <code>MacOSXBundleInfo.plist.in</code> located in the <code>CMAKE_MODULE_PATH</code>. This property specifies an alternative template file name which may be a full path. </p>
<p> The following target properties may be set to specify content to be configured into the file:</p>
<dl>
<dt><code>MACOSX_BUNDLE_BUNDLE_NAME</code></p>
<dd>Sets <code>CFBundleName</code>.</p>
<dt><code>MACOSX_BUNDLE_BUNDLE_VERSION</code></p>
<dd>Sets <code>CFBundleVersion</code>.</p>
<dt><code>MACOSX_BUNDLE_COPYRIGHT</code></p>
<dd>Sets <code>NSHumanReadableCopyright</code>.</p>
<dt><code>MACOSX_BUNDLE_GUI_IDENTIFIER</code></p>
<dd>Sets <code>CFBundleIdentifier</code>.</p>
<dt><code>MACOSX_BUNDLE_ICON_FILE</code></p>
<dd>Sets <code>CFBundleIconFile</code>.</p>
<dt><code>MACOSX_BUNDLE_INFO_STRING</code></p>
<dd>Sets <code>CFBundleGetInfoString</code>.</p>
<dt><code>MACOSX_BUNDLE_LONG_VERSION_STRING</code></p>
<dd>Sets <code>CFBundleLongVersionString</code>.</p>
<dt><code>MACOSX_BUNDLE_SHORT_VERSION_STRING</code></p>
<dd>Sets <code>CFBundleShortVersionString</code>. </p>
<p> CMake variables of the same name may be set to affect all targets in a directory that do not have each specific property set. If a custom <code>Info.plist</code> is specified by this property it may of course hard-code all the settings instead of using the target properties.</dl>
<h3>Macosx_Framework_Info_Plist</h3>
<p>Specify a custom <code>Info.plist</code> template for a macOS and iOS Framework. </p>
<p> A library target with <code>FRAMEWORK</code> enabled will be built as a framework on macOS. By default its <code>Info.plist</code> file is created by configuring a template called <code>MacOSXFrameworkInfo.plist.in</code> located in the <code>CMAKE_MODULE_PATH</code>. This property specifies an alternative template file name which may be a full path. </p>
<p> The following target properties may be set to specify content to be configured into the file:</p>
<dl>
<dt><code>MACOSX_FRAMEWORK_BUNDLE_VERSION</code></p>
<dd>Sets <code>CFBundleVersion</code>.</p>
<dt><code>MACOSX_FRAMEWORK_ICON_FILE</code></p>
<dd>Sets <code>CFBundleIconFile</code>.</p>
<dt><code>MACOSX_FRAMEWORK_IDENTIFIER</code></p>
<dd>Sets <code>CFBundleIdentifier</code>.</p>
<dt><code>MACOSX_FRAMEWORK_SHORT_VERSION_STRING</code></p>
<dd>Sets <code>CFBundleShortVersionString</code>. </p>
<p> CMake variables of the same name may be set to affect all targets in a directory that do not have each specific property set. If a custom <code>Info.plist</code> is specified by this property it may of course hard-code all the settings instead of using the target properties.</dl>
<h3>Macosx_Rpath</h3>
<p>Whether this target on macOS or iOS is located at runtime using rpaths. </p>
<p> When this property is set to <code>TRUE</code>, the directory portion of the <code>install_name</code> field of this shared library will be <code>@rpath</code> unless overridden by <code>INSTALL_NAME_DIR</code>. This indicates the shared library is to be found at runtime using runtime paths (rpaths). </p>
<p> This property is initialized by the value of the variable <code>CMAKE_MACOSX_RPATH</code> if it is set when a target is created. </p>
<p> Runtime paths will also be embedded in binaries using this target and can be controlled by the <code>INSTALL_RPATH</code> target property on the target linking to this target. </p>
<p> Policy <code>CMP0042</code> was introduced to change the default value of <code>MACOSX_RPATH</code> to <code>TRUE</code>. This is because use of <code>@rpath</code> is a more flexible and powerful alternative to <code>@executable_path</code> and <code>@loader_path</code>.</p>
<h3>Manually_Added_Dependencies</h3>
<p>New in version 3.8. </p>
<p> Get manually added dependencies to other top-level targets. </p>
<p> This read-only property can be used to query all dependencies that were added for this target with the <code>add_dependencies()</code> command.</p>
<h3>MAP_IMPORTED_CONFIG_&lt;CONFIG&gt;</h3>
<p>Map from project configuration to imported target&#8217;s configuration. </p>
<p> Set this to the list of configurations of an imported target that may be used for the current project&#8217;s <code>&lt;CONFIG&gt;</code> configuration. Targets imported from another project may not provide the same set of configuration names available in the current project. Setting this property tells CMake what imported configurations are suitable for use when building the <code>&lt;CONFIG&gt;</code> configuration. The first configuration in the list found to be provided by the imported target (i.e. via <code>IMPORTED_LOCATION_&lt;CONFIG&gt;</code> for the mapped-to <code>&lt;CONFIG&gt;</code>) is selected. As a special case, an empty list element refers to the configuration-less imported target location (i.e. <code>IMPORTED_LOCATION</code>). </p>
<p> If this property is set and no matching configurations are available, then the imported target is considered to be not found. This property is ignored for non-imported targets. </p>
<p> This property is initialized by the value of the <code>CMAKE_MAP_IMPORTED_CONFIG_&lt;CONFIG&gt;</code> variable if it is set when a target is created.</p>
<h3>Example</h3>
<p>For example creating imported C++ library <code>foo</code>: </p>
<pre>add_library(foo STATIC IMPORTED)</pre>
<p>Use <code>foo_debug</code> path for <code>Debug</code> build type: </p>
<pre>set_property(
 TARGET foo APPEND PROPERTY IMPORTED_CONFIGURATIONS DEBUG
 )
set_target_properties(foo PROPERTIES
 IMPORTED_LINK_INTERFACE_LANGUAGES_DEBUG &quot;CXX&quot;
 IMPORTED_LOCATION_DEBUG &quot;${foo_debug}&quot;
 )</pre>
<p>Use <code>foo_release</code> path for <code>Release</code> build type: </p>
<pre>set_property(
 TARGET foo APPEND PROPERTY IMPORTED_CONFIGURATIONS RELEASE
 )
set_target_properties(foo PROPERTIES
 IMPORTED_LINK_INTERFACE_LANGUAGES_RELEASE &quot;CXX&quot;
 IMPORTED_LOCATION_RELEASE &quot;${foo_release}&quot;
 )</pre>
<p>Use <code>Release</code> version of library for <code>MinSizeRel</code> and <code>RelWithDebInfo</code> build types: </p>
<pre>set_target_properties(foo PROPERTIES
 MAP_IMPORTED_CONFIG_MINSIZEREL Release
 MAP_IMPORTED_CONFIG_RELWITHDEBINFO Release
 )</pre>
<h3>Msvc_Runtime_Library</h3>
<p>New in version 3.15. </p>
<p> Select the MSVC runtime library for use by compilers targeting the MSVC ABI. </p>
<p> The allowed values are:</p>
<dl>
<dt><code>MultiThreaded</code></p>
<dd>Compile with <code>-MT</code> or equivalent flag(s) to use a multi-threaded statically-linked runtime library.</p>
<dt><code>MultiThreadedDLL</code></p>
<dd>Compile with <code>-MD</code> or equivalent flag(s) to use a multi-threaded dynamically-linked runtime library.</p>
<dt><code>MultiThreadedDebug</code></p>
<dd>Compile with <code>-MTd</code> or equivalent flag(s) to use a multi-threaded statically-linked runtime library.</p>
<dt><code>MultiThreadedDebugDLL</code></p>
<dd>Compile with <code>-MDd</code> or equivalent flag(s) to use a multi-threaded dynamically-linked runtime library. </p>
<p> The value is ignored on non-MSVC compilers but an unsupported value will be rejected as an error when using a compiler targeting the MSVC ABI. </p>
<p> The value may also be the empty string (<code>&quot;&quot;</code>) in which case no runtime library selection flag will be added explicitly by CMake. Note that with Visual Studio Generators the native build system may choose to add its own default runtime library selection flag. </p>
