<p> Use <code>generator expressions</code> to support per-configuration specification. For example, the code: </p>
<pre>add_executable(foo foo.c)
set_property(TARGET foo PROPERTY
 MSVC_RUNTIME_LIBRARY &quot;MultiThreaded$&lt;$&lt;CONFIG:Debug&gt;:Debug&gt;&quot;)</pre>
<p>selects for the target <code>foo</code> a multi-threaded statically-linked runtime library with or without debug information depending on the configuration. </p>
<p> If this property is not set then CMake uses the default value <code>MultiThreaded$&lt;$&lt;CONFIG:Debug&gt;:Debug&gt;DLL</code> to select a MSVC runtime library. </p>
<p> <code>NOTE:</code> This property has effect only when policy <code>CMP0091</code> is set to <code>NEW</code> prior to the first <code>project()</code> or <code>enable_language()</code> command that enables a language using a compiler targeting the MSVC ABI.</dl>
<h3>Name</h3>
<p>Logical name for the target. </p>
<p> Read-only logical name for the target as used by CMake.</p>
<h3>No_Soname</h3>
<p>Whether to set <code>soname</code> when linking a shared library. </p>
<p> Enable this boolean property if a generated <code>SHARED</code> library should not have <code>soname</code> set. Default is to set <code>soname</code> on all shared libraries as long as the platform supports it. Generally, use this property only for leaf private libraries or plugins. If you use it on normal shared libraries which other targets link against, on some platforms a linker will insert a full path to the library (as specified at link time) into the dynamic section of the dependent binary. Therefore, once installed, dynamic loader may eventually fail to locate the library for the binary.</p>
<h3>No_System_From_Imported</h3>
<p>Do not treat include directories from the interfaces of consumed imported targets as <code>SYSTEM</code>. </p>
<p> The contents of the <code>INTERFACE_INCLUDE_DIRECTORIES</code> target property of imported targets are treated as <code>SYSTEM</code> includes by default. If this property is enabled on a target, compilation of sources in that target will not treat the contents of the <code>INTERFACE_INCLUDE_DIRECTORIES</code> of consumed imported targets as system includes. </p>
<p> This property is initialized by the value of the <code>CMAKE_NO_SYSTEM_FROM_IMPORTED</code> variable if it is set when a target is created.</p>
<h3>Objc_Extensions</h3>
<p>New in version 3.16. </p>
<p> Boolean specifying whether compiler specific extensions are requested. </p>
<p> This property specifies whether compiler specific extensions should be used. For some compilers, this results in adding a flag such as <code>-std=gnu11</code> instead of <code>-std=c11</code> to the compile line. This property is <code>ON</code> by default. The basic OBJC standard level is controlled by the <code>OBJC_STANDARD</code> target property. </p>
<p> If the property is not set, and the project has set the <code>C_EXTENSIONS</code>, the value of <code>C_EXTENSIONS</code> is set for <i>OBJC_EXTENSIONS</i>. </p>
<p> See the <code><a href="/docs/linux/man/7-cmake-compile-features/">cmake-compile-features</a>(7)</code> manual for information on compile features and a list of supported compilers. </p>
<p> This property is initialized by the value of the <code>CMAKE_OBJC_EXTENSIONS</code> variable if set when a target is created and otherwise by the value of <code>CMAKE_OBJC_EXTENSIONS_DEFAULT</code> (see <code>CMP0128</code>).</p>
<h3>Objc_Standard</h3>
<p>New in version 3.16. </p>
<p> The OBJC standard whose features are requested to build this target. </p>
<p> This property specifies the OBJC standard whose features are requested to build this target. For some compilers, this results in adding a flag such as <code>-std=gnu11</code> to the compile line. </p>
<p> Supported values are:</p>
<dl>
<dt><code>90</code></p>
<dd>Objective C89/C90</p>
<dt><code>99</code></p>
<dd>Objective C99</p>
<dt><code>11</code></p>
<dd>Objective C11 </p>
<p> If the value requested does not result in a compile flag being added for the compiler in use, a previous standard flag will be added instead. This means that using: </p>
<pre>set_property(TARGET tgt PROPERTY OBJC_STANDARD 11)</pre>
<p>with a compiler which does not support <code>-std=gnu11</code> or an equivalent flag will not result in an error or warning, but will instead add the <code>-std=gnu99</code> or <code>-std=gnu90</code> flag if supported. This &quot;decay&quot; behavior may be controlled with the <code>OBJC_STANDARD_REQUIRED</code> target property. Additionally, the <code>OBJC_EXTENSIONS</code> target property may be used to control whether compiler-specific extensions are enabled on a per-target basis. </p>
<p> If the property is not set, and the project has set the <code>C_STANDARD</code>, the value of <code>C_STANDARD</code> is set for <i>OBJC_STANDARD</i>. </p>
<p> See the <code><a href="/docs/linux/man/7-cmake-compile-features/">cmake-compile-features</a>(7)</code> manual for information on compile features and a list of supported compilers. </p>
<p> This property is initialized by the value of the <code>CMAKE_OBJC_STANDARD</code> variable if it is set when a target is created.</dl>
<h3>Objc_Standard_Required</h3>
<p>New in version 3.16. </p>
<p> Boolean describing whether the value of <code>OBJC_STANDARD</code> is a requirement. </p>
<p> If this property is set to <code>ON</code>, then the value of the <code>OBJC_STANDARD</code> target property is treated as a requirement. If this property is <code>OFF</code> or unset, the <code>OBJC_STANDARD</code> target property is treated as optional and may &quot;decay&quot; to a previous standard if the requested is not available. </p>
<p> If the property is not set, and the project has set the <code>C_STANDARD_REQUIRED</code>, the value of <code>C_STANDARD_REQUIRED</code> is set for <i>OBJC_STANDARD_REQUIRED</i>. </p>
<p> See the <code><a href="/docs/linux/man/7-cmake-compile-features/">cmake-compile-features</a>(7)</code> manual for information on compile features and a list of supported compilers. </p>
<p> This property is initialized by the value of the <code>CMAKE_OBJC_STANDARD_REQUIRED</code> variable if it is set when a target is created.</p>
<h3>Objcxx_Extensions</h3>
<p>New in version 3.16. </p>
<p> Boolean specifying whether compiler specific extensions are requested. </p>
<p> This property specifies whether compiler specific extensions should be used. For some compilers, this results in adding a flag such as <code>-std=gnu++11</code> instead of <code>-std=c++11</code> to the compile line. This property is <code>ON</code> by default. The basic ObjC++ standard level is controlled by the <code>OBJCXX_STANDARD</code> target property. </p>
<p> See the <code><a href="/docs/linux/man/7-cmake-compile-features/">cmake-compile-features</a>(7)</code> manual for information on compile features and a list of supported compilers. </p>
<p> If the property is not set, and the project has set the <code>CXX_EXTENSIONS</code>, the value of <code>CXX_EXTENSIONS</code> is set for <i>OBJCXX_EXTENSIONS</i>. </p>
<p> This property is initialized by the value of the <code>CMAKE_OBJCXX_EXTENSIONS</code> variable if set when a target is created and otherwise by the value of <code>CMAKE_OBJCXX_EXTENSIONS_DEFAULT</code> (see <code>CMP0128</code>).</p>
<h3>Objcxx_Standard</h3>
<p>New in version 3.16. </p>
<p> The ObjC++ standard whose features are requested to build this target. </p>
<p> This property specifies the ObjC++ standard whose features are requested to build this target. For some compilers, this results in adding a flag such as <code>-std=gnu++11</code> to the compile line. </p>
<p> Supported values are:</p>
<dl>
<dt><code>98</code></p>
<dd>Objective C++98</p>
<dt><code>11</code></p>
<dd>Objective C++11</p>
<dt><code>14</code></p>
<dd>Objective C++14</p>
<dt><code>17</code></p>
<dd>Objective C++17</p>
<dt><code>20</code></p>
<dd>Objective C++20</p>
<dt><code>23</code></p>
<dd>New in version 3.20. </p>
<p> Objective C++23 </p>
<p> If the value requested does not result in a compile flag being added for the compiler in use, a previous standard flag will be added instead. This means that using: </p>
<pre>set_property(TARGET tgt PROPERTY OBJCXX_STANDARD 11)</pre>
<p>with a compiler which does not support <code>-std=gnu++11</code> or an equivalent flag will not result in an error or warning, but will instead add the <code>-std=gnu++98</code> flag if supported. This &quot;decay&quot; behavior may be controlled with the <code>OBJCXX_STANDARD_REQUIRED</code> target property. Additionally, the <code>OBJCXX_EXTENSIONS</code> target property may be used to control whether compiler-specific extensions are enabled on a per-target basis. </p>
<p> If the property is not set, and the project has set the <code>CXX_STANDARD</code>, the value of <code>CXX_STANDARD</code> is set for <i>OBJCXX_STANDARD</i>. </p>
<p> See the <code><a href="/docs/linux/man/7-cmake-compile-features/">cmake-compile-features</a>(7)</code> manual for information on compile features and a list of supported compilers. </p>
<p> This property is initialized by the value of the <code>CMAKE_OBJCXX_STANDARD</code> variable if it is set when a target is created.</dl>
<h3>Objcxx_Standard_Required</h3>
<p>New in version 3.16. </p>
<p> Boolean describing whether the value of <code>OBJCXX_STANDARD</code> is a requirement. </p>
<p> If this property is set to <code>ON</code>, then the value of the <code>OBJCXX_STANDARD</code> target property is treated as a requirement. If this property is <code>OFF</code> or unset, the <code>OBJCXX_STANDARD</code> target property is treated as optional and may &quot;decay&quot; to a previous standard if the requested is not available. </p>
<p> If the property is not set, and the project has set the <code>CXX_STANDARD_REQUIRED</code>, the value of <code>CXX_STANDARD_REQUIRED</code> is set for <i>OBJCXX_STANDARD_REQUIRED</i>. </p>
<p> See the <code><a href="/docs/linux/man/7-cmake-compile-features/">cmake-compile-features</a>(7)</code> manual for information on compile features and a list of supported compilers. </p>
<p> This property is initialized by the value of the <code>CMAKE_OBJCXX_STANDARD_REQUIRED</code> variable if it is set when a target is created.</p>
<h3>Optimize_Dependencies</h3>
<p>New in version 3.19. </p>
<p> Activates dependency optimization of static and object libraries. </p>
<p> When this property is set to true, some dependencies for a static or object library may be removed at generation time if they are not necessary to build the library, since static and object libraries don&#8217;t actually link against anything. </p>
<p> If a static or object library has dependency optimization enabled, it first discards all dependencies. Then, it looks through all of the direct and indirect dependencies that it initially had, and adds them back if they meet any of the following criteria:</p>
<dl>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd>The dependency was added to the library by <code>add_dependencies()</code>.</p>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd>The dependency was added to the library through a source file in the library generated by a custom command that uses the dependency.</p>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd>The dependency has any <code>PRE_BUILD</code>, <code>PRE_LINK</code>, or <code>POST_BUILD</code> custom commands associated with it.</p>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd>The dependency contains any source files that were generated by a custom command.</p>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd>The dependency contains any languages which produce side effects that are relevant to the library. Currently, all languages except C, C++, Objective-C, Objective-C++, assembly, and CUDA are assumed to produce side effects. However, side effects from one language are assumed not to be relevant to another (for example, a Fortran library is assumed to not have any side effects that are relevant for a Swift library.) </p>
<p> As an example, assume you have a static Fortran library which depends on a static C library, which in turn depends on a static Fortran library. The top-level Fortran library has optimization enabled, but the middle C library does not. If you build the top Fortran library, the bottom Fortran library will also build, but not the middle C library, since the C library does not have any side effects that are relevant for the Fortran library. However, if you build the middle C library, the bottom Fortran library will also build, even though it does not have any side effects that are relevant to the C library, since the C library does not have optimization enabled. </p>
<p> This property is initialized by the value of the <code>CMAKE_OPTIMIZE_DEPENDENCIES</code> variable when the target is created.</dl>
<h3>Osx_Architectures</h3>
<p>Target specific architectures for macOS. </p>
<p> The <code>OSX_ARCHITECTURES</code> property sets the target binary architecture for targets on macOS (<code>-arch</code>). This property is initialized by the value of the variable <code>CMAKE_OSX_ARCHITECTURES</code> if it is set when a target is created. Use <code>OSX_ARCHITECTURES_&lt;CONFIG&gt;</code> to set the binary architectures on a per-configuration basis, where <code>&lt;CONFIG&gt;</code> is an upper-case name (e.g. <code>OSX_ARCHITECTURES_DEBUG</code>).</p>
<h3>OSX_ARCHITECTURES_&lt;CONFIG&gt;</h3>
<p>Per-configuration macOS and iOS binary architectures for a target. </p>
<p> This property is the configuration-specific version of <code>OSX_ARCHITECTURES</code>.</p>
<h3>Output_Name</h3>
<p>Output name for target files. </p>
<p> This sets the base name for output files created for an executable or library target. If not set, the logical target name is used by default during generation. The value is not set by default during configuration. </p>
<p> Contents of <code>OUTPUT_NAME</code> and the variants listed below may use <code>generator expressions</code>. </p>
<p> See also the variants:</p>
<dl>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd><code>OUTPUT_NAME_&lt;CONFIG&gt;</code></p>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd><code>ARCHIVE_OUTPUT_NAME_&lt;CONFIG&gt;</code></p>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd><code>ARCHIVE_OUTPUT_NAME</code></p>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd><code>LIBRARY_OUTPUT_NAME_&lt;CONFIG&gt;</code></p>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd><code>LIBRARY_OUTPUT_NAME</code></p>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd><code>RUNTIME_OUTPUT_NAME_&lt;CONFIG&gt;</code></p>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd><code>RUNTIME_OUTPUT_NAME</code></dl>
<h3>OUTPUT_NAME_&lt;CONFIG&gt;</h3>
<p>Per-configuration target file base name. </p>
<p> This is the configuration-specific version of the <code>OUTPUT_NAME</code> target property.</p>
<h3>Pch_Warn_Invalid</h3>
<p>New in version 3.18. </p>
<p> When this property is set to true, the precompile header compiler options will contain a compiler flag which should warn about invalid precompiled headers e.g. <code>-Winvalid-pch</code> for GNU compiler. </p>
<p> This property is initialized by the value of the <code>CMAKE_PCH_WARN_INVALID</code> variable if it is set when a target is created. If that variable is not set, the property defaults to <code>ON</code>.</p>
<h3>Pch_Instantiate_Templates</h3>
<p>New in version 3.19. </p>
<p> When this property is set to true, the precompiled header compiler options will contain a flag to instantiate templates during the generation of the PCH if supported. This can significantly improve compile times. Supported in Clang since version 11. </p>
<p> This property is initialized by the value of the <code>CMAKE_PCH_INSTANTIATE_TEMPLATES</code> variable if it is set when a target is created. If that variable is not set, the property defaults to <code>ON</code>.</p>
<h3>Pdb_Name</h3>
<p>Output name for the MS debug symbol <code>.pdb</code> file generated by the linker for an executable or shared library target. </p>
<p> This property specifies the base name for the debug symbols file. If not set, the <code>OUTPUT_NAME</code> target property value or logical target name is used by default. </p>
<p> <code>NOTE:</code> This property does not apply to STATIC library targets because no linker is invoked to produce them so they have no linker-generated <code>.pdb</code> file containing debug symbols. </p>
<p> The linker-generated program database files are specified by the <code>/pdb</code> linker flag and are not the same as compiler-generated program database files specified by the <code>/Fd</code> compiler flag. Use the <code>COMPILE_PDB_NAME</code> property to specify the latter.</p>
<h3>PDB_NAME_&lt;CONFIG&gt;</h3>
<p>Per-configuration output name for the MS debug symbol <code>.pdb</code> file generated by the linker for an executable or shared library target. </p>
<p> This is the configuration-specific version of <code>PDB_NAME</code>. </p>
<p> <code>NOTE:</code> This property does not apply to STATIC library targets because no linker is invoked to produce them so they have no linker-generated <code>.pdb</code> file containing debug symbols. </p>
<p> The linker-generated program database files are specified by the <code>/pdb</code> linker flag and are not the same as compiler-generated program database files specified by the <code>/Fd</code> compiler flag. Use the <code>COMPILE_PDB_NAME_&lt;CONFIG&gt;</code> property to specify the latter.</p>
<h3>Pdb_Output_Directory</h3>
<p>Output directory for the MS debug symbols <code>.pdb</code> file generated by the linker for an executable or shared library target. </p>
<p> This property specifies the directory into which the MS debug symbols will be placed by the linker. The property value may use <code>generator expressions</code>. Multi-configuration generators append a per-configuration subdirectory to the specified directory unless a generator expression is used. </p>
<p> This property is initialized by the value of the <code>CMAKE_PDB_OUTPUT_DIRECTORY</code> variable if it is set when a target is created. </p>
<p> <code>NOTE:</code> This property does not apply to STATIC library targets because no linker is invoked to produce them so they have no linker-generated <code>.pdb</code> file containing debug symbols. </p>
<p> The linker-generated program database files are specified by the <code>/pdb</code> linker flag and are not the same as compiler-generated program database files specified by the <code>/Fd</code> compiler flag. Use the <code>COMPILE_PDB_OUTPUT_DIRECTORY</code> property to specify the latter.</p>
<h3>PDB_OUTPUT_DIRECTORY_&lt;CONFIG&gt;</h3>
<p>Per-configuration output directory for the MS debug symbol <code>.pdb</code> file generated by the linker for an executable or shared library target. </p>
<p> This is a per-configuration version of <code>PDB_OUTPUT_DIRECTORY</code>, but multi-configuration generators (Visual Studio Generators, <code>Xcode</code>) do NOT append a per-configuration subdirectory to the specified directory. This property is initialized by the value of the <code>CMAKE_PDB_OUTPUT_DIRECTORY_&lt;CONFIG&gt;</code> variable if it is set when a target is created. </p>
<p> Contents of <code>PDB_OUTPUT_DIRECTORY_&lt;CONFIG&gt;</code> may use <code>generator expressions</code>. </p>
<p> <code>NOTE:</code> This property does not apply to STATIC library targets because no linker is invoked to produce them so they have no linker-generated <code>.pdb</code> file containing debug symbols. </p>
<p> The linker-generated program database files are specified by the <code>/pdb</code> linker flag and are not the same as compiler-generated program database files specified by the <code>/Fd</code> compiler flag. Use the <code>COMPILE_PDB_OUTPUT_DIRECTORY_&lt;CONFIG&gt;</code> property to specify the latter.</p>
<h3>Position_Independent_Code</h3>
<p>Whether to create a position-independent target </p>
<p> The <code>POSITION_INDEPENDENT_CODE</code> property determines whether position independent executables or shared libraries will be created. This property is <code>True</code> by default for <code>SHARED</code> and <code>MODULE</code> library targets and <code>False</code> otherwise. This property is initialized by the value of the <code>CMAKE_POSITION_INDEPENDENT_CODE</code> variable if it is set when a target is created. </p>
<p> <code>NOTE:</code> For executable targets, the link step is controlled by the <code>CMP0083</code> policy and the <code>CheckPIESupported</code> module.</p>
<h3>Precompile_Headers</h3>
<p>New in version 3.16. </p>
<p> List of header files to precompile. </p>
<p> This property holds a semicolon-separated list of header files to precompile specified so far for its target. Use the <code>target_precompile_headers()</code> command to append more header files. </p>
<p> This property supports <code>generator expressions</code>.</p>
<h3>Precompile_Headers_Reuse_From</h3>
<p>New in version 3.16. </p>
<p> Target from which to reuse the precompiled headers build artifact. </p>
<p> See the second signature of <code>target_precompile_headers()</code> command for more detailed information.</p>
<h3>Prefix</h3>
<p>What comes before the library name. </p>
<p> A target property that can be set to override the prefix (such as <code>lib</code>) on a library name.</p>
<h3>Private_Header</h3>
<p>Specify private header files in a <code>FRAMEWORK</code> shared library target. </p>
<p> Shared library targets marked with the <code>FRAMEWORK</code> property generate frameworks on macOS, iOS and normal shared libraries on other platforms. This property may be set to a list of header files to be placed in the PrivateHeaders directory inside the framework folder. On non-Apple platforms these headers may be installed using the <code>PRIVATE_HEADER</code> option to the <code>install(TARGETS)</code> command.</p>
<h3>Project_Label</h3>
<p>Change the name of a target in an IDE. </p>
<p> Can be used to change the name of the target in an IDE like Visual Studio.</p>
<h3>Public_Header</h3>
<p>Specify public header files in a <code>FRAMEWORK</code> shared library target. </p>
<p> Shared library targets marked with the <code>FRAMEWORK</code> property generate frameworks on macOS, iOS and normal shared libraries on other platforms. This property may be set to a list of header files to be placed in the <code>Headers</code> directory inside the framework folder. On non-Apple platforms these headers may be installed using the <code>PUBLIC_HEADER</code> option to the <code>install(TARGETS)</code> command.</p>
<h3>Resource</h3>
<p>Specify resource files in a <code>FRAMEWORK</code> or <code>BUNDLE</code>. </p>
<p> Target marked with the <code>FRAMEWORK</code> or <code>BUNDLE</code> property generate framework or application bundle (both macOS and iOS is supported) or normal shared libraries on other platforms. This property may be set to a list of files to be placed in the corresponding directory (eg. <code>Resources</code> directory for macOS) inside the bundle. On non-Apple platforms these files may be installed using the <code>RESOURCE</code> option to the <code>install(TARGETS)</code> command. </p>
<p> Following example of Application Bundle: </p>
<pre>add_executable(ExecutableTarget
 addDemo.c
 resourcefile.txt
 appresourcedir/appres.txt)
target_link_libraries(ExecutableTarget heymath mul)
set(RESOURCE_FILES
 resourcefile.txt
 appresourcedir/appres.txt)
set_target_properties(ExecutableTarget PROPERTIES
 MACOSX_BUNDLE TRUE
 MACOSX_FRAMEWORK_IDENTIFIER org.cmake.ExecutableTarget
 RESOURCE &quot;${RESOURCE_FILES}&quot;)</pre>
<p>will produce flat structure for iOS systems: </p>
<pre>ExecutableTarget.app
 appres.txt
 ExecutableTarget
 Info.plist
 resourcefile.txt</pre>
<p>For macOS systems it will produce following directory structure: </p>
<pre>ExecutableTarget.app/
 Contents
 Info.plist
 MacOS
 ExecutableTarget
 Resources
 appres.txt
 resourcefile.txt</pre>
<p>For Linux, such CMake script produce following files: </p>
<pre>ExecutableTarget
Resources
 appres.txt
 resourcefile.txt</pre>
<h3>Rule_Launch_Compile</h3>
<p>Specify a launcher for compile rules. </p>
<p> See the global property of the same name for details. This overrides the global and directory property for a target.</p>
<h3>Rule_Launch_Custom</h3>
<p>Specify a launcher for custom rules. </p>
<p> See the global property of the same name for details. This overrides the global and directory property for a target.</p>
<h3>Rule_Launch_Link</h3>
<p>Specify a launcher for link rules. </p>
<p> See the global property of the same name for details. This overrides the global and directory property for a target.</p>
<h3>Runtime_Output_Directory</h3>
<p>Output directory in which to build RUNTIME target files. </p>
<p> This property specifies the directory into which runtime target files should be built. The property value may use <code>generator expressions</code>. Multi-configuration generators (Visual Studio, <code>Xcode</code>, <code>Ninja Multi-Config</code>) append a per-configuration subdirectory to the specified directory unless a generator expression is used. </p>
<p> This property is initialized by the value of the <code>CMAKE_RUNTIME_OUTPUT_DIRECTORY</code> variable if it is set when a target is created. </p>
<p> See also the <code>RUNTIME_OUTPUT_DIRECTORY_&lt;CONFIG&gt;</code> target property.</p>
<h3>RUNTIME_OUTPUT_DIRECTORY_&lt;CONFIG&gt;</h3>
<p>Per-configuration output directory for RUNTIME target files. </p>
<p> This is a per-configuration version of the <code>RUNTIME_OUTPUT_DIRECTORY</code> target property, but multi-configuration generators (Visual Studio Generators, <code>Xcode</code>) do NOT append a per-configuration subdirectory to the specified directory. This property is initialized by the value of the <code>CMAKE_RUNTIME_OUTPUT_DIRECTORY_&lt;CONFIG&gt;</code> variable if it is set when a target is created. </p>
<p> Contents of <code>RUNTIME_OUTPUT_DIRECTORY_&lt;CONFIG&gt;</code> may use <code>generator expressions</code>.</p>
<h3>Runtime_Output_Name</h3>
<p>Output name for RUNTIME target files. </p>
<p> This property specifies the base name for runtime target files. It overrides <code>OUTPUT_NAME</code> and <code>OUTPUT_NAME_&lt;CONFIG&gt;</code> properties. </p>
<p> See also the <code>RUNTIME_OUTPUT_NAME_&lt;CONFIG&gt;</code> target property.</p>
<h3>RUNTIME_OUTPUT_NAME_&lt;CONFIG&gt;</h3>
<p>Per-configuration output name for RUNTIME target files. </p>
<p> This is the configuration-specific version of the <code>RUNTIME_OUTPUT_NAME</code> target property.</p>
<h3>Skip_Build_Rpath</h3>
<p>Should rpaths be used for the build tree. </p>
<p> <code>SKIP_BUILD_RPATH</code> is a boolean specifying whether to skip automatic generation of an rpath allowing the target to run from the build tree. This property is initialized by the value of the variable <code>CMAKE_SKIP_BUILD_RPATH</code> if it is set when a target is created.</p>
<h3>Source_Dir</h3>
<p>New in version 3.4. </p>
<p> This read-only property reports the value of the <code>CMAKE_CURRENT_SOURCE_DIR</code> variable in the directory in which the target was defined.</p>
<h3>Sources</h3>
<p>Source names specified for a target. </p>
<p> List of sources specified for a target.</p>
<h3>Soversion</h3>
<p>What version number is this target. </p>
<p> For shared libraries <code>VERSION</code> and <code>SOVERSION</code> can be used to specify the build version and API version respectively. When building or installing appropriate symlinks are created if the platform supports symlinks and the linker supports so-names. If only one of both is specified the missing is assumed to have the same version number. <code>SOVERSION</code> is ignored if <code>NO_SONAME</code> property is set.</p>
<h3>Windows Versions</h3>
<p>For shared libraries and executables on Windows the <code>VERSION</code> attribute is parsed to extract a <code>&lt;major&gt;.&lt;minor&gt;</code> version number. These numbers are used as the image version of the binary.</p>
<h3>Mach-O Versions</h3>
<p>For shared libraries and executables on Mach-O systems (e.g. macOS, iOS), the <code>SOVERSION</code> property corresponds to the <i>compatibility version</i> and <code>VERSION</code> corresponds to the <i>current version</i> (unless Mach-O specific overrides are provided, as discussed below). See the <code>FRAMEWORK</code> target property for an example. </p>
<p> For shared libraries, the <code>MACHO_COMPATIBILITY_VERSION</code> and <code>MACHO_CURRENT_VERSION</code> properties can be used to override the <i>compatibility version</i> and <i>current version</i> respectively. Note that <code>SOVERSION</code> will still be used to form the <code>install_name</code> and both <code>SOVERSION</code> and <code>VERSION</code> may also affect the file and symlink names. </p>
<p> Versions of Mach-O binaries may be checked with the <code>otool -L &lt;binary&gt;</code> command.</p>
<h3>Static_Library_Flags</h3>
<p>Archiver (or MSVC librarian) flags for a static library target. Targets that are shared libraries, modules, or executables need to use the <code>LINK_OPTIONS</code> or <code>LINK_FLAGS</code> target properties. </p>
<p> The <code>STATIC_LIBRARY_FLAGS</code> property, managed as a string, can be used to add extra flags to the link step of a static library target. <code>STATIC_LIBRARY_FLAGS_&lt;CONFIG&gt;</code> will add to the configuration <code>&lt;CONFIG&gt;</code>, for example, <code>DEBUG</code>, <code>RELEASE</code>, <code>MINSIZEREL</code>, <code>RELWITHDEBINFO</code>, &#8230; </p>
<p> <code>NOTE:</code> This property has been superseded by <code>STATIC_LIBRARY_OPTIONS</code> property.</p>
<h3>STATIC_LIBRARY_FLAGS_&lt;CONFIG&gt;</h3>
<p>Per-configuration archiver (or MSVC librarian) flags for a static library target. </p>
<p> This is the configuration-specific version of <code>STATIC_LIBRARY_FLAGS</code>. </p>
<p> <code>NOTE:</code> This property has been superseded by <code>STATIC_LIBRARY_OPTIONS</code> property.</p>
<h3>Static_Library_Options</h3>
<p>New in version 3.13. </p>
<p> Archiver (or MSVC librarian) flags for a static library target. Targets that are shared libraries, modules, or executables need to use the <code>LINK_OPTIONS</code> target property. </p>
<p> This property holds a semicolon-separated list of options specified so far for its target. Use <code>set_target_properties()</code> or <code>set_property()</code> commands to set its content. </p>
<p> Contents of <code>STATIC_LIBRARY_OPTIONS</code> may use &quot;generator expressions&quot; with the syntax <code>$&lt;&#8230;&gt;</code>. See the <code><a href="/docs/linux/man/7-cmake-generator-expressions/">cmake-generator-expressions</a>(7)</code> manual for available expressions. See the <code><a href="/docs/linux/man/7-cmake-buildsystem/">cmake-buildsystem</a>(7)</code> manual for more on defining buildsystem properties. </p>
<p> <code>NOTE:</code> This property must be used in preference to <code>STATIC_LIBRARY_FLAGS</code> property.</p>
<h3>Option De-duplication</h3>
<p>The final set of options used for a target is constructed by accumulating options from the current target and the usage requirements of its dependencies. The set of options is de-duplicated to avoid repetition. </p>
<p> New in version 3.12: While beneficial for individual options, the de-duplication step can break up option groups. For example, <code>-option A -option B</code> becomes <code>-option A B</code>. One may specify a group of options using shell-like quoting along with a <code>SHELL:</code> prefix. The <code>SHELL:</code> prefix is dropped, and the rest of the option string is parsed using the <code>separate_arguments()</code> <code>UNIX_COMMAND</code> mode. For example, <code>&quot;SHELL:-option A&quot; &quot;SHELL:-option B&quot;</code> becomes <code>-option A -option B</code>.</p>
<h3>Suffix</h3>
<p>What comes after the target name. </p>
<p> A target property that can be set to override the suffix (such as <code>.so</code> or <code>.exe</code>) on the name of a library, module or executable.</p>
<h3>Swift_DEPENDENCIES_FILE</h3>
<p>New in version 3.15. </p>
<p> This property sets the path for the Swift dependency file (swiftdep) for the target. If one is not specified, it will default to <code>&lt;TARGET&gt;.swiftdeps</code>.</p>
<h3>Swift_LANGUAGE_VERSION</h3>
<p>New in version 3.16. </p>
<p> This property sets the language version for the Swift sources in the target. If one is not specified, it will default to <code>&lt;CMAKE_Swift_LANGUAGE_VERSION&gt;</code> if specified, otherwise it is the latest version supported by the compiler.</p>
<h3>Swift_MODULE_DIRECTORY</h3>
<p>New in version 3.15. </p>
<p> Specify output directory for Swift modules provided by the target. </p>
<p> If the target contains Swift source files, this specifies the directory in which the modules will be placed. When this property is not set, the modules will be placed in the build directory corresponding to the target&#8217;s source directory. If the variable <code>CMAKE_Swift_MODULE_DIRECTORY</code> is set when a target is created its value is used to initialize this property.</p>
<h3>Swift_MODULE_NAME</h3>
<p>New in version 3.15. </p>
<p> This property specifies the name of the Swift module. It is defaulted to the name of the target.</p>
<h3>Type</h3>
<p>The type of the target. </p>
<p> This read-only property can be used to test the type of the given target. It will be one of <code>STATIC_LIBRARY</code>, <code>MODULE_LIBRARY</code>, <code>SHARED_LIBRARY</code>, <code>OBJECT_LIBRARY</code>, <code>INTERFACE_LIBRARY</code>, <code>EXECUTABLE</code> or one of the internal target types.</p>
<h3>Unity_Build</h3>
<p>New in version 3.16. </p>
<p> When this property is set to true, the target source files will be combined into batches for faster compilation. This is done by creating a (set of) unity sources which <code>#include</code> the original sources, then compiling these unity sources instead of the originals. This is known as a <i>Unity</i> or <i>Jumbo</i> build. </p>
<p> CMake provides different algorithms for selecting which sources are grouped together into a <i>bucket</i>. Algorithm selection is decided by the <code>UNITY_BUILD_MODE</code> target property, which has the following acceptable values:</p>
<dl>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd><code>BATCH</code> When in this mode CMake determines which files are grouped together. The <code>UNITY_BUILD_BATCH_SIZE</code> property controls the upper limit on how many sources can be combined per unity source file.</p>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd><code>GROUP</code> When in this mode each target explicitly specifies how to group source files. Each source file that has the same <code>UNITY_GROUP</code> value will be grouped together. Any sources that don&#8217;t have this property will be compiled individually. The <code>UNITY_BUILD_BATCH_SIZE</code> property is ignored when using this mode. </p>
<p> If no explicit <code>UNITY_BUILD_MODE</code> has been specified, CMake will default to <code>BATCH</code>. </p>
<p> Unity builds are not currently supported for all languages. CMake version 3.22.1 supports combining <code>C</code> and <code>CXX</code> source files. For targets that mix source files from more than one language, CMake will separate the languages such that each generated unity source file only contains sources for a single language. </p>
<p> This property is initialized by the value of the <code>CMAKE_UNITY_BUILD</code> variable when a target is created. </p>
<p> <code>NOTE:</code> Projects should not directly set the <code>UNITY_BUILD</code> property or its associated <code>CMAKE_UNITY_BUILD</code> variable to true. Depending on the capabilities of the build machine and compiler used, it might or might not be appropriate to enable unity builds. Therefore, this feature should be under developer control, which would normally be through the developer choosing whether or not to set the <code>CMAKE_UNITY_BUILD</code> variable on the <code><a href="/docs/linux/man/1-cmake/">cmake</a>(1)</code> command line or some other equivalent method. However, it IS recommended to set the <code>UNITY_BUILD</code> target property to false if it is known that enabling unity builds for the target can lead to problems.</dl>
<h3>ODR (One definition rule) errors</h3>
<p>When multiple source files are included into one source file, as is done for unity builds, it can potentially lead to ODR errors. CMake provides a number of measures to help address such problems:</p>
<dl>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd>Any source file that has a non-empty <code>COMPILE_OPTIONS</code>, <code>COMPILE_DEFINITIONS</code>, <code>COMPILE_FLAGS</code>, or <code>INCLUDE_DIRECTORIES</code> source property will not be combined into a unity source.</p>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd>Projects can prevent an individual source file from being combined into a unity source by setting its <code>SKIP_UNITY_BUILD_INCLUSION</code> source property to true. This can be a more effective way to prevent problems with specific files than disabling unity builds for an entire target.</p>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd>Projects can set <code>UNITY_BUILD_UNIQUE_ID</code> to cause a valid C-identifier to be generated which is unique per file in a unity build. This can be used to avoid problems with anonymous namespaces in unity builds.</p>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd>The <code>UNITY_BUILD_CODE_BEFORE_INCLUDE</code> and <code>UNITY_BUILD_CODE_AFTER_INCLUDE</code> target properties can be used to inject code into the unity source files before and after every <code>#include</code> statement.</p>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd>The order of source files added to the target via commands like <code>add_library()</code>, <code>add_executable()</code> or <code>target_sources()</code> will be preserved in the generated unity source files. This can be used to manually enforce a specific grouping based on the <code>UNITY_BUILD_BATCH_SIZE</code> target property.</dl>
<h3>Unity_Build_Batch_Size</h3>
<p>New in version 3.16. </p>
<p> Specifies the maximum number of source files that can be combined into any one unity source file when unity builds are enabled by the <code>UNITY_BUILD</code> target property. The original source files will be distributed across as many unity source files as necessary to honor this limit. </p>
<p> The initial value for this property is taken from the <code>CMAKE_UNITY_BUILD_BATCH_SIZE</code> variable when the target is created. If that variable has not been set, the initial value will be 8. </p>
<p> The batch size needs to be selected carefully. If set too high, the size of the combined source files could result in the compiler using excessive memory or hitting other similar limits. In extreme cases, this can even result in build failure. On the other hand, if the batch size is too low, there will be little gain in build performance. </p>
<p> Although strongly discouraged, the batch size may be set to a value of 0 to combine all the sources for the target into a single unity file, regardless of how many sources are involved. This runs the risk of creating an excessively large unity source file and negatively impacting the build performance, so a value of 0 is not generally recommended.</p>
<h3>Unity_Build_Code_After_Include</h3>
<p>New in version 3.16. </p>
<p> Code snippet which is included verbatim by the <code>UNITY_BUILD</code> feature just after every <code>#include</code> statement in the generated unity source files. For example: </p>
<pre>set(after [[
#if defined(NOMINMAX)
#undef NOMINMAX
#endif
]])
set_target_properties(myTarget PROPERTIES
 UNITY_BUILD_CODE_AFTER_INCLUDE &quot;${after}&quot;
)</pre>
<p>See also <code>UNITY_BUILD_CODE_BEFORE_INCLUDE</code>.</p>
<h3>Unity_Build_Code_Before_Include</h3>
<p>New in version 3.16. </p>
<p> Code snippet which is included verbatim by the <code>UNITY_BUILD</code> feature just before every <code>#include</code> statement in the generated unity source files. For example: </p>
<pre>set(before [[
#if !defined(NOMINMAX)
#define NOMINMAX
#endif
]])
set_target_properties(myTarget PROPERTIES
 UNITY_BUILD_CODE_BEFORE_INCLUDE &quot;${before}&quot;
)</pre>
<p>See also <code>UNITY_BUILD_CODE_AFTER_INCLUDE</code>.</p>
<h3>Unity_Build_Mode</h3>
<p>New in version 3.18. </p>
<p> CMake provides different algorithms for selecting which sources are grouped together into a <i>bucket</i>. Selection is decided by this property, which has the following acceptable values:</p>
<dl>
<dt><code>BATCH</code></p>
<dd>When in this mode CMake determines which files are grouped together. The <code>UNITY_BUILD_BATCH_SIZE</code> property controls the upper limit on how many sources can be combined per unity source file. </p>
<p> Example usage: </p>
<pre>add_library(example_library
 source1.cxx
 source2.cxx
 source3.cxx
 source4.cxx)
set_target_properties(example_library PROPERTIES
 UNITY_BUILD_MODE BATCH
 UNITY_BUILD_BATCH_SIZE 2
 )</pre>
<dt><code>GROUP</code></p>
<dd>When in this mode each target explicitly specifies how to group source files. Each source file that has the same <code>UNITY_GROUP</code> value will be grouped together. Any sources that don&#8217;t have this property will be compiled individually. The <code>UNITY_BUILD_BATCH_SIZE</code> property is ignored when using this mode. </p>
<p> Example usage: </p>
<pre>add_library(example_library
 source1.cxx
 source2.cxx
 source3.cxx
 source4.cxx)
set_target_properties(example_library PROPERTIES
 UNITY_BUILD_MODE GROUP
 )
set_source_files_properties(source1.cxx source2.cxx source3.cxx
 PROPERTIES UNITY_GROUP &quot;bucket1&quot;
 )
set_source_files_properties(source4.cxx
 PROPERTIES UNITY_GROUP &quot;bucket2&quot;
 )</pre>
<p>If no explicit <i>UNITY_BUILD_MODE</i> has been specified, CMake will default to <code>BATCH</code>.</dl>
<h3>Unity_Build_Unique_Id</h3>
<p>New in version 3.20. </p>
<p> The name of a valid C-identifier which is set to a unique per-file value during unity builds. </p>
<p> When this property is populated and when <code>UNITY_BUILD</code> is true, the property value is used to define a compiler definition of the specified name. The value of the defined symbol is unspecified, but it is unique per file path. </p>
<p> Given: </p>
<pre>set_target_properties(myTarget PROPERTIES
 UNITY_BUILD &quot;ON&quot;
 UNITY_BUILD_UNIQUE_ID &quot;MY_UNITY_ID&quot;
)</pre>
<p>the <code>MY_UNITY_ID</code> symbol is defined to a unique per-file value. </p>
<p> One known use case for this identifier is to disambiguate the variables in an anonymous namespace in a limited scope. Anonymous namespaces present a problem for unity builds because they are used to ensure that certain variables and declarations are scoped to a translation unit which is approximated by a single source file. When source files are combined in a unity build file, those variables in different files are combined in a single translation unit and the names clash. This property can be used to avoid that with code like the following: </p>
<pre>// Needed for when unity builds are disabled
#ifndef MY_UNITY_ID
#define MY_UNITY_ID
#endif
namespace { namespace MY_UNITY_ID {
 // The name 'i' clashes (or could clash) with other
 // variables in other anonymous namespaces
 int i = 42;
}}
int use_var()
{
 return MY_UNITY_ID::i;
}</pre>
<p>The pseudonymous namespace is used within a truly anonymous namespace. On many platforms, this maintains the invariant that the symbols within do not get external linkage when performing a unity build.</p>
<h3>Version</h3>
<p>What version number is this target. </p>
<p> For shared libraries <code>VERSION</code> and <code>SOVERSION</code> can be used to specify the build version and API version respectively. When building or installing appropriate symlinks are created if the platform supports symlinks and the linker supports so-names. If only one of both is specified the missing is assumed to have the same version number. For executables <code>VERSION</code> can be used to specify the build version. When building or installing appropriate symlinks are created if the platform supports symlinks.</p>
<h3>Windows Versions</h3>
<p>For shared libraries and executables on Windows the <code>VERSION</code> attribute is parsed to extract a <code>&lt;major&gt;.&lt;minor&gt;</code> version number. These numbers are used as the image version of the binary.</p>
<h3>Mach-O Versions</h3>
<p>For shared libraries and executables on Mach-O systems (e.g. macOS, iOS), the <code>SOVERSION</code> property corresponds to the <i>compatibility version</i> and <code>VERSION</code> corresponds to the <i>current version</i> (unless Mach-O specific overrides are provided, as discussed below). See the <code>FRAMEWORK</code> target property for an example. </p>
<p> For shared libraries, the <code>MACHO_COMPATIBILITY_VERSION</code> and <code>MACHO_CURRENT_VERSION</code> properties can be used to override the <i>compatibility version</i> and <i>current version</i> respectively. Note that <code>SOVERSION</code> will still be used to form the <code>install_name</code> and both <code>SOVERSION</code> and <code>VERSION</code> may also affect the file and symlink names. </p>
<p> Versions of Mach-O binaries may be checked with the <code>otool -L &lt;binary&gt;</code> command.</p>
<h3>Visibility_Inlines_Hidden</h3>
<p>Whether to add a compile flag to hide symbols of inline functions </p>
<p> The <code>VISIBILITY_INLINES_HIDDEN</code> property determines whether a flag for hiding symbols for inline functions, such as <code>-fvisibility-inlines-hidden</code>, should be used when invoking the compiler. This property affects compilation in sources of all types of targets (subject to policy <code>CMP0063</code>). </p>
<p> This property is initialized by the value of the <code>CMAKE_VISIBILITY_INLINES_HIDDEN</code> variable if it is set when a target is created.</p>
<h3>Vs_Configuration_Type</h3>
<p>New in version 3.6. </p>
<p> Visual Studio project configuration type. </p>
<p> Sets the <code>ConfigurationType</code> attribute for a generated Visual Studio project. The property value may use <code>generator expressions</code>. If this property is set, it overrides the default setting that is based on the target type (e.g. <code>StaticLibrary</code>, <code>Application</code>, &#8230;). </p>
<p> Supported on Visual Studio Generators for VS 2010 and higher.</p>
<h3>Vs_Debugger_Command</h3>
<p>New in version 3.12. </p>
<p> Sets the local debugger command for Visual Studio C++ targets. The property value may use <code>generator expressions</code>. This is defined in <code>&lt;LocalDebuggerCommand&gt;</code> in the Visual Studio project file. </p>
<p> This property only works for Visual Studio 2010 and above; it is ignored on other generators.</p>
<h3>Vs_Debugger_Command_Arguments</h3>
<p>New in version 3.13. </p>
<p> Sets the local debugger command line arguments for Visual Studio C++ targets. The property value may use <code>generator expressions</code>. This is defined in <code>&lt;LocalDebuggerCommandArguments&gt;</code> in the Visual Studio project file. </p>
<p> This property only works for Visual Studio 2010 and above; it is ignored on other generators.</p>
<h3>Vs_Debugger_Environment</h3>
<p>New in version 3.13. </p>
<p> Sets the local debugger environment for Visual Studio C++ targets. The property value may use <code>generator expressions</code>. This is defined in <code>&lt;LocalDebuggerEnvironment&gt;</code> in the Visual Studio project file. </p>
<p> This property only works for Visual Studio 2010 and above; it is ignored on other generators.</p>
<h3>Vs_Debugger_Working_Directory</h3>
<p>New in version 3.8. </p>
<p> Sets the local debugger working directory for Visual Studio C++ targets. The property value may use <code>generator expressions</code>. This is defined in <code>&lt;LocalDebuggerWorkingDirectory&gt;</code> in the Visual Studio project file. </p>
<p> This property only works for Visual Studio 2010 and above; it is ignored on other generators.</p>
<h3>Vs_Desktop_Extensions_Version</h3>
<p>New in version 3.4. </p>
<p> Visual Studio Windows 10 Desktop Extensions Version </p>
<p> Specifies the version of the Desktop Extensions that should be included in the target. For example <code>10.0.10240.0</code>. If the value is not specified, the Desktop Extensions will not be included. To use the same version of the extensions as the Windows 10 SDK that is being used, you can use the <code>CMAKE_VS_WINDOWS_TARGET_PLATFORM_VERSION</code> variable.</p>
<h3>Vs_Dotnet_Documentation_File</h3>
<p>New in version 3.17. </p>
<p> Visual Studio managed project .NET documentation output </p>
<p> Sets the target XML documentation file output.</p>
<h3>VS_DOTNET_REFERENCE_&lt;refname&gt;</h3>
<p>New in version 3.8. </p>
<p> Visual Studio managed project .NET reference with name <code>&lt;refname&gt;</code> and hint path. </p>
<p> Adds one .NET reference to generated Visual Studio project. The reference will have the name <code>&lt;refname&gt;</code> and will point to the assembly given as value of the property. </p>
<p> See also <code>VS_DOTNET_REFERENCES</code> and <code>VS_DOTNET_REFERENCES_COPY_LOCAL</code></p>
<h3>VS_DOTNET_REFERENCEPROP_&lt;refname&gt;_TAG_&lt;tagname&gt;</h3>
<p>New in version 3.10. </p>
<p> Defines an XML property <code>&lt;tagname&gt;</code> for a .NET reference <code>&lt;refname&gt;</code>. </p>
<p> Reference properties can be set for .NET references which are defined by the target properties <code>VS_DOTNET_REFERENCES</code>, <code>VS_DOTNET_REFERENCE_&lt;refname&gt;</code> and also for project references to other C# targets which are established by <code>target_link_libraries()</code>. </p>
<p> This property is only applicable to C# targets and Visual Studio generators 2010 and later.</p>
<h3>Vs_Dotnet_References</h3>
<p>Visual Studio managed project .NET references </p>
<p> Adds one or more semicolon-delimited .NET references to a generated Visual Studio project. For example, &quot;System;System.Windows.Forms&quot;.</p>
<h3>Vs_Dotnet_References_Copy_Local</h3>
<p>New in version 3.8. </p>
<p> Sets the <code>Copy Local</code> property for all .NET hint references in the target </p>
<p> Boolean property to enable/disable copying of .NET hint references to output directory. The default is <code>ON</code>.</p>
<h3>Vs_Dotnet_Target_Framework_Version</h3>
<p>Specify the .NET target framework version. </p>
<p> Used to specify the .NET target framework version for C++/CLI. For example, &quot;v4.5&quot;. </p>
<p> This property is deprecated and should not be used anymore. Use <code>DOTNET_TARGET_FRAMEWORK</code> or <code>DOTNET_TARGET_FRAMEWORK_VERSION</code> instead.</p>
<h3>Vs_Dpi_Aware</h3>
<p>New in version 3.16. </p>
<p> Set the Manifest Tool -&gt; Input and Output -&gt; DPI Awareness in the Visual Studio target project properties. </p>
<p> Valid values are <code>PerMonitor</code>, <code>ON</code>, or <code>OFF</code>. </p>
<p> For example: </p>
<pre>add_executable(myproject myproject.cpp)
set_property(TARGET myproject PROPERTY VS_DPI_AWARE &quot;PerMonitor&quot;)</pre>
<h3>Vs_Global_Keyword</h3>
<p>Visual Studio project keyword for VS 10 (2010) and newer. </p>
<p> Sets the &quot;keyword&quot; attribute for a generated Visual Studio project. Defaults to &quot;Win32Proj&quot;. You may wish to override this value with &quot;ManagedCProj&quot;, for example, in a Visual Studio managed C++ unit test project. </p>
<p> Use the <code>VS_KEYWORD</code> target property to set the keyword for Visual Studio 9 (2008) and older.</p>
<h3>Vs_Global_Project_Types</h3>
<p>Visual Studio project type(s). </p>
<p> Can be set to one or more UUIDs recognized by Visual Studio to indicate the type of project. This value is copied verbatim into the generated project file. Example for a managed C++ unit testing project: </p>
<pre>{3AC096D0-A1C2-E12C-1390-A8335801FDAB};{8BC9CEB8-8B4A-11D0-8D11-00A0C91BC942}</pre>
<p>UUIDs are semicolon-delimited.</p>
<h3>Vs_Global_Rootnamespace</h3>
<p>Visual Studio project root namespace. </p>
<p> Sets the &quot;RootNamespace&quot; attribute for a generated Visual Studio project. The attribute will be generated only if this is set.</p>
<h3>VS_GLOBAL_&lt;variable&gt;</h3>
<p>Visual Studio project-specific global variable. </p>
<p> Tell the Visual Studio generator to set the global variable &#8216;&lt;variable&gt;&#8217; to a given value in the generated Visual Studio project. Ignored on other generators. Qt integration works better if VS_GLOBAL_QtVersion is set to the version FindQt4.cmake found. For example, &quot;4.7.3&quot;</p>
<h3>Vs_Iot_Extensions_Version</h3>
<p>New in version 3.4. </p>
<p> Visual Studio Windows 10 IoT Extensions Version </p>
<p> Specifies the version of the IoT Extensions that should be included in the target. For example <code>10.0.10240.0</code>. If the value is not specified, the IoT Extensions will not be included. To use the same version of the extensions as the Windows 10 SDK that is being used, you can use the <code>CMAKE_VS_WINDOWS_TARGET_PLATFORM_VERSION</code> variable.</p>
<h3>Vs_Iot_Startup_Task</h3>
<p>New in version 3.4. </p>
<p> Visual Studio Windows 10 IoT Continuous Background Task </p>
<p> Specifies that the target should be compiled as a Continuous Background Task library.</p>
<h3>Vs_Just_My_Code_Debugging</h3>
<p>New in version 3.15. </p>
<p> Enable Just My Code with Visual Studio debugger. </p>
<p> Supported on Visual Studio Generators for VS 2010 and higher, Makefile Generators and the <code>Ninja</code> generators. </p>
<p> This property is initialized by the <code>CMAKE_VS_JUST_MY_CODE_DEBUGGING</code> variable if it is set when a target is created.</p>
<h3>Vs_Keyword</h3>
<p>Visual Studio project keyword for VS 9 (2008) and older. </p>
<p> Can be set to change the visual studio keyword, for example Qt integration works better if this is set to Qt4VSv1.0. </p>
<p> Use the <code>VS_GLOBAL_KEYWORD</code> target property to set the keyword for Visual Studio 10 (2010) and newer.</p>
<h3>Vs_Mobile_Extensions_Version</h3>
<p>New in version 3.4. </p>
<p> Visual Studio Windows 10 Mobile Extensions Version </p>
<p> Specifies the version of the Mobile Extensions that should be included in the target. For example <code>10.0.10240.0</code>. If the value is not specified, the Mobile Extensions will not be included. To use the same version of the extensions as the Windows 10 SDK that is being used, you can use the <code>CMAKE_VS_WINDOWS_TARGET_PLATFORM_VERSION</code> variable.</p>
<h3>Vs_No_Solution_Deploy</h3>
<p>New in version 3.15. </p>
<p> Specify that the target should not be marked for deployment to a Windows CE or Windows Phone device in the generated Visual Studio solution. </p>
<p> Be default, all EXE and shared library (DLL) targets are marked to deploy to the target device in the generated Visual Studio solution. </p>
<p> Generator expressions are supported. </p>
<p> There are reasons one might want to exclude a target / generated project from deployment:</p>
<dl>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd>The library or executable may not be necessary in the primary deploy/debug scenario, and excluding from deployment saves time in the develop/download/debug cycle.</p>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd>There may be insufficient space on the target device to accommodate all of the build products.</p>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd>Visual Studio 2013 requires a target device IP address be entered for each target marked for deployment. For large numbers of targets, this can be tedious. NOTE: Visual Studio <i>will</i> deploy all project dependencies of a project tagged for deployment to the IP address configured for that project even if those dependencies are not tagged for deployment.</dl>
<h3>Example 1</h3>
<p>This shows setting the variable for the target foo. </p>
<pre>add_library(foo SHARED foo.cpp)
set_property(TARGET foo PROPERTY VS_NO_SOLUTION_DEPLOY ON)</pre>
<h3>Example 2</h3>
<p>This shows setting the variable for the Release configuration only. </p>
<pre>add_library(foo SHARED foo.cpp)
set_property(TARGET foo PROPERTY VS_NO_SOLUTION_DEPLOY &quot;$&lt;CONFIG:Release&gt;&quot;)</pre>
<h3>Vs_Package_References</h3>
<p>New in version 3.15. </p>
<p> Visual Studio package references for nuget. </p>
<p> Adds one or more semicolon-delimited package references to a generated Visual Studio project. The version of the package will be underscore delimited. For example, <code>boost_1.7.0;nunit_3.12.*</code>. </p>
<pre>set_property(TARGET ${TARGET_NAME} PROPERTY
 VS_PACKAGE_REFERENCES &quot;boost_1.7.0&quot;)</pre>
<h3>Vs_Platform_Toolset</h3>
<p>New in version 3.18. </p>
<p> Overrides the platform toolset used to build a target. </p>
<p> Only supported when the compiler used by the given toolset is the same as the compiler used to build the whole source tree. </p>
<p> This is especially useful to create driver projects with the toolsets &quot;WindowsUserModeDriver10.0&quot; or &quot;WindowsKernelModeDriver10.0&quot;.</p>
<h3>Vs_Project_Import</h3>
<p>New in version 3.15. </p>
<p> Visual Studio managed project imports </p>
<p> Adds to a generated Visual Studio project one or more semicolon-delimited paths to .props files needed when building projects from some NuGet packages. For example, <code>my_packages_path/MyPackage.1.0.0/build/MyPackage.props</code>.</p>
<h3>Vs_Scc_Auxpath</h3>
<p>Visual Studio Source Code Control Aux Path. </p>
<p> Can be set to change the visual studio source code control auxpath property.</p>
<h3>Vs_Scc_Localpath</h3>
<p>Visual Studio Source Code Control Local Path. </p>
<p> Can be set to change the visual studio source code control local path property.</p>
<h3>Vs_Scc_Projectname</h3>
<p>Visual Studio Source Code Control Project. </p>
<p> Can be set to change the visual studio source code control project name property.</p>
<h3>Vs_Scc_Provider</h3>
<p>Visual Studio Source Code Control Provider. </p>
<p> Can be set to change the visual studio source code control provider property.</p>
<h3>Vs_Sdk_References</h3>
<p>New in version 3.7. </p>
<p> Visual Studio project SDK references. Specify a semicolon-separated list of SDK references to be added to a generated Visual Studio project, e.g. <code>Microsoft.AdMediatorWindows81, Version=1.0</code>.</p>
<h3>Vs_Solution_Deploy</h3>
<p>New in version 3.18. </p>
<p> Specify that the target should be marked for deployment when not targeting Windows CE, Windows Phone or a Windows Store application. </p>
<p> If the target platform doesn&#8217;t support deployment, this property won&#8217;t have any effect. </p>
<p> <code>Generator expressions</code> are supported.</p>
<h3>Examples</h3>
<p>Always deploy target <code>foo</code>: </p>
<pre>add_executable(foo SHARED foo.cpp)
set_property(TARGET foo PROPERTY VS_SOLUTION_DEPLOY ON)</pre>
<p>Deploy target <code>foo</code> for all configurations except <code>Release</code>: </p>
<pre>add_executable(foo SHARED foo.cpp)
set_property(TARGET foo PROPERTY VS_SOLUTION_DEPLOY &quot;$&lt;NOT:$&lt;CONFIG:Release&gt;&gt;&quot;)</pre>
<h3>VS_SOURCE_SETTINGS_&lt;tool&gt;</h3>
<p>New in version 3.18. </p>
<p> Set any item metadata on all non-built files that use &lt;tool&gt;. </p>
<p> Takes a list of <code>Key=Value</code> pairs. Tells the Visual Studio generator to set <code>Key</code> to <code>Value</code> as item metadata on all non-built files that use <code>&lt;tool&gt;</code>. </p>
<p> For example: </p>
<pre>set_property(TARGET main PROPERTY VS_SOURCE_SETTINGS_FXCompile &quot;Key=Value&quot; &quot;Key2=Value2&quot;)</pre>
<p>will set <code>Key</code> to <code>Value</code> and <code>Key2</code> to <code>Value2</code> for all non-built files that use <code>FXCompile</code>. </p>
<p> <code>Generator expressions</code> are supported.</p>
<h3>Vs_User_Props</h3>
<p>New in version 3.8. </p>
<p> Sets the user props file to be included in the visual studio C++ project file. The standard path is <code>$(UserRootDir)\Microsoft.Cpp.$(Platform).user.props</code>, which is in most cases the same as <code>%LOCALAPPDATA%\Microsoft\MSBuild\v4.0\Microsoft.Cpp.Win32.user.props</code> or <code>%LOCALAPPDATA%\Microsoft\MSBuild\v4.0\Microsoft.Cpp.x64.user.props</code>. </p>
<p> The <code>*.user.props</code> files can be used for Visual Studio wide configuration which is independent from cmake.</p>
<h3>Vs_Windows_Target_Platform_Min_Version</h3>
<p>New in version 3.4. </p>
<p> Visual Studio Windows Target Platform Minimum Version </p>
<p> For Windows 10. Specifies the minimum version of the OS that is being targeted. For example <code>10.0.10240.0</code>. If the value is not specified, the value of <code>CMAKE_VS_WINDOWS_TARGET_PLATFORM_VERSION</code> will be used on WindowsStore projects otherwise the target platform minimum version will not be specified for the project.</p>
<h3>Vs_Winrt_Component</h3>
<p>New in version 3.1. </p>
<p> Mark a target as a Windows Runtime component for the Visual Studio generator. Compile the target with <code>C++/CX</code> language extensions for Windows Runtime. For <code>SHARED</code> and <code>MODULE</code> libraries, this also defines the <code>_WINRT_DLL</code> preprocessor macro. </p>
<p> <code>NOTE:</code> Currently this is implemented only by Visual Studio generators. Support may be added to other generators in the future.</p>
<h3>Vs_Winrt_Extensions</h3>
<p>Deprecated. Use <code>VS_WINRT_COMPONENT</code> instead. This property was an experimental partial implementation of that one.</p>
<h3>Vs_Winrt_References</h3>
<p>Visual Studio project Windows Runtime Metadata references </p>
<p> Adds one or more semicolon-delimited WinRT references to a generated Visual Studio project. For example, &quot;Windows;Windows.UI.Core&quot;.</p>
<h3>Win32_Executable</h3>
<p>Build an executable with a WinMain entry point on windows. </p>
<p> When this property is set to true the executable when linked on Windows will be created with a WinMain() entry point instead of just main(). This makes it a GUI executable instead of a console application. See the <code>CMAKE_MFC_FLAG</code> variable documentation to configure use of the Microsoft Foundation Classes (MFC) for WinMain executables. This property is initialized by the value of the <code>CMAKE_WIN32_EXECUTABLE</code> variable if it is set when a target is created. </p>
<p> This property supports <code>generator expressions</code>, except if the target is managed (contains C# code.)</p>
<h3>Windows_Export_All_Symbols</h3>
<p>New in version 3.4. </p>
<p> This property is implemented only for MS-compatible tools on Windows. </p>
<p> Enable this boolean property to automatically create a module definition (<code>.def</code>) file with all global symbols found in the input <code>.obj</code> files for a <code>SHARED</code> library (or executable with <code>ENABLE_EXPORTS</code>) on Windows. The module definition file will be passed to the linker causing all symbols to be exported from the <code>.dll</code>. For global <i>data</i> symbols, <code>__declspec(dllimport)</code> must still be used when compiling against the code in the <code>.dll</code>. All other function symbols will be automatically exported and imported by callers. This simplifies porting projects to Windows by reducing the need for explicit <code>dllexport</code> markup, even in <code>C++</code> classes. </p>
<p> When this property is enabled, zero or more <code>.def</code> files may also be specified as source files of the target. The exports named by these files will be merged with those detected from the object files to generate a single module definition file to be passed to the linker. This can be used to export symbols from a <code>.dll</code> that are not in any of its object files but are added by the linker from dependencies (e.g. <code>msvcrt.lib</code>). </p>
<p> This property is initialized by the value of the <code>CMAKE_WINDOWS_EXPORT_ALL_SYMBOLS</code> variable if it is set when a target is created.</p>
<h3>XCODE_ATTRIBUTE_&lt;an-attribute&gt;</h3>
<p>Set Xcode target attributes directly. </p>
<p> Tell the <code>Xcode</code> generator to set <code>&lt;an-attribute&gt;</code> to a given value in the generated Xcode project. Ignored on other generators. </p>
<p> This offers low-level control over the generated Xcode project file. It is meant as a last resort for specifying settings that CMake does not otherwise have a way to control. Although this can override a setting CMake normally produces on its own, doing so bypasses CMake&#8217;s model of the project and can break things. </p>
<p> See the <code>CMAKE_XCODE_ATTRIBUTE_&lt;an-attribute&gt;</code> variable to set attributes on all targets in a directory tree. </p>
<p> Contents of <code>XCODE_ATTRIBUTE_&lt;an-attribute&gt;</code> may use &quot;generator expressions&quot; with the syntax <code>$&lt;&#8230;&gt;</code>. See the <code><a href="/docs/linux/man/7-cmake-generator-expressions/">cmake-generator-expressions</a>(7)</code> manual for available expressions. See the <code><a href="/docs/linux/man/7-cmake-buildsystem/">cmake-buildsystem</a>(7)</code> manual for more on defining buildsystem properties.</p>
<h3>Xcode_Embed_Frameworks_Code_Sign_On_Copy</h3>
<p>New in version 3.20. </p>
<p> Tell the <code>Xcode</code> generator to perform code signing for all the frameworks and libraries that are embedded using the <code>XCODE_EMBED_FRAMEWORKS</code> property. </p>
<p> New in version 3.21. </p>
<p> This property was generalized to other types of embedded items. See <code>XCODE_EMBED_&lt;type&gt;_CODE_SIGN_ON_COPY</code> for the more general form.</p>
<h3>Xcode_Embed_Frameworks_Remove_Headers_On_Copy</h3>
<p>New in version 3.20. </p>
<p> Tell the <code>Xcode</code> generator to remove headers from all the frameworks that are embedded using the <code>XCODE_EMBED_FRAMEWORKS</code> property. </p>
<p> New in version 3.21. </p>
<p> This property was generalized to other types of embedded items. See <code>XCODE_EMBED_&lt;type&gt;_REMOVE_HEADERS_ON_COPY</code> for the more general form.</p>
<h3>XCODE_EMBED_&lt;type&gt;</h3>
<p>New in version 3.20. </p>
<p> Tell the <code>Xcode</code> generator to embed the specified list of items into the target bundle. <code>&lt;type&gt;</code> specifies the embed build phase to use. See the Xcode documentation for the base location of each <code>&lt;type&gt;</code>. </p>
<p> The supported values for <code>&lt;type&gt;</code> are:</p>
<dl>
<dt><code>FRAMEWORKS</code></p>
<dd>The specified items will be added to the <code>Embed Frameworks</code> build phase. The items can be CMake target names or paths to frameworks or libraries.</p>
<dt><code>APP_EXTENSIONS</code></p>
<dd>New in version 3.21. </p>
<p> The specified items will be added to the <code>Embed App Extensions</code> build phase. They must be CMake target names. </p>
<p> See also <code>XCODE_EMBED_&lt;type&gt;_PATH</code>, <code>XCODE_EMBED_&lt;type&gt;_REMOVE_HEADERS_ON_COPY</code> and <code>XCODE_EMBED_&lt;type&gt;_CODE_SIGN_ON_COPY</code>.</dl>
<h3>XCODE_EMBED_&lt;type&gt;_CODE_SIGN_ON_COPY</h3>
<p>New in version 3.20. </p>
<p> Boolean property used only by the <code>Xcode</code> generator. It specifies whether to perform code signing for the items that are embedded using the <code>XCODE_EMBED_&lt;type&gt;</code> property. </p>
<p> The supported values for <code>&lt;type&gt;</code> are: </p>
<p> <code>FRAMEWORKS</code></p>
<dl>
<dt><code>APP_EXTENSIONS</code></p>
<dd>New in version 3.21. </p>
<p> If a <code>XCODE_EMBED_&lt;type&gt;_CODE_SIGN_ON_COPY</code> property is not defined on the target, no code signing on copy will be performed for that <code>&lt;type&gt;</code>.</dl>
<h3>XCODE_EMBED_&lt;type&gt;_PATH</h3>
<p>New in version 3.20. </p>
<p> This property is used only by the <code>Xcode</code> generator. When defined, it specifies the relative path to use when embedding the items specified by <code>XCODE_EMBED_&lt;type&gt;</code>. The path is relative to the base location of the <code>Embed XXX</code> build phase associated with <code>&lt;type&gt;</code>. See the Xcode documentation for the base location of each <code>&lt;type&gt;</code>. </p>
<p> The supported values for <code>&lt;type&gt;</code> are: </p>
<p> <code>FRAMEWORKS</code></p>
<dl>
<dt><code>APP_EXTENSIONS</code></p>
<dd>New in version 3.21. </p>
</dl>
<h3>XCODE_EMBED_&lt;type&gt;_REMOVE_HEADERS_ON_COPY</h3>
<p>New in version 3.20. </p>
<p> Boolean property used only by the <code>Xcode</code> generator. It specifies whether to remove headers from all the frameworks that are embedded using the <code>XCODE_EMBED_&lt;type&gt;</code> property. </p>
<p> The supported values for <code>&lt;type&gt;</code> are:</p>
<dl>
<dt><code>FRAMEWORKS</code></p>
<dd>If the <code>XCODE_EMBED_FRAMEWORKS_REMOVE_HEADERS_ON_COPY</code> property is not defined, headers will not be removed on copy by default.</p>
<dt><code>APP_EXTENSIONS</code></p>
<dd>New in version 3.21. </p>
<p> If the <code>XCODE_EMBED_APP_EXTENSIONS_REMOVE_HEADERS_ON_COPY</code> property is not defined, headers WILL be removed on copy by default.</dl>
<h3>Xcode_Explicit_File_Type</h3>
<p>New in version 3.8. </p>
<p> Set the Xcode <code>explicitFileType</code> attribute on its reference to a target. CMake computes a default based on target type but can be told explicitly with this property. </p>
<p> See also <code>XCODE_PRODUCT_TYPE</code>.</p>
<h3>Xcode_Generate_Scheme</h3>
<p>New in version 3.15. </p>
<p> If enabled, the <code>Xcode</code> generator will generate schema files. These are useful to invoke analyze, archive, build-for-testing and test actions from the command line. </p>
<p> This property is initialized by the value of the variable <code>CMAKE_XCODE_GENERATE_SCHEME</code> if it is set when a target is created. </p>
<p> The following target properties overwrite the default of the corresponding settings on the &quot;Diagnostic&quot; tab for each schema file. Each of those is initialized by the respective <code>CMAKE_</code> variable at target creation time.</p>
<dl>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd><code>XCODE_SCHEME_ADDRESS_SANITIZER</code></p>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd><code>XCODE_SCHEME_ADDRESS_SANITIZER_USE_AFTER_RETURN</code></p>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd><code>XCODE_SCHEME_DISABLE_MAIN_THREAD_CHECKER</code></p>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd><code>XCODE_SCHEME_DYNAMIC_LIBRARY_LOADS</code></p>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd><code>XCODE_SCHEME_DYNAMIC_LINKER_API_USAGE</code></p>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd><code>XCODE_SCHEME_GUARD_MALLOC</code></p>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd><code>XCODE_SCHEME_MAIN_THREAD_CHECKER_STOP</code></p>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd><code>XCODE_SCHEME_MALLOC_GUARD_EDGES</code></p>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd><code>XCODE_SCHEME_MALLOC_SCRIBBLE</code></p>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd><code>XCODE_SCHEME_MALLOC_STACK</code></p>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd><code>XCODE_SCHEME_THREAD_SANITIZER</code></p>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd><code>XCODE_SCHEME_THREAD_SANITIZER_STOP</code></p>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd><code>XCODE_SCHEME_UNDEFINED_BEHAVIOUR_SANITIZER</code></p>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd><code>XCODE_SCHEME_UNDEFINED_BEHAVIOUR_SANITIZER_STOP</code></p>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd><code>XCODE_SCHEME_ZOMBIE_OBJECTS</code> </p>
<p> The following target properties will be applied on the &quot;Info&quot;, &quot;Arguments&quot;, and &quot;Options&quot; tab:</p>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd><code>XCODE_SCHEME_ARGUMENTS</code></p>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd><code>XCODE_SCHEME_DEBUG_AS_ROOT</code></p>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd><code>XCODE_SCHEME_DEBUG_DOCUMENT_VERSIONING</code></p>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd><code>XCODE_SCHEME_ENVIRONMENT</code></p>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd><code>XCODE_SCHEME_EXECUTABLE</code></p>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd><code>XCODE_SCHEME_WORKING_DIRECTORY</code></dl>
<h3>Xcode_Link_Build_Phase_Mode</h3>
<p>New in version 3.19. </p>
<p> When using the <code>Xcode</code> generator, libraries to be linked will be specified in the Xcode project file using either the &quot;Link Binary With Libraries&quot; build phase or directly as linker flags. The former allows Xcode to manage build paths, which may be necessary when creating Xcode archives because it may use different build paths to a regular build. </p>
<p> This property controls usage of &quot;Link Binary With Libraries&quot; build phase for a target that is an app bundle, executable, shared library, shared framework or a module library. </p>
<p> Possible values are:</p>
<dl>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd><code>NONE</code> The libraries will be linked by specifying the linker flags directly.</p>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd><code>BUILT_ONLY</code> The &quot;Link Binary With Libraries&quot; build phase will be used to link to another target under the following conditions:</p>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd>The target to be linked to is a regular non-imported, non-interface library target.</p>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd>The output directory of the target being built has not been changed from its default (see <code>RUNTIME_OUTPUT_DIRECTORY</code> and <code>LIBRARY_OUTPUT_DIRECTORY</code>).</p>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd><code>KNOWN_LOCATION</code> The &quot;Link Binary With Libraries&quot; build phase will be used to link to another target under the same conditions as with <code>BUILT_ONLY</code> and also:</p>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd>Imported library targets except those of type <code>UNKNOWN</code>.</p>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd>Any non-target library specified directly with a path. </p>
<p> For all other cases, the libraries will be linked by specifying the linker flags directly. </p>
<p> <code>WARNING:</code> Libraries linked using &quot;Link Binary With Libraries&quot; are linked after the ones linked through regular linker flags. This order should be taken into account when different static libraries contain symbols with the same name, as the former ones will take precedence over the latter. </p>
<p> <code>WARNING:</code> If two or more directories contain libraries with identical file names and some libraries are linked from those directories, the library search path lookup will end up linking libraries from the first directory. This is a known limitation of Xcode. </p>
<p> This property is initialized by the value of the <code>CMAKE_XCODE_LINK_BUILD_PHASE_MODE</code> variable if it is set when a target is created.</dl>
<h3>Xcode_Product_Type</h3>
<p>New in version 3.8. </p>
<p> Set the Xcode <code>productType</code> attribute on its reference to a target. CMake computes a default based on target type but can be told explicitly with this property. </p>
<p> See also <code>XCODE_EXPLICIT_FILE_TYPE</code>.</p>
<h3>Xcode_Scheme_Address_Sanitizer</h3>
<p>New in version 3.13. </p>
<p> Whether to enable <code>Address Sanitizer</code> in the Diagnostics section of the generated Xcode scheme. </p>
<p> This property is initialized by the value of the variable <code>CMAKE_XCODE_SCHEME_ADDRESS_SANITIZER</code> if it is set when a target is created. </p>
<p> Please refer to the <code>XCODE_GENERATE_SCHEME</code> target property documentation to see all Xcode schema related properties.</p>
<h3>Xcode_Scheme_Address_Sanitizer_Use_After_Return</h3>
<p>New in version 3.13. </p>
<p> Whether to enable <code>Detect use of stack after return</code> in the Diagnostics section of the generated Xcode scheme. </p>
<p> This property is initialized by the value of the variable <code>CMAKE_XCODE_SCHEME_ADDRESS_SANITIZER_USE_AFTER_RETURN</code> if it is set when a target is created. </p>
<p> Please refer to the <code>XCODE_GENERATE_SCHEME</code> target property documentation to see all Xcode schema related properties.</p>
<h3>Xcode_Scheme_Arguments</h3>
<p>New in version 3.13. </p>
<p> Specify command line arguments that should be added to the Arguments section of the generated Xcode scheme. </p>
<p> If set to a list of arguments those will be added to the scheme. </p>
<p> Please refer to the <code>XCODE_GENERATE_SCHEME</code> target property documentation to see all Xcode schema related properties.</p>
<h3>Xcode_Scheme_Debug_As_Root</h3>
<p>New in version 3.15. </p>
<p> Whether to debug the target as &#8216;root&#8217;. </p>
<p> Please refer to the <code>XCODE_GENERATE_SCHEME</code> target property documentation to see all Xcode schema related properties.</p>
<h3>Xcode_Scheme_Debug_Document_Versioning</h3>
<p>New in version 3.16. </p>
<p> Whether to enable <code>Allow debugging when using document Versions Browser</code> in the Options section of the generated Xcode scheme. </p>
<p> This property is initialized by the value of the variable <code>CMAKE_XCODE_SCHEME_DEBUG_DOCUMENT_VERSIONING</code> if it is set when a target is created. </p>
<p> Please refer to the <code>XCODE_GENERATE_SCHEME</code> target property documentation to see all Xcode schema related properties.</p>
<h3>Xcode_Scheme_Disable_Main_Thread_Checker</h3>
<p>New in version 3.13. </p>
<p> Whether to disable the <code>Main Thread Checker</code> in the Diagnostics section of the generated Xcode scheme. </p>
<p> This property is initialized by the value of the variable <code>CMAKE_XCODE_SCHEME_DISABLE_MAIN_THREAD_CHECKER</code> if it is set when a target is created. </p>
<p> Please refer to the <code>XCODE_GENERATE_SCHEME</code> target property documentation to see all Xcode schema related properties.</p>
<h3>Xcode_Scheme_Dynamic_Library_Loads</h3>
<p>New in version 3.13. </p>
<p> Whether to enable <code>Dynamic Library Loads</code> in the Diagnostics section of the generated Xcode scheme. </p>
<p> This property is initialized by the value of the variable <code>CMAKE_XCODE_SCHEME_DYNAMIC_LIBRARY_LOADS</code> if it is set when a target is created. </p>
<p> Please refer to the <code>XCODE_GENERATE_SCHEME</code> target property documentation to see all Xcode schema related properties.</p>
<h3>Xcode_Scheme_Dynamic_Linker_Api_Usage</h3>
<p>New in version 3.13. </p>
<p> Whether to enable <code>Dynamic Linker API usage</code> in the Diagnostics section of the generated Xcode scheme. </p>
<p> This property is initialized by the value of the variable <code>CMAKE_XCODE_SCHEME_DYNAMIC_LINKER_API_USAGE</code> if it is set when a target is created. </p>
<p> Please refer to the <code>XCODE_GENERATE_SCHEME</code> target property documentation to see all Xcode schema related properties.</p>
<h3>Xcode_Scheme_Environment</h3>
<p>New in version 3.13. </p>
<p> Specify environment variables that should be added to the Arguments section of the generated Xcode scheme. </p>
<p> If set to a list of environment variables and values of the form <code>MYVAR=value</code> those environment variables will be added to the scheme. </p>
<p> Please refer to the <code>XCODE_GENERATE_SCHEME</code> target property documentation to see all Xcode schema related properties.</p>
<h3>Xcode_Scheme_Executable</h3>
<p>New in version 3.13. </p>
<p> Specify path to executable in the Info section of the generated Xcode scheme. If not set the schema generator will select the current target if it is actually executable. </p>
<p> Please refer to the <code>XCODE_GENERATE_SCHEME</code> target property documentation to see all Xcode schema related properties.</p>
<h3>Xcode_Scheme_Guard_Malloc</h3>
<p>New in version 3.13. </p>
<p> Whether to enable <code>Guard Malloc</code> in the Diagnostics section of the generated Xcode scheme. </p>
<p> This property is initialized by the value of the variable <code>CMAKE_XCODE_SCHEME_GUARD_MALLOC</code> if it is set when a target is created. </p>
<p> Please refer to the <code>XCODE_GENERATE_SCHEME</code> target property documentation to see all Xcode schema related properties.</p>
<h3>Xcode_Scheme_Main_Thread_Checker_Stop</h3>

<p> This property is initialized by the value of the variable <code>CMAKE_XCODE_SCHEME_MAIN_THREAD_CHECKER_STOP</code> if it is set when a target is created. </p>
<p> Please refer to the <code>XCODE_GENERATE_SCHEME</code> target property documentation to see all Xcode schema related properties.</p>
<h3>Xcode_Scheme_Malloc_Guard_Edges</h3>
<p>New in version 3.13. </p>
<p> Whether to enable <code>Malloc Guard Edges</code> in the Diagnostics section of the generated Xcode scheme. </p>
<p> This property is initialized by the value of the variable <code>CMAKE_XCODE_SCHEME_MALLOC_GUARD_EDGES</code> if it is set when a target is created. </p>
<p> Please refer to the <code>XCODE_GENERATE_SCHEME</code> target property documentation to see all Xcode schema related properties.</p>
