<p> Whether to enable <code>Malloc Scribble</code> in the Diagnostics section of the generated Xcode scheme. </p>
<p> This property is initialized by the value of the variable <code>CMAKE_XCODE_SCHEME_MALLOC_SCRIBBLE</code> if it is set when a target is created. </p>

<h3>Xcode_Scheme_Malloc_Stack</h3>

<p> Please refer to the <code>XCODE_GENERATE_SCHEME</code> target property documentation to see all Xcode schema related properties.</p>
<h3>Xcode_Scheme_Thread_Sanitizer</h3>

<p> Whether to enable <code>Thread Sanitizer</code> in the Diagnostics section of the generated Xcode scheme. </p>
<p> This property is initialized by the value of the variable <code>CMAKE_XCODE_SCHEME_THREAD_SANITIZER</code> if it is set when a target is created. </p>

<h3>Xctest</h3>
<p>New in version 3.3. </p>
<p> This target is a XCTest CFBundle on the Mac. </p>
<p> This property will usually get set via the <code>xctest_add_bundle()</code> macro in <code>FindXCTest</code> module. </p>
<p> If a module library target has this property set to true it will be built as a CFBundle when built on the Mac. It will have the directory structure required for a CFBundle. </p>
<p> This property depends on <code>BUNDLE</code> to be effective.</p>
<h2>Properties On Tests</h2>
<h3>Attached_Files</h3>
<p>Attach a list of files to a dashboard submission. </p>
<p> Set this property to a list of files that will be encoded and submitted to the dashboard as an addition to the test result.</p>
<h3>Attached_Files_On_Fail</h3>
<p>Attach a list of files to a dashboard submission if the test fails. </p>
<p> Same as <code>ATTACHED_FILES</code>, but these files will only be included if the test does not pass.</p>
<h3>Cost</h3>
<p>This property describes the cost of a test. When parallel testing is enabled, tests in the test set will be run in descending order of cost. Projects can explicitly define the cost of a test by setting this property to a floating point value. </p>
<p> When the cost of a test is not defined by the project, <code>ctest</code> will initially use a default cost of <code>0</code>. It computes a weighted average of the cost each time a test is run and uses that as an improved estimate of the cost for the next run. The more a test is re-run in the same build directory, the more representative the cost should become.</p>
<h3>Depends</h3>
<p>Specifies that this test should only be run after the specified list of tests. </p>
<p> Set this to a list of tests that must finish before this test is run. The results of those tests are not considered, the dependency relationship is purely for order of execution (i.e. it is really just a <i>run after</i> relationship). Consider using test fixtures with setup tests if a dependency with successful completion is required (see <code>FIXTURES_REQUIRED</code>).</p>
<h3>Examples</h3>
<pre>add_test(NAME baseTest1 ...)
add_test(NAME baseTest2 ...)
add_test(NAME dependsTest12 ...)
set_tests_properties(dependsTest12 PROPERTIES DEPENDS &quot;baseTest1;baseTest2&quot;)
# dependsTest12 runs after baseTest1 and baseTest2, even if they fail</pre>
<h3>Disabled</h3>
<p>New in version 3.9. </p>
<p> If set to <code>True</code>, the test will be skipped and its status will be &#8216;Not Run&#8217;. A <code>DISABLED</code> test will not be counted in the total number of tests and its completion status will be reported to CDash as <code>Disabled</code>. </p>
<p> A <code>DISABLED</code> test does not participate in test fixture dependency resolution. If a <code>DISABLED</code> test has fixture requirements defined in its <code>FIXTURES_REQUIRED</code> property, it will not cause setup or cleanup tests for those fixtures to be added to the test set. </p>
<p> If a test with the <code>FIXTURES_SETUP</code> property set is <code>DISABLED</code>, the fixture behavior will be as though that setup test was passing and any test case requiring that fixture will still run.</p>
<h3>Environment</h3>
<p>Specify environment variables that should be defined for running a test. </p>
<p> If set to a list of environment variables and values of the form <code>MYVAR=value</code> those environment variables will be defined while running the test. The environment changes from this property do not affect other tests.</p>
<h3>Environment_Modification</h3>
<p>New in version 3.22. </p>
<p> Specify environment variables that should be modified for running a test. Note that the operations performed by this property are performed after the <code>ENVIRONMENT</code> property is already applied. </p>
<p> If set to a list of environment variables and values of the form <code>MYVAR=OP:VALUE</code>, where <code>MYVAR</code> is the case-sensitive name of an environment variable to be modified. Entries are considered in the order specified in the property&#8217;s value. The <code>OP</code> may be one of:</p>
<dl>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd><code>reset</code>: Reset to the unmodified value, ignoring all modifications to <code>MYVAR</code> prior to this entry. Note that this will reset the variable to the value set by <code>ENVIRONMENT</code>, if it was set, and otherwise to its state from the rest of the CTest execution.</p>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd><code>set</code>: Replaces the current value of <code>MYVAR</code> with <code>VALUE</code>.</p>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd><code>unset</code>: Unsets the current value of <code>MYVAR</code>.</p>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd><code>string_append</code>: Appends <code>VALUE</code> to the current value of <code>MYVAR</code>.</p>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd><code>string_prepend</code>: Prepends <code>VALUE</code> to the current value of <code>MYVAR</code>.</p>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd><code>path_list_append</code>: Appends <code>VALUE</code> to the current value of <code>MYVAR</code> using the host platform&#8217;s path list separator (<code>;</code> on Windows and <code>:</code> elsewhere).</p>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd><code>path_list_prepend</code>: Prepends <code>VALUE</code> to the current value of <code>MYVAR</code> using the host platform&#8217;s path list separator (<code>;</code> on Windows and <code>:</code> elsewhere).</p>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd><code>cmake_list_append</code>: Appends <code>VALUE</code> to the current value of <code>MYVAR</code> using <code>;</code> as the separator.</p>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd><code>cmake_list_prepend</code>: Prepends <code>VALUE</code> to the current value of <code>MYVAR</code> using <code>;</code> as the separator. </p>
<p> Unrecognized <code>OP</code> values will result in the test failing before it is executed. This is so that future operations may be added without changing valid behavior of existing tests. </p>
<p> The environment changes from this property do not affect other tests.</dl>
<h3>Fail_Regular_Expression</h3>
<p>If the output matches this regular expression the test will fail, regardless of the process exit code. </p>
<p> If set, if the output matches one of specified regular expressions, the test will fail. Example: </p>
<pre>set_tests_properties(mytest PROPERTIES
 FAIL_REGULAR_EXPRESSION &quot;[^a-z]Error;ERROR;Failed&quot;
)</pre>
<p><code>FAIL_REGULAR_EXPRESSION</code> expects a list of regular expressions. </p>
<p> See also the <code>PASS_REGULAR_EXPRESSION</code> and <code>SKIP_REGULAR_EXPRESSION</code> test properties.</p>
<h3>Fixtures_Cleanup</h3>
<p>New in version 3.7. </p>
<p> Specifies a list of fixtures for which the test is to be treated as a cleanup test. These fixture names are distinct from test case names and are not required to have any similarity to the names of tests associated with them. </p>
<p> Fixture cleanup tests are ordinary tests with all of the usual test functionality. Setting the <code>FIXTURES_CLEANUP</code> property for a test has two primary effects:</p>
<dl>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd>CTest will ensure the test executes after all other tests which list any of the fixtures in its <code>FIXTURES_REQUIRED</code> property.</p>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd>If CTest is asked to run only a subset of tests (e.g. using regular expressions or the <code>&#8211;rerun-failed</code> option) and the cleanup test is not in the set of tests to run, it will automatically be added if any tests in the set require any fixture listed in <code>FIXTURES_CLEANUP</code>. </p>
<p> A cleanup test can have multiple fixtures listed in its <code>FIXTURES_CLEANUP</code> property. It will execute only once for the whole CTest run, not once for each fixture. A fixture can also have more than one cleanup test defined. If there are multiple cleanup tests for a fixture, projects can control their order with the usual <code>DEPENDS</code> test property if necessary. </p>
<p> A cleanup test is allowed to require other fixtures, but not any fixture listed in its <code>FIXTURES_CLEANUP</code> property. For example: </p>
<pre># Ok: Dependent fixture is different to cleanup
set_tests_properties(cleanupFoo PROPERTIES
 FIXTURES_CLEANUP Foo
 FIXTURES_REQUIRED Bar
)
# Error: cannot require same fixture as cleanup
set_tests_properties(cleanupFoo PROPERTIES
 FIXTURES_CLEANUP Foo
 FIXTURES_REQUIRED Foo
)</pre>
<p>Cleanup tests will execute even if setup or regular tests for that fixture fail or are skipped. </p>
<p> See <code>FIXTURES_REQUIRED</code> for a more complete discussion of how to use test fixtures.</dl>
<h3>Fixtures_Required</h3>
<p>New in version 3.7. </p>
<p> Specifies a list of fixtures the test requires. Fixture names are case sensitive and they are not required to have any similarity to test names. </p>
<p> Fixtures are a way to attach setup and cleanup tasks to a set of tests. If a test requires a given fixture, then all tests marked as setup tasks for that fixture will be executed first (once for the whole set of tests, not once per test requiring the fixture). After all tests requiring a particular fixture have completed, CTest will ensure all tests marked as cleanup tasks for that fixture are then executed. Tests are marked as setup tasks with the <code>FIXTURES_SETUP</code> property and as cleanup tasks with the <code>FIXTURES_CLEANUP</code> property. If any of a fixture&#8217;s setup tests fail, all tests listing that fixture in their <code>FIXTURES_REQUIRED</code> property will not be executed. The cleanup tests for the fixture will always be executed, even if some setup tests fail. </p>
<p> When CTest is asked to execute only a subset of tests (e.g. by the use of regular expressions or when run with the <code>&#8211;rerun-failed</code> command line option), it will automatically add any setup or cleanup tests for fixtures required by any of the tests that are in the execution set. This behavior can be overridden with the <code>-FS</code>, <code>-FC</code> and <code>-FA</code> command line options to <code><a href="/docs/linux/man/1-ctest/">ctest</a>(1)</code> if desired. </p>
<p> Since setup and cleanup tasks are also tests, they can have an ordering specified by the <code>DEPENDS</code> test property just like any other tests. This can be exploited to implement setup or cleanup using multiple tests for a single fixture to modularise setup or cleanup logic. </p>
<p> The concept of a fixture is different to that of a resource specified by <code>RESOURCE_LOCK</code>, but they may be used together. A fixture defines a set of tests which share setup and cleanup requirements, whereas a resource lock has the effect of ensuring a particular set of tests do not run in parallel. Some situations may need both, such as setting up a database, serializing test access to that database and deleting the database again at the end. For such cases, tests would populate both <code>FIXTURES_REQUIRED</code> and <code>RESOURCE_LOCK</code> to combine the two behaviors. Names used for <code>RESOURCE_LOCK</code> have no relationship with names of fixtures, so note that a resource lock does not imply a fixture and vice versa. </p>
<p> Consider the following example which represents a database test scenario similar to that mentioned above: </p>
<pre>add_test(NAME testsDone COMMAND emailResults)
add_test(NAME fooOnly COMMAND testFoo)
add_test(NAME dbOnly COMMAND testDb)
add_test(NAME dbWithFoo COMMAND testDbWithFoo)
add_test(NAME createDB COMMAND initDB)
add_test(NAME setupUsers COMMAND userCreation)
add_test(NAME cleanupDB COMMAND deleteDB)
add_test(NAME cleanupFoo COMMAND removeFoos)
set_tests_properties(setupUsers PROPERTIES DEPENDS createDB)
set_tests_properties(createDB PROPERTIES FIXTURES_SETUP DB)
set_tests_properties(setupUsers PROPERTIES FIXTURES_SETUP DB)
set_tests_properties(cleanupDB PROPERTIES FIXTURES_CLEANUP DB)
set_tests_properties(cleanupFoo PROPERTIES FIXTURES_CLEANUP Foo)
set_tests_properties(testsDone PROPERTIES FIXTURES_CLEANUP &quot;DB;Foo&quot;)
set_tests_properties(fooOnly PROPERTIES FIXTURES_REQUIRED Foo)
set_tests_properties(dbOnly PROPERTIES FIXTURES_REQUIRED DB)
set_tests_properties(dbWithFoo PROPERTIES FIXTURES_REQUIRED &quot;DB;Foo&quot;)
set_tests_properties(dbOnly dbWithFoo createDB setupUsers cleanupDB
 PROPERTIES RESOURCE_LOCK DbAccess)</pre>
<p>Key points from this example:</p>
<dl>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd>Two fixtures are defined: <code>DB</code> and <code>Foo</code>. Tests can require a single fixture as <code>fooOnly</code> and <code>dbOnly</code> do, or they can depend on multiple fixtures like <code>dbWithFoo</code> does.</p>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd>A <code>DEPENDS</code> relationship is set up to ensure <code>setupUsers</code> happens after <code>createDB</code>, both of which are setup tests for the <code>DB</code> fixture and will therefore be executed before the <code>dbOnly</code> and <code>dbWithFoo</code> tests automatically.</p>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd>No explicit <code>DEPENDS</code> relationships were needed to make the setup tests run before or the cleanup tests run after the regular tests.</p>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd>The <code>Foo</code> fixture has no setup tests defined, only a single cleanup test.</p>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd><code>testsDone</code> is a cleanup test for both the <code>DB</code> and <code>Foo</code> fixtures. Therefore, it will only execute once regular tests for both fixtures have finished (i.e. after <code>fooOnly</code>, <code>dbOnly</code> and <code>dbWithFoo</code>). No <code>DEPENDS</code> relationship was specified for <code>testsDone</code>, so it is free to run before, after or concurrently with other cleanup tests for either fixture.</p>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd>The setup and cleanup tests never list the fixtures they are for in their own <code>FIXTURES_REQUIRED</code> property, as that would result in a dependency on themselves and be considered an error.</dl>
<h3>Fixtures_Setup</h3>
<p>New in version 3.7. </p>
<p> Specifies a list of fixtures for which the test is to be treated as a setup test. These fixture names are distinct from test case names and are not required to have any similarity to the names of tests associated with them. </p>
<p> Fixture setup tests are ordinary tests with all of the usual test functionality. Setting the <code>FIXTURES_SETUP</code> property for a test has two primary effects:</p>
<dl>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd>CTest will ensure the test executes before any other test which lists the fixture name(s) in its <code>FIXTURES_REQUIRED</code> property.</p>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd>If CTest is asked to run only a subset of tests (e.g. using regular expressions or the <code>&#8211;rerun-failed</code> option) and the setup test is not in the set of tests to run, it will automatically be added if any tests in the set require any fixture listed in <code>FIXTURES_SETUP</code>. </p>
<p> A setup test can have multiple fixtures listed in its <code>FIXTURES_SETUP</code> property. It will execute only once for the whole CTest run, not once for each fixture. A fixture can also have more than one setup test defined. If there are multiple setup tests for a fixture, projects can control their order with the usual <code>DEPENDS</code> test property if necessary. </p>
<p> A setup test is allowed to require other fixtures, but not any fixture listed in its <code>FIXTURES_SETUP</code> property. For example: </p>
<pre># Ok: dependent fixture is different to setup
set_tests_properties(setupFoo PROPERTIES
 FIXTURES_SETUP Foo
 FIXTURES_REQUIRED Bar
)
# Error: cannot require same fixture as setup
set_tests_properties(setupFoo PROPERTIES
 FIXTURES_SETUP Foo
 FIXTURES_REQUIRED Foo
)</pre>
<p>If any of a fixture&#8217;s setup tests fail, none of the tests listing that fixture in its <code>FIXTURES_REQUIRED</code> property will be run. Cleanup tests will, however, still be executed. </p>
<p> See <code>FIXTURES_REQUIRED</code> for a more complete discussion of how to use test fixtures.</dl>
<h3>Labels</h3>
<p>Specify a list of text labels associated with a test. The labels are reported in both the <code>ctest</code> output summary and in dashboard submissions. They can also be used to filter the set of tests to be executed (see the <code>ctest -L</code> and <code>ctest -LE</code> CTest Options). </p>
<p> See Additional Labels for adding labels to a test dynamically during test execution.</p>
<h3>Measurement</h3>
<p>Specify a <code>CDASH</code> measurement and value to be reported for a test. </p>
<p> If set to a name then that name will be reported to <code>CDASH</code> as a named measurement with a value of <code>1</code>. You may also specify a value by setting <code>MEASUREMENT</code> to <code>measurement=value</code>.</p>
<h3>Pass_Regular_Expression</h3>
<p>The output must match this regular expression for the test to pass. The process exit code is ignored. </p>
<p> If set, the test output will be checked against the specified regular expressions and at least one of the regular expressions has to match, otherwise the test will fail. Example: </p>
<pre>set_tests_properties(mytest PROPERTIES
 PASS_REGULAR_EXPRESSION &quot;TestPassed;All ok&quot;
)</pre>
<p><code>PASS_REGULAR_EXPRESSION</code> expects a list of regular expressions. </p>
<p> See also the <code>FAIL_REGULAR_EXPRESSION</code> and <code>SKIP_REGULAR_EXPRESSION</code> test properties.</p>
<h3>Processor_Affinity</h3>
<p>New in version 3.12. </p>
<p> Set to a true value to ask CTest to launch the test process with CPU affinity for a fixed set of processors. If enabled and supported for the current platform, CTest will choose a set of processors to place in the CPU affinity mask when launching the test process. The number of processors in the set is determined by the <code>PROCESSORS</code> test property or the number of processors available to CTest, whichever is smaller. The set of processors chosen will be disjoint from the processors assigned to other concurrently running tests that also have the <code>PROCESSOR_AFFINITY</code> property enabled.</p>
<h3>Processors</h3>
<p>Set to specify how many process slots this test requires. If not set, the default is <code>1</code> processor. </p>
<p> Denotes the number of processors that this test will require. This is typically used for MPI tests, and should be used in conjunction with the <code>ctest_test()</code> <code>PARALLEL_LEVEL</code> option. </p>
<p> This will also be used to display a weighted test timing result in label and subproject summaries in the command line output of <code><a href="/docs/linux/man/1-ctest/">ctest</a>(1)</code>. The wall clock time for the test run will be multiplied by this property to give a better idea of how much cpu resource CTest allocated for the test. </p>
<p> See also the <code>PROCESSOR_AFFINITY</code> test property.</p>
<h3>Required_Files</h3>
<p>List of files required to run the test. The filenames are relative to the test <code>WORKING_DIRECTORY</code> unless an absolute path is specified. </p>
<p> If set to a list of files, the test will not be run unless all of the files exist.</p>
<h3>Examples</h3>
<p>Suppose that <code>test.txt</code> is created by test <code>baseTest</code> and <code>none.txt</code> does not exist: </p>
<pre>add_test(NAME baseTest ...) # Assumed to create test.txt
add_test(NAME fileTest ...)
# The following ensures that if baseTest is successful, test.txt will
# have been created before fileTest is run
set_tests_properties(fileTest PROPERTIES
 DEPENDS baseTest
 REQUIRED_FILES test.txt
)
add_test(NAME notRunTest ...)
# The following makes notRunTest depend on two files. Nothing creates
# the none.txt file, so notRunTest will fail with status &quot;Not Run&quot;.
set_tests_properties(notRunTest PROPERTIES
 REQUIRED_FILES &quot;test.txt;none.txt&quot;
)</pre>
<p>The above example demonstrates how <code>REQUIRED_FILES</code> works, but it is not the most robust way to implement test ordering with failure detection. For that, test fixtures are a better alternative (see <code>FIXTURES_REQUIRED</code>).</p>
<h3>Resource_Groups</h3>
<p>New in version 3.16. </p>
<p> Specify resources required by a test, grouped in a way that is meaningful to the test. See resource allocation for more information on how this property integrates into the CTest resource allocation feature. </p>
<p> The <code>RESOURCE_GROUPS</code> property is a semicolon-separated list of group descriptions. Each entry consists of an optional number of groups using the description followed by a series of resource requirements for those groups. These requirements (and the number of groups) are separated by commas. The resource requirements consist of the name of a resource type, followed by a colon, followed by an unsigned integer specifying the number of slots required on one resource of the given type. </p>
<p> The <code>RESOURCE_GROUPS</code> property tells CTest what resources a test expects to use grouped in a way meaningful to the test. The test itself must read the environment variables to determine which resources have been allocated to each group. For example, each group may correspond to a process the test will spawn when executed. </p>
<p> Consider the following example: </p>
<pre>add_test(NAME MyTest COMMAND MyExe)
set_property(TEST MyTest PROPERTY RESOURCE_GROUPS
 &quot;2,gpus:2&quot;
 &quot;gpus:4,crypto_chips:2&quot;)</pre>
<p>In this example, there are two group descriptions (implicitly separated by a semicolon.) The content of the first description is <code>2,gpus:2</code>. This description specifies 2 groups, each of which requires 2 slots from a single GPU. The content of the second description is <code>gpus:4,crypto_chips:2</code>. This description does not specify a group count, so a default of 1 is assumed. This single group requires 4 slots from a single GPU and 2 slots from a single cryptography chip. In total, 3 resource groups are specified for this test, each with its own unique requirements. </p>
<p> Note that the number of slots following the resource type specifies slots from a <i>single</i> instance of the resource. If the resource group can tolerate receiving slots from different instances of the same resource, it can indicate this by splitting the specification into multiple requirements of one slot. For example: </p>
<pre>add_test(NAME MyTest COMMAND MyExe)
set_property(TEST MyTest PROPERTY RESOURCE_GROUPS
 &quot;gpus:1,gpus:1,gpus:1,gpus:1&quot;)</pre>
<p>In this case, the single resource group indicates that it needs four GPU slots, all of which may come from separate GPUs (though they don&#8217;t have to; CTest may still assign slots from the same GPU.) </p>
<p> When CTest sets the environment variables for a test, it assigns a group number based on the group description, starting at 0 on the left and the number of groups minus 1 on the right. For example, in the example above, the two groups in the first description would have IDs of 0 and 1, and the single group in the second description would have an ID of 2. </p>
<p> Both the <code>RESOURCE_GROUPS</code> and <code>RESOURCE_LOCK</code> properties serve similar purposes, but they are distinct and orthogonal. Resources specified by <code>RESOURCE_GROUPS</code> do not affect <code>RESOURCE_LOCK</code>, and vice versa. Whereas <code>RESOURCE_LOCK</code> is a simpler property that is used for locking one global resource, <code>RESOURCE_GROUPS</code> is a more advanced property that allows multiple tests to simultaneously use multiple resources of the same type, specifying their requirements in a fine-grained manner.</p>
<h3>Resource_Lock</h3>
<p>Specify a list of resources that are locked by this test. </p>
<p> If multiple tests specify the same resource lock, they are guaranteed not to run concurrently. </p>
<p> See also <code>FIXTURES_REQUIRED</code> if the resource requires any setup or cleanup steps. </p>
<p> Both the <code>RESOURCE_GROUPS</code> and <code>RESOURCE_LOCK</code> properties serve similar purposes, but they are distinct and orthogonal. Resources specified by <code>RESOURCE_GROUPS</code> do not affect <code>RESOURCE_LOCK</code>, and vice versa. Whereas <code>RESOURCE_LOCK</code> is a simpler property that is used for locking one global resource, <code>RESOURCE_GROUPS</code> is a more advanced property that allows multiple tests to simultaneously use multiple resources of the same type, specifying their requirements in a fine-grained manner.</p>
<h3>Run_Serial</h3>
<p>Do not run this test in parallel with any other test. </p>
<p> Use this option in conjunction with the ctest_test <code>PARALLEL_LEVEL</code> option to specify that this test should not be run in parallel with any other tests.</p>
<h3>Skip_Regular_Expression</h3>
<p>New in version 3.16. </p>
<p> If the output matches this regular expression the test will be marked as skipped. </p>
<p> If set, if the output matches one of specified regular expressions, the test will be marked as skipped. Example: </p>
<pre>set_property(TEST mytest PROPERTY
 SKIP_REGULAR_EXPRESSION &quot;[^a-z]Skip&quot; &quot;SKIP&quot; &quot;Skipped&quot;
)</pre>
<p><code>SKIP_REGULAR_EXPRESSION</code> expects a list of regular expressions. </p>
<p> See also the <code>SKIP_RETURN_CODE</code>, <code>PASS_REGULAR_EXPRESSION</code>, and <code>FAIL_REGULAR_EXPRESSION</code> test properties.</p>
<h3>Skip_Return_Code</h3>
<p>Return code to mark a test as skipped. </p>
<p> Sometimes only a test itself can determine if all requirements for the test are met. If such a situation should not be considered a hard failure a return code of the process can be specified that will mark the test as <code>Not Run</code> if it is encountered. Valid values are in the range of 0 to 255, inclusive. </p>
<p> See also the <code>SKIP_REGULAR_EXPRESSION</code> property.</p>
<h3>Timeout</h3>
<p>How many seconds to allow for this test. </p>
<p> This property if set will limit a test to not take more than the specified number of seconds to run. If it exceeds that the test process will be killed and ctest will move to the next test. This setting takes precedence over <code>CTEST_TEST_TIMEOUT</code>.</p>
<h3>Timeout_After_Match</h3>
<p>New in version 3.6. </p>
<p> Change a test&#8217;s timeout duration after a matching line is encountered in its output.</p>
<h3>Usage</h3>
<pre>add_test(mytest ...)
set_property(TEST mytest PROPERTY TIMEOUT_AFTER_MATCH &quot;${seconds}&quot; &quot;${regex}&quot;)</pre>
<h3>Description</h3>
<p>Allow a test <code>seconds</code> to complete after <code>regex</code> is encountered in its output. </p>
<p> When the test outputs a line that matches <code>regex</code> its start time is reset to the current time and its timeout duration is changed to <code>seconds</code>. Prior to this, the timeout duration is determined by the <code>TIMEOUT</code> property or the <code>CTEST_TEST_TIMEOUT</code> variable if either of these are set. Because the test&#8217;s start time is reset, its execution time will not include any time that was spent waiting for the matching output. </p>
<p> <i>TIMEOUT_AFTER_MATCH</i> is useful for avoiding spurious timeouts when your test must wait for some system resource to become available before it can execute. Set <code>TIMEOUT</code> to a longer duration that accounts for resource acquisition and use <i>TIMEOUT_AFTER_MATCH</i> to control how long the actual test is allowed to run. </p>
<p> If the required resource can be controlled by CTest you should use <code>RESOURCE_LOCK</code> instead of <i>TIMEOUT_AFTER_MATCH</i>. This property should be used when only the test itself can determine when its required resources are available.</p>
<h3>Will_Fail</h3>
<p>If set to true, this will invert the pass/fail flag of the test. </p>
<p> This property can be used for tests that are expected to fail and return a non zero return code.</p>
<h3>Working_Directory</h3>
<p>The directory from which the test executable will be called. </p>
<p> If this is not set, the test will be run with the working directory set to the binary directory associated with where the test was created (i.e. the <code>CMAKE_CURRENT_BINARY_DIR</code> for where <code>add_test()</code> was called).</p>
<h2>Properties On Source Files</h2>
<h3>Abstract</h3>
<p>Is this source file an abstract class. </p>
<p> A property on a source file that indicates if the source file represents a class that is abstract. This only makes sense for languages that have a notion of an abstract class and it is only used by some tools that wrap classes into other languages.</p>
<h3>Autorcc_Options</h3>
<p>Additional options for <code>rcc</code> when using <code>AUTORCC</code> </p>
<p> This property holds additional command line options which will be used when <code>rcc</code> is executed during the build via <code>AUTORCC</code>, i.e. it is equivalent to the optional <code>OPTIONS</code> argument of the <code>qt4_add_resources()</code> macro. </p>
<p> By default it is empty. </p>
<p> The options set on the <code>.qrc</code> source file may override <code>AUTORCC_OPTIONS</code> set on the target.</p>
<h3>Example</h3>
<pre># ...
set_property(SOURCE resources.qrc PROPERTY AUTORCC_OPTIONS &quot;--compress;9&quot;)
# ...</pre>
<h3>Autouic_Options</h3>
<p>Additional options for <code>uic</code> when using <code>AUTOUIC</code> </p>
<p> This property holds additional command line options which will be used when <code>uic</code> is executed during the build via <code>AUTOUIC</code>, i.e. it is equivalent to the optional <code>OPTIONS</code> argument of the <code>qt4_wrap_ui()</code> macro. </p>
<p> By default it is empty. </p>
<p> The options set on the <code>.ui</code> source file may override <code>AUTOUIC_OPTIONS</code> set on the target.</p>
<h3>Example</h3>
<pre># ...
set_property(SOURCE widget.ui PROPERTY AUTOUIC_OPTIONS &quot;--no-protection&quot;)
# ...</pre>
<h3>Compile_Definitions</h3>
<p>Preprocessor definitions for compiling a source file. </p>
<p> The <code>COMPILE_DEFINITIONS</code> property may be set to a semicolon-separated list of preprocessor definitions using the syntax <code>VAR</code> or <code>VAR=value</code>. Function-style definitions are not supported. CMake will automatically escape the value correctly for the native build system (note that CMake language syntax may require escapes to specify some values). This property may be set on a per-configuration basis using the name <code>COMPILE_DEFINITIONS_&lt;CONFIG&gt;</code> where <code>&lt;CONFIG&gt;</code> is an upper-case name (ex. <code>COMPILE_DEFINITIONS_DEBUG</code>). </p>
<p> CMake will automatically drop some definitions that are not supported by the native build tool. Xcode does not support per-configuration definitions on source files. </p>
<p> Disclaimer: Most native build tools have poor support for escaping certain values. CMake has work-arounds for many cases but some values may just not be possible to pass correctly. If a value does not seem to be escaped correctly, do not attempt to work-around the problem by adding escape sequences to the value. Your work-around may break in a future version of CMake that has improved escape support. Instead consider defining the macro in a (configured) header file. Then report the limitation. Known limitations include: </p>
<pre># - broken almost everywhere
; - broken in VS IDE 7.0 and Borland Makefiles
, - broken in VS IDE
% - broken in some cases in NMake
&amp; | - broken in some cases on MinGW
^ &lt; &gt; \&quot; - broken in most Make tools on Windows</pre>
<p>CMake does not reject these values outright because they do work in some cases. Use with caution. </p>
<p> Contents of <code>COMPILE_DEFINITIONS</code> may use <code><a href="/docs/linux/man/7-cmake-generator-expressions/">cmake-generator-expressions</a>(7)</code> with the syntax <code>$&lt;&#8230;&gt;</code>. See the <code><a href="/docs/linux/man/7-cmake-generator-expressions/">cmake-generator-expressions</a>(7)</code> manual for available expressions. However, <code>Xcode</code> does not support per-config per-source settings, so expressions that depend on the build configuration are not allowed with that generator. </p>
<p> Generator expressions should be preferred instead of setting the alternative per-configuration property.</p>
<h3>Compile_Flags</h3>
<p>Additional flags to be added when compiling this source file. </p>
<p> The <code>COMPILE_FLAGS</code> property, managed as a string, sets additional compiler flags used that will be added to the list of compile flags when this source file builds. The flags will be added after target-wide flags (except in some cases not supported by the <code>Visual Studio 9 2008</code> generator). </p>
<p> Use <code>COMPILE_DEFINITIONS</code> to pass additional preprocessor definitions. </p>
<p> Contents of <code>COMPILE_FLAGS</code> may use &quot;generator expressions&quot; with the syntax <code>$&lt;&#8230;&gt;</code>. See the <code><a href="/docs/linux/man/7-cmake-generator-expressions/">cmake-generator-expressions</a>(7)</code> manual for available expressions. However, <code>Xcode</code> does not support per-config per-source settings, so expressions that depend on the build configuration are not allowed with that generator. </p>
<p> <code>NOTE:</code> This property has been superseded by the <code>COMPILE_OPTIONS</code> property.</p>
<h3>Compile_Options</h3>
<p>New in version 3.11. </p>
<p> List of additional options to pass to the compiler. </p>
<p> This property holds a semicolon-separated list of options and will be added to the list of compile flags when this source file builds. The options will be added after target-wide options (except in some cases not supported by the <code>Visual Studio 9 2008</code> generator). </p>
<p> Contents of <code>COMPILE_OPTIONS</code> may use &quot;generator expressions&quot; with the syntax <code>$&lt;&#8230;&gt;</code>. See the <code><a href="/docs/linux/man/7-cmake-generator-expressions/">cmake-generator-expressions</a>(7)</code> manual for available expressions. However, <code>Xcode</code> does not support per-config per-source settings, so expressions that depend on the build configuration are not allowed with that generator. </p>
<p> Usage example: </p>
<pre>set_source_files_properties(foo.cpp PROPERTIES COMPILE_OPTIONS &quot;-Wno-unused-parameter;-Wno-missing-field-initializer&quot;)</pre>
<p>Related properties:</p>
<dl>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd>Prefer this property over <code>COMPILE_FLAGS</code>.</p>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd>Use <code>COMPILE_DEFINITIONS</code> to pass additional preprocessor definitions.</p>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd>Use <code>INCLUDE_DIRECTORIES</code> to pass additional include directories. </p>
<p> Related commands:</p>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd><code>add_compile_options()</code> for directory-wide settings</p>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd><code>target_compile_options()</code> for target-specific settings</dl>
<h3>External_Object</h3>
<p>If set to true then this is an object file. </p>
<p> If this property is set to <code>True</code> then the source file is really an object file and should not be compiled. It will still be linked into the target though.</p>
<h3>Fortran_FORMAT</h3>
<p>Set to <code>FIXED</code> or <code>FREE</code> to indicate the Fortran source layout. </p>
<p> This property tells CMake whether a given Fortran source file uses fixed-format or free-format. CMake will pass the corresponding format flag to the compiler. Consider using the target-wide <code>Fortran_FORMAT</code> property if all source files in a target share the same format. </p>
<p> <code>NOTE:</code> For some compilers, <code>NAG</code>, <code>PGI</code> and <code>Solaris Studio</code>, setting this to <code>OFF</code> will have no effect.</p>
<h3>Fortran_PREPROCESS</h3>
<p>New in version 3.18. </p>
<p> Control whether the Fortran source file should be unconditionally preprocessed. </p>
<p> If unset or empty, rely on the compiler to determine whether the file should be preprocessed. If explicitly set to <code>OFF</code> then the file does not need to be preprocessed. If explicitly set to <code>ON</code>, then the file does need to be preprocessed as part of the compilation step. </p>
<p> When using the <code>Ninja</code> generator, all source files are first preprocessed in order to generate module dependency information. Setting this property to <code>OFF</code> will make <code>Ninja</code> skip this step. </p>
<p> Consider using the target-wide <code>Fortran_PREPROCESS</code> property if all source files in a target need to be preprocessed.</p>
<h3>Generated</h3>
<p>Is this source file generated as part of the build or CMake process. </p>
<p> Changed in version 3.20: The GENERATED source file property is now visible in all directories. </p>
<p> Tells the internal CMake engine that a source file is generated by an outside process such as another build step, or the execution of CMake itself. This information is then used to exempt the file from any existence or validity checks. </p>
<p> Any file that is</p>
<dl>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd>created by the execution of commands such as <code>add_custom_command()</code> and <code>file(GENERATE)</code></p>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd>listed as one of the <code>BYPRODUCTS</code> of an <code>add_custom_command()</code> or <code>add_custom_target()</code> command, or</p>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd>created by a CMake <code>AUTOGEN</code> operation such as <code>AUTOMOC</code>, <code>AUTORCC</code>, or <code>AUTOUIC</code> </p>
<p> will be marked with the <code>GENERATED</code> property. </p>
<p> When a generated file created as the <code>OUTPUT</code> of an <code>add_custom_command()</code> command is explicitly listed as a source file for any target in the same directory scope (which usually means the same <code>CMakeLists.txt</code> file), CMake will automatically create a dependency to make sure the file is generated before building that target. </p>
<p> The Makefile Generators will remove <code>GENERATED</code> files during <code>make clean</code>. </p>
<p> Generated sources may be hidden in some IDE tools, while in others they might be shown. For the special case of sources generated by CMake&#8217;s <code>AUTOMOC</code>, <code>AUTORCC</code> or <code>AUTOUIC</code> functionality, the <code>AUTOGEN_SOURCE_GROUP</code>, <code>AUTOMOC_SOURCE_GROUP</code>, <code>AUTORCC_SOURCE_GROUP</code> and <code>AUTOUIC_SOURCE_GROUP</code> target properties may influence where the generated sources are grouped in the project&#8217;s file lists. </p>
<p> <code>NOTE:</code> Starting with CMake 3.20 the <code>GENERATED</code> source file property can be set and retrieved from any directory scope. It is an all-or-nothing property. It also can no longer be removed or unset if it was set to <code>TRUE</code>. Policy <code>CMP0118</code> was introduced to allow supporting the <code>OLD</code> behavior for some time.</dl>
<h3>Header_File_Only</h3>
<p>Is this source file only a header file. </p>
<p> A property on a source file that indicates if the source file is a header file with no associated implementation. This is set automatically based on the file extension and is used by CMake to determine if certain dependency information should be computed. </p>
<p> By setting this property to <code>ON</code>, you can disable compilation of the given source file, even if it should be compiled because it is part of the library&#8217;s/executable&#8217;s sources. </p>
<p> This is useful if you have some source files which you somehow pre-process, and then add these pre-processed sources via <code>add_library()</code> or <code>add_executable()</code>. Normally, in IDE, there would be no reference of the original sources, only of these pre-processed sources. So by setting this property for all the original source files to <code>ON</code>, and then either calling <code>add_library()</code> or <code>add_executable()</code> while passing both the pre-processed sources and the original sources, or by using <code>target_sources()</code> to add original source files will do exactly what would one expect, i.e. the original source files would be visible in IDE, and will not be built.</p>
<h3>Include_Directories</h3>
<p>New in version 3.11. </p>
<p> List of preprocessor include file search directories. </p>
<p> This property holds a semicolon-separated list of paths and will be added to the list of include directories when this source file builds. These directories will take precedence over directories defined at target level except for <code>Xcode</code> generator due to technical limitations. </p>
<p> Relative paths should not be added to this property directly. </p>
<p> Contents of <code>INCLUDE_DIRECTORIES</code> may use &quot;generator expressions&quot; with the syntax <code>$&lt;&#8230;&gt;</code>. See the <code><a href="/docs/linux/man/7-cmake-generator-expressions/">cmake-generator-expressions</a>(7)</code> manual for available expressions. However, <code>Xcode</code> does not support per-config per-source settings, so expressions that depend on the build configuration are not allowed with that generator.</p>
<h3>Keep_Extension</h3>
<p>Make the output file have the same extension as the source file. </p>
<p> If this property is set then the file extension of the output file will be the same as that of the source file. Normally the output file extension is computed based on the language of the source file, for example <code>.cxx</code> will go to a <code>.o</code> extension.</p>
<h3>Labels</h3>
<p>Specify a list of text labels associated with a source file. </p>
<p> This property has meaning only when the source file is listed in a target whose <code>LABELS</code> property is also set. No other semantics are currently specified.</p>
<h3>Language</h3>
<p>Specify the programming language in which a source file is written. </p>
<p> A property that can be set to indicate what programming language the source file is. If it is not set the language is determined based on the file extension. Typical values are <code>CXX</code> (i.e. C++), <code>C</code>, <code>CSharp</code>, <code>CUDA</code>, <code>Fortran</code>, <code>HIP</code>, <code>ISPC</code>, and <code>ASM</code>. Setting this property for a file means this file will be compiled. Do not set this for headers or files that should not be compiled. </p>
<p> Changed in version 3.20: Setting this property causes the source file to be compiled as the specified language, using explicit flags if possible. Previously it only caused the specified language&#8217;s compiler to be used. See policy <code>CMP0119</code>.</p>
<h3>Location</h3>
<p>The full path to a source file. </p>
<p> A read only property on a SOURCE FILE that contains the full path to the source file.</p>
<h3>Macosx_Package_Location</h3>
<p>Place a source file inside a Application Bundle (<code>MACOSX_BUNDLE</code>), Core Foundation Bundle (<code>BUNDLE</code>), or Framework Bundle (<code>FRAMEWORK</code>). It is applicable for macOS and iOS. </p>
<p> Executable targets with the <code>MACOSX_BUNDLE</code> property set are built as macOS or iOS application bundles on Apple platforms. Shared library targets with the <code>FRAMEWORK</code> property set are built as macOS or iOS frameworks on Apple platforms. Module library targets with the <code>BUNDLE</code> property set are built as macOS <code>CFBundle</code> bundles on Apple platforms. Source files listed in the target with this property set will be copied to a directory inside the bundle or framework content folder specified by the property value. For macOS Application Bundles the content folder is <code>&lt;name&gt;.app/Contents</code>. For macOS Frameworks the content folder is <code>&lt;name&gt;.framework/Versions/&lt;version&gt;</code>. For macOS CFBundles the content folder is <code>&lt;name&gt;.bundle/Contents</code> (unless the extension is changed). See the <code>PUBLIC_HEADER</code>, <code>PRIVATE_HEADER</code>, and <code>RESOURCE</code> target properties for specifying files meant for <code>Headers</code>, <code>PrivateHeaders</code>, or <code>Resources</code> directories. </p>
<p> If the specified location is equal to <code>Resources</code>, the resulting location will be the same as if the <code>RESOURCE</code> property had been used. If the specified location is a sub-folder of <code>Resources</code>, it will be placed into the respective sub-folder. Note: For iOS Apple uses a flat bundle layout where no <code>Resources</code> folder exist. Therefore CMake strips the <code>Resources</code> folder name from the specified location.</p>
<h3>Object_Depends</h3>
<p>Additional files on which a compiled object file depends. </p>
<p> Specifies a semicolon-separated list of full-paths to files on which any object files compiled from this source file depend. On Makefile Generators and the <code>Ninja</code> generator an object file will be recompiled if any of the named files is newer than it. Visual Studio Generators and the <code>Xcode</code> generator cannot implement such compilation dependencies. </p>
<p> This property need not be used to specify the dependency of a source file on a generated header file that it includes. Although the property was originally introduced for this purpose, it is no longer necessary. If the generated header file is created by a custom command in the same target as the source file, the automatic dependency scanning process will recognize the dependency. If the generated header file is created by another target, an inter-target dependency should be created with the <code>add_dependencies()</code> command (if one does not already exist due to linking relationships).</p>
<h3>Object_Outputs</h3>
<p>Additional outputs for a <code>Ninja</code> or Makefile Generators rule. </p>
<p> Additional outputs created by compilation of this source file. If any of these outputs is missing the object will be recompiled. This is supported only on the <code>Ninja</code> and Makefile Generators and will be ignored on other generators. </p>
<p> This property supports <code>generator expressions</code>.</p>
<h3>Skip_Autogen</h3>
<p>New in version 3.8. </p>
<p> Exclude the source file from <code>AUTOMOC</code>, <code>AUTOUIC</code> and <code>AUTORCC</code> processing (for Qt projects). </p>
<p> For finer exclusion control see <code>SKIP_AUTOMOC</code>, <code>SKIP_AUTOUIC</code> and <code>SKIP_AUTORCC</code>.</p>
<h3>Example</h3>
<pre># ...
set_property(SOURCE file.h PROPERTY SKIP_AUTOGEN ON)
# ...</pre>
<h3>Skip_Automoc</h3>
<p>New in version 3.8. </p>
<p> Exclude the source file from <code>AUTOMOC</code> processing (for Qt projects). </p>
<p> For broader exclusion control see <code>SKIP_AUTOGEN</code>.</p>
<h3>Example</h3>
<pre># ...
set_property(SOURCE file.h PROPERTY SKIP_AUTOMOC ON)
# ...</pre>
<h3>Skip_Autorcc</h3>
<p>New in version 3.8. </p>
<p> Exclude the source file from <code>AUTORCC</code> processing (for Qt projects). </p>
<p> For broader exclusion control see <code>SKIP_AUTOGEN</code>.</p>
<h3>Example</h3>
<pre># ...
set_property(SOURCE file.qrc PROPERTY SKIP_AUTORCC ON)
# ...</pre>
<h3>Skip_Autouic</h3>
<p>New in version 3.8. </p>
<p> Exclude the source file from <code>AUTOUIC</code> processing (for Qt projects). </p>
<p> <i>SKIP_AUTOUIC</i> can be set on C++ header and source files and on <code>.ui</code> files. </p>
<p> For broader exclusion control see <code>SKIP_AUTOGEN</code>.</p>
<h3>Example</h3>
<pre># ...
set_property(SOURCE file.h PROPERTY SKIP_AUTOUIC ON)
set_property(SOURCE file.cpp PROPERTY SKIP_AUTOUIC ON)
set_property(SOURCE widget.ui PROPERTY SKIP_AUTOUIC ON)
# ...</pre>
<h3>Skip_Precompile_Headers</h3>
<p>New in version 3.16. </p>
<p> Is this source file skipped by <code>PRECOMPILE_HEADERS</code> feature. </p>
<p> This property helps with build problems that one would run into when using the <code>PRECOMPILE_HEADERS</code> feature. </p>
<p> One example would be the usage of Objective-C (<code>*.m</code>) files, and Objective-C++ (<code>*.mm</code>) files, which lead to compilation failure because they are treated (in case of Ninja / Makefile generator) as C, and CXX respectively. The precompile headers are not compatible between languages.</p>
<h3>Skip_Unity_Build_Inclusion</h3>
<p>New in version 3.16. </p>
<p> Setting this property to true ensures the source file will be skipped by unity builds when its associated target has its <code>UNITY_BUILD</code> property set to true. The source file will instead be compiled on its own in the same way as it would with unity builds disabled. </p>
<p> This property helps with &quot;ODR (One definition rule)&quot; problems where combining a particular source file with others might lead to build errors or other unintended side effects.</p>
<h3>Swift_DEPENDENCIES_FILE</h3>
<p>New in version 3.15. </p>
<p> This property sets the path for the Swift dependency file (swiftdeps) for the source. If one is not specified, it will default to <code>&lt;OBJECT&gt;.swiftdeps</code>.</p>
<h3>Swift_DIAGNOSTICS_FILE</h3>
<p>New in version 3.15. </p>
<p> This property controls where the Swift diagnostics are serialized.</p>
<h3>Symbolic</h3>
<p>Is this just a name for a rule. </p>
<p> If <code>SYMBOLIC</code> (boolean) is set to <code>True</code> the build system will be informed that the source file is not actually created on disk but instead used as a symbolic name for a build rule.</p>
<h3>Unity_Group</h3>
<p>New in version 3.18. </p>
<p> This property controls which <i>bucket</i> the source will be part of when the <code>UNITY_BUILD_MODE</code> is set to <code>GROUP</code>.</p>
<h3>Vs_Copy_To_Out_Dir</h3>
<p>New in version 3.8. </p>
<p> Sets the <code>&lt;CopyToOutputDirectory&gt;</code> tag for a source file in a Visual Studio project file. Valid values are <code>Never</code>, <code>Always</code> and <code>PreserveNewest</code>.</p>
<h3>VS_CSHARP_&lt;tagname&gt;</h3>
<p>New in version 3.8. </p>
<p> Visual Studio and CSharp source-file-specific configuration. </p>
<p> Tell the <code>Visual Studio generators</code> to set the source file tag <code>&lt;tagname&gt;</code> to a given value in the generated Visual Studio CSharp project. Ignored on other generators and languages. This property can be used to define dependencies between source files or set any other Visual Studio specific parameters. </p>
<p> Example usage: </p>
<pre>set_source_files_property(&lt;filename&gt;
 PROPERTIES
 VS_CSHARP_DependentUpon &lt;other file&gt;
 VS_CSHARP_SubType &quot;Form&quot;)</pre>
<h3>Vs_Deployment_Content</h3>
<p>New in version 3.1. </p>
<p> Mark a source file as content for deployment with a Windows Phone or Windows Store application when built with a <code>Visual Studio generators</code>. The value must evaluate to either <code>1</code> or <code>0</code> and may use <code>generator expressions</code> to make the choice based on the build configuration. The <code>.vcxproj</code> file entry for the source file will be marked either <code>DeploymentContent</code> or <code>ExcludedFromBuild</code> for values <code>1</code> and <code>0</code>, respectively.</p>
<h3>Vs_Deployment_Location</h3>
<p>New in version 3.1. </p>
<p> Specifies the deployment location for a content source file with a Windows Phone or Windows Store application when built with a <code>Visual Studio generators</code>. This property is only applicable when using <code>VS_DEPLOYMENT_CONTENT</code>. The value represent the path relative to the app package and applies to all configurations.</p>
<h3>Vs_Include_In_Vsix</h3>
<p>New in version 3.8. </p>
<p> Boolean property to specify if the file should be included within a VSIX (Visual Studio Integration Extension) extension package. This is needed for development of Visual Studio extensions.</p>
<h3>Vs_Resource_Generator</h3>
<p>New in version 3.8. </p>
<p> This property allows to specify the resource generator to be used on this file. It defaults to <code>PublicResXFileCodeGenerator</code> if not set. </p>
<p> This property only applies to C# projects.</p>
<h3>Vs_Settings</h3>
<p>New in version 3.18. </p>
<p> Set any item metadata on a file. </p>
<p> New in version 3.22: This property is honored for all source file types. Previously it worked only for non-built files. </p>
<p> Takes a list of <code>Key=Value</code> pairs. Tells the Visual Studio generator to set <code>Key</code> to <code>Value</code> as item metadata on the file. </p>
<p> For example: </p>
<pre>set_property(SOURCE file.hlsl PROPERTY VS_SETTINGS &quot;Key=Value&quot; &quot;Key2=Value2&quot;)</pre>
<p>will set <code>Key</code> to <code>Value</code> and <code>Key2</code> to <code>Value2</code> on the <code>file.hlsl</code> item as metadata. </p>
<p> <code>Generator expressions</code> are supported.</p>
<h3>Vs_Shader_Disable_Optimizations</h3>
<p>New in version 3.11. </p>
<p> Disable compiler optimizations for an <code>.hlsl</code> source file. This adds the <code>-Od</code> flag to the command line for the FxCompiler tool. Specify the value <code>true</code> for this property to disable compiler optimizations.</p>
<h3>Vs_Shader_Enable_Debug</h3>
<p>New in version 3.11. </p>
<p> Enable debugging information for an <code>.hlsl</code> source file. This adds the <code>-Zi</code> flag to the command line for the FxCompiler tool. Specify the value <code>true</code> to generate debugging information for the compiled shader.</p>
<h3>Vs_Shader_Entrypoint</h3>
<p>New in version 3.1. </p>
<p> Specifies the name of the entry point for the shader of a <code>.hlsl</code> source file.</p>
<h3>Vs_Shader_Flags</h3>
<p>New in version 3.2. </p>
<p> Set additional Visual Studio shader flags of a <code>.hlsl</code> source file.</p>
<h3>Vs_Shader_Model</h3>
<p>New in version 3.1. </p>
<p> Specifies the shader model of a <code>.hlsl</code> source file. Some shader types can only be used with recent shader models</p>
<h3>Vs_Shader_Object_File_Name</h3>
<p>New in version 3.12. </p>
<p> Specifies a file name for the compiled shader object file for an <code>.hlsl</code> source file. This adds the <code>-Fo</code> flag to the command line for the FxCompiler tool.</p>
<h3>Vs_Shader_Output_Header_File</h3>
<p>New in version 3.10. </p>
<p> Set filename for output header file containing object code of a <code>.hlsl</code> source file.</p>
<h3>Vs_Shader_Type</h3>
<p>New in version 3.1. </p>
<p> Set the Visual Studio shader type of a <code>.hlsl</code> source file.</p>
<h3>Vs_Shader_Variable_Name</h3>
<p>New in version 3.10. </p>
<p> Set name of variable in header file containing object code of a <code>.hlsl</code> source file.</p>
<h3>Vs_Tool_Override</h3>
<p>New in version 3.7. </p>
<p> Override the default Visual Studio tool that will be applied to the source file with a new tool not based on the extension of the file.</p>
<h3>Vs_Xaml_Type</h3>
<p>New in version 3.3. </p>
<p> Mark a Extensible Application Markup Language (XAML) source file as a different type than the default <code>Page</code>. The most common usage would be to set the default <code>App.xaml</code> file as <code>ApplicationDefinition</code>.</p>
<h3>Wrap_Exclude</h3>
<p>Exclude this source file from any code wrapping techniques. </p>
<p> Some packages can wrap source files into alternate languages to provide additional functionality. </p>
<p> For example, C++ code can be wrapped into Java or Python, using SWIG. If <code>WRAP_EXCLUDE</code> is set to <code>True</code>, that indicates that this source file should not be wrapped.</p>
<h3>Xcode_Explicit_File_Type</h3>
<p>New in version 3.1. </p>
<p> Set the <code>Xcode</code> <code>explicitFileType</code> attribute on its reference to a source file. CMake computes a default based on file extension but can be told explicitly with this property. </p>
<p> See also <code>XCODE_LAST_KNOWN_FILE_TYPE</code>.</p>
<h3>Xcode_File_Attributes</h3>
<p>New in version 3.7. </p>
<p> Add values to the <code>Xcode</code> <code>ATTRIBUTES</code> setting on its reference to a source file. Among other things, this can be used to set the role on a <code>.mig</code> file: </p>
<pre>set_source_files_properties(defs.mig
 PROPERTIES
 XCODE_FILE_ATTRIBUTES &quot;Client;Server&quot;
)</pre>
<h3>Xcode_Last_Known_File_Type</h3>
<p>New in version 3.1. </p>
<p> Set the <code>Xcode</code> <code>lastKnownFileType</code> attribute on its reference to a source file. CMake computes a default based on file extension but can be told explicitly with this property. </p>
<p> See also <code>XCODE_EXPLICIT_FILE_TYPE</code>, which is preferred over this property if set.</p>
<h2>Properties On Cache Entries</h2>
<h3>Advanced</h3>
<p>True if entry should be hidden by default in GUIs. </p>
<p> This is a boolean value indicating whether the entry is considered interesting only for advanced configuration. The <code>mark_as_advanced()</code> command modifies this property.</p>
<h3>Helpstring</h3>
<p>Help associated with entry in GUIs. </p>
<p> This string summarizes the purpose of an entry to help users set it through a CMake GUI.</p>
<h3>Modified</h3>
<p>Internal management property. Do not set or get. </p>
<p> This is an internal cache entry property managed by CMake to track interactive user modification of entries. Ignore it.</p>
<h3>Strings</h3>
<p>Enumerate possible <code>STRING</code> entry values for GUI selection. </p>
<p> For cache entries with type <code>STRING</code>, this enumerates a set of values. CMake GUIs may use this to provide a selection widget instead of a generic string entry field. This is for convenience only. CMake does not enforce that the value matches one of those listed.</p>
<h3>Type</h3>
<p>Widget type for entry in GUIs. </p>
<p> Cache entry values are always strings, but CMake GUIs present widgets to help users set values. The GUIs use this property as a hint to determine the widget type. Valid <code>TYPE</code> values are: </p>
<pre>BOOL = Boolean ON/OFF value.
PATH = Path to a directory.
FILEPATH = Path to a file.
STRING = Generic string value.
INTERNAL = Do not present in GUI at all.
STATIC = Value managed by CMake, do not change.
UNINITIALIZED = Type not yet specified.</pre>
<p>Generally the <code>TYPE</code> of a cache entry should be set by the command which creates it ( <code>set()</code>, <code>option()</code>, <code>find_library()</code>, etc.).</p>
<h3>Value</h3>
<p>Value of a cache entry. </p>
<p> This property maps to the actual value of a cache entry. Setting this property always sets the value without checking, so use with care.</p>
<h2>Properties On Installed Files</h2>
<h3>Cpack_Desktop_Shortcuts</h3>
<p>New in version 3.3. </p>
<p> Species a list of shortcut names that should be created on the <i>Desktop</i> for this file. </p>
<p> The property is currently only supported by the <code>CPack WIX Generator</code>.</p>
<h3>Cpack_Never_Overwrite</h3>
<p>New in version 3.1. </p>
<p> Request that this file not be overwritten on install or reinstall. </p>
<p> The property is currently only supported by the <code>CPack WIX Generator</code>.</p>
<h3>Cpack_Permanent</h3>
<p>New in version 3.1. </p>
<p> Request that this file not be removed on uninstall. </p>
<p> The property is currently only supported by the <code>CPack WIX Generator</code>.</p>
<h3>Cpack_Start_Menu_Shortcuts</h3>
<p>New in version 3.3. </p>
<p> Species a list of shortcut names that should be created in the <i>Start Menu</i> for this file. </p>
<p> The property is currently only supported by the <code>CPack WIX Generator</code>.</p>
<h3>Cpack_Startup_Shortcuts</h3>
<p>New in version 3.3. </p>
<p> Species a list of shortcut names that should be created in the <i>Startup</i> folder for this file. </p>
<p> The property is currently only supported by the <code>CPack WIX Generator</code>.</p>
<h3>Cpack_Wix_Acl</h3>
<p>New in version 3.1. </p>
<p> Specifies access permissions for files or directories installed by a WiX installer. </p>
<p> The property can contain multiple list entries, each of which has to match the following format. </p>
<pre>&lt;user&gt;[@&lt;domain&gt;]=&lt;permission&gt;[,&lt;permission&gt;]</pre>
<p><code>&lt;user&gt;</code> and <code>&lt;domain&gt;</code> specify the windows user and domain for which the <code>&lt;Permission&gt;</code> element should be generated. </p>
<p> <code>&lt;permission&gt;</code> is any of the YesNoType attributes listed here: </p>
<pre><a rel="nofollow" href="http://wixtoolset.org/documentation/manual/v3/xsd/wix/permission.html">http://wixtoolset.org/documentation/manual/v3/xsd/wix/permission.html</a></pre>
<p>The property is currently only supported by the <code>CPack WIX Generator</code>.</p>
<h2>Deprecated Properties On Directories</h2>
<h3>Additional_Make_Clean_Files</h3>
<p>Deprecated since version 3.15: Use <code>ADDITIONAL_CLEAN_FILES</code> instead. </p>
<p> Additional files to remove during the clean stage. </p>
<p> A ;-list of files that will be removed as a part of the <code>make clean</code> target. </p>
<p> Arguments to <i>ADDITIONAL_MAKE_CLEAN_FILES</i> may use <code>generator expressions</code>. </p>
<p> This property only works for the Makefile generators. It is ignored on other generators.</p>
<h3>COMPILE_DEFINITIONS_&lt;CONFIG&gt;</h3>
<p>Ignored. See CMake Policy <code>CMP0043</code>. </p>
<p> Per-configuration preprocessor definitions in a directory. </p>
<p> This is the configuration-specific version of <code>COMPILE_DEFINITIONS</code> where <code>&lt;CONFIG&gt;</code> is an upper-case name (ex. <code>COMPILE_DEFINITIONS_DEBUG</code>). </p>
<p> This property will be initialized in each directory by its value in the directory&#8217;s parent. </p>
<p> Contents of <code>COMPILE_DEFINITIONS_&lt;CONFIG&gt;</code> may use &quot;generator expressions&quot; with the syntax <code>$&lt;&#8230;&gt;</code>. See the <code><a href="/docs/linux/man/7-cmake-generator-expressions/">cmake-generator-expressions</a>(7)</code> manual for available expressions. See the <code><a href="/docs/linux/man/7-cmake-buildsystem/">cmake-buildsystem</a>(7)</code> manual for more on defining buildsystem properties. </p>
<p> Generator expressions should be preferred instead of setting this property.</p>
<h3>Test_Include_File</h3>
<p>Deprecated. Use <code>TEST_INCLUDE_FILES</code> instead. </p>
<p> A cmake file that will be included when ctest is run. </p>
<p> If you specify <code>TEST_INCLUDE_FILE</code>, that file will be included and processed when ctest is run on the directory.</p>
<h2>Deprecated Properties On Targets</h2>
<h3>COMPILE_DEFINITIONS_&lt;CONFIG&gt;</h3>
<p>Ignored. See CMake Policy <code>CMP0043</code>. </p>
<p> Per-configuration preprocessor definitions on a target. </p>
<p> This is the configuration-specific version of <code>COMPILE_DEFINITIONS</code> where <code>&lt;CONFIG&gt;</code> is an upper-case name (ex. <code>COMPILE_DEFINITIONS_DEBUG</code>). </p>
<p> Contents of <code>COMPILE_DEFINITIONS_&lt;CONFIG&gt;</code> may use &quot;generator expressions&quot; with the syntax <code>$&lt;&#8230;&gt;</code>. See the <code><a href="/docs/linux/man/7-cmake-generator-expressions/">cmake-generator-expressions</a>(7)</code> manual for available expressions. See the <code><a href="/docs/linux/man/7-cmake-buildsystem/">cmake-buildsystem</a>(7)</code> manual for more on defining buildsystem properties. </p>
<p> Generator expressions should be preferred instead of setting this property.</p>
<h3>Post_Install_Script</h3>
<p>Deprecated install support. </p>
<p> The <code>PRE_INSTALL_SCRIPT</code> and <code>POST_INSTALL_SCRIPT</code> properties are the old way to specify CMake scripts to run before and after installing a target. They are used only when the old <code>INSTALL_TARGETS</code> command is used to install the target. Use the <code>install()</code> command instead.</p>
<h3>Pre_Install_Script</h3>
<p>Deprecated install support. </p>
<p> The <code>PRE_INSTALL_SCRIPT</code> and <code>POST_INSTALL_SCRIPT</code> properties are the old way to specify CMake scripts to run before and after installing a target. They are used only when the old <code>INSTALL_TARGETS</code> command is used to install the target. Use the <code>install()</code> command instead.</p>
<h2>Deprecated Properties On Source Files</h2>
<h3>COMPILE_DEFINITIONS_&lt;CONFIG&gt;</h3>
<p>Ignored. See CMake Policy <code>CMP0043</code>. </p>
<p> Per-configuration preprocessor definitions on a source file. </p>
<p> This is the configuration-specific version of <code>COMPILE_DEFINITIONS</code>. Note that <code>Xcode</code> does not support per-configuration source file flags so this property will be ignored by the <code>Xcode</code> generator.</p>
